11858 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Mother of the Qur’an is the Seven Oft-Repeated Verses and the Magnificent Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4704).
Al-Tirmidhī (3124) also narrated it through another route with the wording:
“‘All praise belongs to Allah’ is the Mother of the Qur’an, the Mother of the Book, and the Seven Oft-Repeated Verses.”
Al-Tirmidhī said:
“This is a good authentic hadith.”

11859 — Abū Saʿīd ibn al-Muʿallā narrated:
I was praying when the Prophet ﷺ called me, but I did not respond to him.
I later said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I was praying.”
He said:
“Has Allah not said:
‘Respond to Allah and to the Messenger when he calls you to that which gives you life’?”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:24]
He then said:
“Shall I teach you the greatest sūrah in the Qur’an before you leave the mosque?”
He took hold of my hand. When we were about to leave, I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you said that you would teach me the greatest sūrah in the Qur’an.”
He said:
“‘All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds.’ It is the Seven Oft-Repeated Verses and the Magnificent Qur’an which I was given.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5006).

11860 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out to Ubayy ibn Kaʿb and called:
“Ubayy!”
Ubayy was praying. He turned toward him but did not answer. He shortened his prayer, then went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“And peace be upon you. What prevented you, Ubayy, from answering me when I called you?”
He replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I was praying.”
He said:
“Did you not find among what was revealed to me:
‘Respond to Allah and to the Messenger when he calls you to that which gives you life’?”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:24]
Ubayy replied:
“Yes. I will not do so again, Allah willing.”
He then said:
“Would you like me to teach you a sūrah, the like of which was not revealed in the Torah, the Gospel, the Psalms, or the Qur’an?”
He replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What do you recite in prayer?”
Ubayy recited the Mother of the Qur’an.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, nothing like it was revealed in the Torah, the Gospel, the Psalms, or the Qur’an. It is the Seven Oft-Repeated Verses and the Magnificent Qur’an which I was given.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2875), al-Nasāʾī (914), Aḥmad (9345), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (861).

11861 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
We were on a journey and stopped at a certain place. A servant girl came and said:
“The chief of the tribe has been bitten by a poisonous creature, and our men are absent. Is there anyone among you who can perform ruqyah?”
A man from among us went with her, although we had not previously known him to perform ruqyah.
He recited over the chief, and the man recovered. The chief ordered that thirty sheep be given to him, and they gave us milk to drink.
When he returned, we asked him:
“Did you know how to perform ruqyah, or had you performed it before?”
He replied:
“No. I only performed ruqyah with the Mother of the Book.”
We said:
“Do not do anything with the sheep until we go to—or ask—the Prophet ﷺ.”
When we arrived in Madinah, we mentioned the matter to the Prophet ﷺ.
He said:
“How did he know that it is a ruqyah? Divide the sheep among yourselves and assign a share to me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5007) and Muslim (2201/66).

11862 — Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was on a journey and stopped at a place. One of his Companions walked beside him.
The Prophet ﷺ turned toward him and said:
“Shall I tell you the finest portion of the Qur’an?”
He then recited to him:
“All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa al-Laylah (723), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (774) and al-Ḥākim (1/560).

11863 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Jābir narrated:
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after he had relieved himself.
I said:
“Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He did not respond.
I repeated:
“Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He did not respond.
I said a third time:
“Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He still did not respond.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ walked away, and I followed behind him until he entered his dwelling. I entered the mosque and sat down, distressed and saddened.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then came out to me after purifying himself and said:
“Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you. Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you. Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you.”
He then said:
“Shall I tell you, ʿAbdullāh ibn Jābir, of the best sūrah in the Qur’an?”
I replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Recite: ‘All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds,’ until you complete the sūrah.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17597).

11864 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
While Gabriel was sitting with the Prophet ﷺ, he heard a creaking sound from above him. He raised his head and said:
“This is a gate in heaven that has been opened today. It had never been opened before today.”
An angel descended through it, and Gabriel said:
“This is an angel who has descended to the earth. He had never descended before today.”
The angel greeted the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Rejoice in two lights which you have been given and which no prophet before you was given: the Opening of the Book and the concluding verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah. You will never recite a single letter from either of them without being granted what it contains.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (806).

11865 — Abū Hurayrah said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever performs a prayer without reciting the Mother of the Qur’an in it, his prayer is incomplete, incomplete, incomplete—not complete.”
I said:
“Abū Hurayrah, sometimes I pray behind the imam.”
He pressed my forearm and said:
“Recite it quietly to yourself, Persian, for I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘Allah, Blessed and Exalted, said: “I have divided the prayer between Myself and My servant into two halves. Half of it is for Me, half of it is for My servant, and My servant will receive whatever he asks.”’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the servant says:
‘All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds,’
Allah, Blessed and Exalted, says: ‘My servant has praised Me.’
When the servant says:
‘The Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,’
Allah says: ‘My servant has extolled Me.’
When the servant says:
‘Master of the Day of Judgment,’
Allah says: ‘My servant has glorified Me.’
When the servant says:
‘You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help,’
Allah says: ‘This verse is between Me and My servant, and My servant will receive whatever he asks.’
When the servant says:
‘Guide us to the straight path—the path of those upon whom You have bestowed Your favor, not those who have incurred wrath, nor those who have gone astray,’
Allah says: ‘These are for My servant, and My servant will receive whatever he asks.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (42) and Muslim (395/39).

11866 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘I have divided the prayer between Myself and My servant into two halves, and he will receive whatever he asks.
When the servant says:
“All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds,”
Allah says: “My servant has praised Me.”
When he says:
“The Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,”
Allah says: “My servant has extolled Me.”
When he says:
“Master of the Day of Judgment,”
Allah says: “My servant has glorified Me.”
This portion is for Me, and the remainder is for him.”’”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (1/202–203).
The Words of Allah: “Not Those Who Have Incurred Wrath, nor Those Who Have Gone Astray”

11867 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the imam says:
‘Not those who have incurred wrath, nor those who have gone astray,’
say: ‘Āmīn.’ Whoever’s saying of ‘Āmīn’ coincides with the saying of the angels will have his previous sins forgiven.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (49), al-Bukhārī (4475), and Muslim (409/71).

11868 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Shaqīq reported from a man who heard the Prophet ﷺ while he was at Wādī al-Qurā, mounted upon his horse.
A man from the tribe of Balqayn asked him:
“O Messenger of Allah, who are those who have incurred wrath?”
He replied:
“They are the Jews.”
He then asked:
“Who are those who have gone astray?”
He replied:
“They are the Christians.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20351) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (1/187).

11869 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim al-Ṭāʾī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The Jews are those who have incurred wrath, and the Christians are those who have gone astray.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2954).
This is because Allah, the Exalted, pronounced wrath upon the Jews in His words:
“Those whom Allah has cursed and with whom He is angry.”
And He described the Christians as having gone astray in His words:
“Do not follow the desires of a people who had already gone astray before.”

11870 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
One night, Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr was reciting in his threshing floor when his horse became agitated. He continued reciting, and it became agitated again. He continued reciting, and it became agitated once more.
Usayd said:
I feared that it might trample Yaḥyā, so I went toward it. I then saw something resembling a canopy above my head, containing objects like lamps. It ascended into the sky until I could no longer see it.
The following morning, I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, last night, in the middle of the night, I was reciting in my threshing floor when my horse became agitated.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Continue reciting, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
“I continued reciting, and it became agitated again.”
He said:
“Continue reciting, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
“I continued reciting, and it became agitated again.”
He said:
“Continue reciting, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
“I then stopped because Yaḥyā was close to the horse and I feared that it might trample him. I saw something resembling a canopy containing objects like lamps. It ascended into the sky until I could no longer see it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Those were angels listening to your recitation. Had you continued reciting, the people would have seen them in the morning, and they would not have concealed themselves from them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (796).

11871 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not turn your homes into graveyards. Satan flees from the house in which Sūrat al-Baqarah is recited.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (780).

11872 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah wrote a Book two thousand years before He created the heavens and the earth. From it, He revealed two verses with which He concluded Sūrat al-Baqarah. They are not recited in a house for three nights except that no devil approaches it.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2882), Aḥmad (18414), Ibn al-Ḍurays (167), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (782).

11873 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Let me not find one of you reclining with one leg placed over the other, entertaining himself, while neglecting Sūrat al-Baqarah and not reciting it. Satan flees from the house in which Sūrat al-Baqarah is recited. The emptiest of homes is the hollow interior that contains nothing from the Book of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10733).
The expression “the emptiest of homes” refers to a house in which the Book of Allah is not recited. It is derived from the idea of emptiness.
The expression “the empty hollow” refers to a heart that has memorized nothing from the Book of Allah.

11874 — Abū Masʿūd al-Badrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever recites the final two verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah during the night, they will suffice him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4008) and Muslim (808/256).
The words “they will suffice him” mean that they will protect him from evil and guard him against harm.
It has also been said that they will suffice him in place of the night prayer, or that they will suffice him in place of reciting the entire Qur’an.

11875 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was taken on the Night Journey, he was brought to Sidrat al-Muntahā—the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary…
He said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was then given three things: he was given the five daily prayers, he was given the concluding verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah, and the destructive major sins were forgiven for those of his Ummah who did not associate anything with Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (173/279).

11876 — Abū Dharr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was given the concluding verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah from a treasure-house beneath the Throne. No prophet before me was given them.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21345).

11877 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah wrote a Book two thousand years before He created the heavens and the earth. From it, He revealed two verses with which He concluded Sūrat al-Baqarah. They are not recited in a house for three nights except that no devil approaches it.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2882).

11878 — Abū Hurayrah narrated, concerning the incident in which he guarded the charity collected for Ramadan:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked me:
“What happened to your prisoner last night?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, he claimed that he would teach me some words through which Allah would benefit me, so I released him.”
He asked:
“What were they?”
I replied:
“He told me: ‘When you go to your bed, recite Āyat al-Kursī from its beginning until you complete the verse:
“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.”
A guardian from Allah will continue to protect you, and no devil will approach you until morning.’”
The Companions were especially eager for anything good.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“He has spoken the truth to you, although he is a habitual liar. Do you know whom you have been speaking to for the past three nights, Abū Hurayrah?”
I replied:
“No.”
He said:
“That was a devil.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2311).

11879 — Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Abū al-Mundhir, do you know which verse in the Book of Allah that you have learned is the greatest?”
I replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He asked again:
“Abū al-Mundhir, do you know which verse in the Book of Allah that you have learned is the greatest?”
I replied:
“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.”
He struck me on the chest and said:
“By Allah, may knowledge bring you joy, Abū al-Mundhir.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (810/258).

11880 — Wāthilah ibn al-Asqaʿ narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ came to them while they were in the shelter of the Emigrants. A man asked him:
“Which verse in the Qur’an is the greatest?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence. Neither drowsiness nor sleep overtakes Him.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4003).

11881 — Abū Umāmah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever recites Āyat al-Kursī after every obligatory prayer, nothing prevents him from entering Paradise except that he must first die.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa al-Laylah (100).

11882 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān al-Kilābī narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“The Qur’an will be brought on the Day of Resurrection, together with its people who used to act according to it. Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān will precede it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave three comparisons for them which I have never forgotten:
“They will appear as though they were two clouds, or two dark canopies with light between them, or two flocks of birds arranged in rows, pleading on behalf of their companion.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (805).
The word “two clouds” refers to two masses of cloud.
The word sharaq means light or radiance.
The word ḥizqān means two groups or flocks.

11883 — Abū Umāmah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Recite the Qur’an, for it will come on the Day of Resurrection as an intercessor for its companions.
Recite the two radiant sūrahs: Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān. They will come on the Day of Resurrection as though they were two clouds, two canopies, or two flocks of birds arranged in rows, pleading on behalf of their companions.
Recite Sūrat al-Baqarah, for taking hold of it is a blessing, abandoning it is a cause of regret, and the practitioners of sorcery cannot withstand it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (804).
The expression “the two radiant sūrahs” means the two luminous sūrahs, because they contain light and guidance.
A ghayāyah is anything that shades a person from above, such as a cloud or similar covering.
The word al-baṭalah refers to sorcerers, as Muʿāwiyah explained. They were called this because what they practice is falsehood.

11884 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
I was sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when I heard him say:
“Learn Sūrat al-Baqarah, for taking hold of it is a blessing, abandoning it is a cause of regret, and the practitioners of sorcery cannot withstand it.”
He then remained silent for a while before saying:
“Learn Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, for they are the two radiant sūrahs. On the Day of Resurrection, they will shade their companion as though they were two clouds, two canopies, or two flocks of birds arranged in rows.
The Qur’an will meet its companion on the Day of Resurrection when his grave opens, appearing like a pale man. It will ask him, ‘Do you recognize me?’ He will reply, ‘I do not recognize you.’
It will say, ‘I am your companion, the Qur’an, which made you thirsty during the heat of the day and kept you awake during the night. Every merchant stands behind his trade, but today you will stand beyond every trade.’
Kingship will be placed in his right hand, and everlasting life in his left. The crown of dignity will be placed upon his head, and his parents will be clothed in two garments whose value cannot be equaled by the entire world.
They will ask, ‘Why have we been clothed in these garments?’ It will be said, ‘Because your child took hold of the Qur’an.’
It will then be said to him, ‘Recite and ascend through the levels and chambers of Paradise.’ He will continue ascending as long as he continues reciting, whether rapidly or with measured recitation.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22950), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2302), al-Dārimī (3435), and al-Ḥākim (1/560).

11885 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Recite the two radiant sūrahs. Recite Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, for they will come on the Day of Resurrection as though they were two clouds or two flocks of birds arranged in rows.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2303).

11886 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever masters the first seven sūrahs is a learned scholar.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (24443), Ibn al-Ḍurays in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (72), and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (27). The wording cited here is that of Aḥmad.
Some narrations state:
“Whoever masters the seven long sūrahs.”
The expression “the first seven” refers to Sūrat al-Baqarah, Āl ʿImrān, al-Nisāʾ, al-Māʾidah, al-Anʿām, and al-Aʿrāf.
There is disagreement concerning the seventh. It has been said to be Sūrat Yūnus, Sūrat al-Kahf, or Sūrat al-Tawbah together with Sūrat al-Anfāl.

11887 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was given seven of the long, oft-repeated sūrahs, while Moses, peace be upon him, was given six. When he cast down the tablets, two were taken away and four remained.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1459) and al-Ḥākim (2/354–355).
Al-Ḥākim also narrated through another route from Muslim al-Baṭīn, from Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ʿAbbās concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“We have certainly given you seven of the oft-repeated verses and the Magnificent Qur’an.”
 [Sūrat al-Ḥijr 15:87]
Ibn ʿAbbās said that they are:
Sūrat al-Baqarah, Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, Sūrat al-Nisāʾ, Sūrat al-Māʾidah, Sūrat al-Anʿām, Sūrat al-Aʿrāf, and Sūrat al-Kahf.
Al-Ḥākim said:
“It is authentic according to the conditions of al-Bukhārī and Muslim.”

11888 — Wāthilah ibn al-Asqaʿ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was given the seven long sūrahs in place of the Torah, the sūrahs of approximately one hundred verses in place of the Psalms, and the oft-repeated sūrahs in place of the Gospel. I was further favored with al-Mufaṣṣal.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (16982) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (22/75).

11889 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When a believer commits a sin, a black spot appears upon his heart. If he repents, abandons the sin, and seeks Allah’s forgiveness, his heart is polished clean. If he increases in sin, the blackness increases. That is the rust which Allah mentioned in His Book:
‘No! Rather, what they used to earn has covered their hearts with rust.’”
 [Sūrat al-Muṭaffifīn 83:14]
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3334), Ibn Mājah (4244), Aḥmad (7952), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (930).

11890 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw rain, he would say:
“May it be beneficial rain.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1032).
In this noble verse, Allah presents another example of the hypocrites.
They resemble a person caught beneath rain falling amid darkness. In their hypocrisy and conduct, they are like someone moving through layers of darkness.
The word:
“Thunder”
refers to the terrifying sound that disturbs the hearts.
The hypocrite lives in a continual state of fear.
The word:
“Lightning”
refers to the intensely flashing light that appears with the clouds.
The word:
“Thunderbolts”
is the plural of ṣāʿiqah, referring to a fiery bolt descending from the sky during a thunderstorm.
The words:
“The lightning nearly snatches away their sight”
mean that because of its intense brightness, they are unable to look at it.
Snatching means taking something suddenly and swiftly, here referring to the loss of sight.
This is the condition of the hypocrites.
They face thunder that terrifies the hearts and lightning that dazzles the eyes. They place their fingers in their ears out of fear and panic, yet their precautions cannot protect them from Allah’s power, for He completely encompasses them.
It is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās concerning the words:
“Whenever it gives them light, they walk in it,”
that whenever the hypocrites experienced the strength and honor of Islam, they felt secure with it. But whenever Islam suffered adversity, they turned back toward disbelief.
This resembles the words of Allah, the Exalted:
“Among mankind is one who worships Allah upon an edge. If good reaches him, he is reassured by it; but if a trial reaches him, he turns back upon his face. He loses this world and the Hereafter. That is the evident loss.”
 [Sūrat al-Ḥajj 22:11]
Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī.
It is reported from Abū al-ʿĀliyah concerning the words:
“Whenever it gives them light, they walk in it, but when darkness falls over them, they stand still,”
that he said:
“Their example is that of people traveling along a road during a dark night containing rain, thunder, and lightning. Whenever the lightning flashes, they see the road and proceed along it. When its light disappears, they become confused.
In the same way, whenever the hypocrite speaks the word of sincerity, light appears for him; but whenever he doubts, he becomes confused and falls into darkness.”
Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr.
The words:
“When darkness falls over them, they stand still”
mean that whenever doubts confront them, they return to their hypocrisy and live in fear.
The words:
“Had Allah willed, He could have taken away their hearing and sight”
mean that if Allah had willed to remove their hearing and vision, He would have done so.
He instead left them in their error, and they lived upon the earth like disbelievers who neither hear nor perceive the truth.
This passage contains a warning to hypocrites who hear the words of Allah but take no lesson from them.
Instead, they place their fingers in their ears and turn away from His commands and prohibitions because of their hypocrisy.
Allah is fully capable of exposing whatever they conceal.

11891 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Which sin is greatest in the sight of Allah?”
He replied:
“That you appoint a rival to Allah even though He created you.”
I said:
“That is indeed a tremendous sin. Then which?”
He said:
“That you kill your child because you fear that he will share your food.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you commit adultery with your neighbor’s wife.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4476) and Muslim (86).

11892 — Muʿādh ibn Jabal narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Muʿādh, do you know Allah’s right over His servants?”
He replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“That they worship Him and do not associate anything with Him. Do you know their right upon Him?”
He replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“That He does not punish them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7373) and Muslim (30/50).

11893 — Qutaylah, a woman from Juhaynah, narrated:
A Jewish man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“You appoint rivals to Allah and commit association. You say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and you will,’ and you say, ‘By the Kaʿbah.’”
The Prophet ﷺ therefore instructed them, whenever they wished to swear an oath, to say:
“By the Lord of the Kaʿbah,”
and to say:
“Whatever Allah wills, then what you will.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3773).

11894 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When one of you swears an oath, he should not say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and you will.’ Rather, he should say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then what you will.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2117).
These verses contain proof of the existence of the Wise Creator, because this perfectly ordered universe must necessarily have a maker.
A desert Arab was asked:
“What is the evidence for the existence of the Lord, the Exalted?”
He replied:
“A camel dropping indicates the presence of a camel. Dung indicates the presence of a donkey. Footprints indicate that someone has traveled.
A sky filled with constellations, an earth containing broad pathways, and seas filled with waves—do these not indicate the existence of the Forbearing, All-Knowing, All-Powerful Creator?”
Al-Rāzī mentioned this in his Tafsīr (2/99–100).

11895 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There was no prophet except that he was given signs through which people believed in him. What I was specifically given was revelation which Allah revealed to me. I therefore hope to have the greatest number of followers on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4981) and Muslim (152).
The words:
“He was given signs through which people believed in him”
mean that every prophet was given extraordinary miracles that caused people to believe in him. This also indicates that each prophet was granted more than one miracle.
The words:
“What I was specifically given was revelation which Allah revealed to me”
refer to the Qur’an.
It is one of the greatest miracles of the Prophet ﷺ, alongside his many other miracles. He was especially distinguished by the Qur’an, just as every prophet was distinguished by a particular miracle. Moses, peace be upon him, was distinguished by the staff, while Jesus, peace be upon him, was distinguished by bringing the dead to life, and similarly with the other prophets.

11896 — Jābir narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“The people of Paradise will eat and drink there, but they will not spit, urinate, defecate, or blow their noses.”
They asked:
“What will happen to the food they consume?”
He replied:
“It will leave through belching and perspiration like the fragrance of musk. They will be inspired to glorify and praise Allah just as you are instinctively inspired to breathe.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2835).
Belching is the release of air from the stomach after it has become full.

11897 — Abū Dharr narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Which words are best?”
He replied:
“The words Allah chose for His angels—or for His servants: ‘Glory and praise belong to Allah.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2731).
The words:
“I know what you do not know”
mean that Allah knew that among these successive generations would be prophets, righteous people, and their followers, while the angels did not know this.
This also affirms that knowledge of the unseen belongs exclusively to Allah, the Exalted, and not to anyone else.

11898 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the son of Adam recites a verse of prostration and then prostrates, Satan withdraws and weeps, saying: ‘Woe to me! The son of Adam was commanded to prostrate and he prostrated, so Paradise is his. I was commanded to prostrate, but I refused, so the Fire is mine.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (81).

11899 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is a group of jinn in Madinah who have embraced Islam. Whoever sees one of these house-dwelling snakes should warn it for three days. If it appears to him after that, he should kill it, for it is a devil.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2236/141).

11900 — Usāmah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“A man will be brought on the Day of Resurrection and thrown into the Fire. His intestines will spill out, and he will circle around them as a donkey circles around a millstone.
The people of the Fire will gather around him and ask, ‘So-and-so, what has happened to you? Did you not command what is right and forbid what is wrong?’
He will reply, ‘Yes. I used to command what is right, but I did not practice it, and I used to forbid what is wrong, but I committed it.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3267) and Muslim (2989).

11901 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“On the night when I was taken on the Night Journey, I passed by people whose lips were being cut with scissors of fire.
I asked, ‘Who are these people?’
It was said, ‘They are preachers from among the people of the world who used to command others to righteousness while forgetting themselves, even though they recited the Scripture. Will they not understand?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (4069) and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (4965).

11902 — Saʿīd ibn Zayd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Truffles are from the manna which Allah sent down upon the Children of Israel, and their water is a cure for the eye.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4478) and Muslim (2049/160). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.
Mujāhid said:
“Manna was a gum-like substance, and al-salwā was a bird.”

11903 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“It was said to the Children of Israel:
‘Enter the gate prostrating and say, “Relieve us of our sins.”’
But they altered the command. They entered crawling upon their buttocks and said, ‘A grain in a hair.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4641) and Muslim (3015).

11904 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said to the Children of Israel:
‘Enter the gate prostrating and say, “Relieve us of our sins.”’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4006).
Al-Bazzār narrated the report in detail in Kashf al-Astār (1812), through Isḥāq ibn Bahlūl, from Muḥammad ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Fudayk, from Hishām ibn Saʿd, from Zayd ibn Aslam, from ʿAṭāʾ ibn Yasār, from Abū Saʿīd, who said:
We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When we reached ʿUsfān, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The scouts of the polytheists are now stationed at Ḍajnān. Which of you knows the route of Dhāt al-Ḥanẓal?”
When evening came, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Is there a man who will dismount and walk ahead of the riding animals?”
A man said:
“I will, O Messenger of Allah.”
He dismounted, but the rocks struck him and the branches caught upon his clothing.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Mount your animal.”
Another man then dismounted, but the rocks struck him and the branches caught upon his clothing.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Mount your animal.”
We eventually found the route and traveled through a mountain pass called al-Ḥanẓal.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The example of this pass is like the gate through which the Children of Israel entered. It was said to them:
‘Enter the gate prostrating and say, “Relieve us of our sins.”’
No one will pass through this mountain pass tonight except that he will be forgiven.”
The people began hurrying through it. The last person to pass was Qatādah ibn al-Nuʿmān, who was at the rear of the group.
The people began crowding and overtaking one another until we all rejoined.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then dismounted, and we also dismounted.
This means that they violated both the action and the statement they had been commanded to perform.
They were commanded to prostrate, meaning to enter humbly, but they replaced humility with crawling upon their buttocks.
They were commanded to say:
“Relieve us of our sins,”
meaning:
“Forgive us,”
but they replaced it with a word referring to wheat, doing so out of mockery and arrogance.

11905 — Ḥudhayfah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Trials will be presented to the hearts one after another, like the reeds of a woven mat. Any heart that absorbs them will have a black mark placed upon it, while any heart that rejects them will have a white mark placed upon it.
Eventually, hearts will become of two kinds. One will be white like a smooth stone, and no trial will harm it as long as the heavens and the earth remain.
The other will be black and dust-colored, like an overturned vessel. It will neither recognize what is right nor reject what is wrong, except according to the desires it has absorbed.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (144).

11906 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“What is lawful is clear, and what is unlawful is clear. Between them are doubtful matters which many people do not know.
Whoever avoids doubtful matters safeguards his religion and honor. Whoever falls into doubtful matters falls into what is unlawful, like a shepherd grazing around a protected sanctuary who is likely to enter it.
Every king has a protected sanctuary, and Allah’s protected sanctuary consists of the things He has forbidden.
Within the body is a piece of flesh. If it is sound, the entire body is sound, and if it is corrupt, the entire body is corrupt. Indeed, it is the heart.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (52) and Muslim (1599). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

11907 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“We are an unlettered nation. We neither write nor calculate. The month is like this and like this.”
He meant that the lunar month is sometimes twenty-nine days and sometimes thirty days.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1913) and Muslim (1080/15).

11908 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Jahl said:
“If I see Muḥammad praying near the Kaʿbah, I will go to him and place my foot upon his neck.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Had he done so, the angels would have seized him before his very eyes. Had the Jews wished for death, they would have died and seen their places in the Fire. Had those who challenged the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to invoke Allah’s curse upon the liars gone forward with it, they would have returned to find neither wealth nor family.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2226), Abū Yaʿlā (2604) — whose wording is cited here — and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2189) in an abridged form.

11909 — Anas narrated:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām heard of the arrival of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was harvesting fruit in an orchard.
He came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“I will ask you about three matters which no one knows except a prophet. What is the first sign of the Hour? What will be the first food eaten by the people of Paradise? What causes a child to resemble his father or his mother?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Gabriel informed me of these matters a short while ago.”
ʿAbdullāh asked:
“Gabriel?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“He is the enemy of the Jews among the angels.”
The Prophet ﷺ then recited:
“Whoever is an enemy to Gabriel—it is he who brought it down upon your heart.”
He then said:
“The first sign of the Hour will be a fire that gathers people from the east to the west. The first food eaten by the people of Paradise will be the extra lobe of a fish’s liver. When the man’s fluid precedes the woman’s fluid, the child resembles him, and when the woman’s fluid precedes, the child resembles her.”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“I testify that there is no deity except Allah, and I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah. O Messenger of Allah, the Jews are a people of false accusations. If they learn of my Islam before you question them concerning me, they will slander me.”
The Jews came, and the Prophet ﷺ asked:
“What kind of man is ʿAbdullāh among you?”
They replied:
“He is the best of us and the son of the best of us, our leader and the son of our leader.”
He asked:
“What would you say if ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām embraced Islam?”
They replied:
“May Allah protect him from that.”
ʿAbdullāh then came out and said:
“I testify that there is no deity except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah.”
They immediately said:
“He is the worst of us and the son of the worst of us,”
and began disparaging him.
ʿAbdullāh said:
“This is exactly what I feared, O Messenger of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4480).

11910 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A group of Jews came to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ one day and said:
“Now tell us: Which angel is your ally? On that basis, we will either join you or separate from you.”
He replied:
“My ally is Gabriel, peace be upon him. Allah never sent a prophet except that Gabriel was his ally.”
They said:
“Then we separate ourselves from you. Had another angel been your ally, we would have followed and believed you.”
He asked:
“What prevents you from believing Gabriel?”
They replied:
“He is our enemy.”
At that time Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Say: Whoever is an enemy to Gabriel—it is he who brought it down upon your heart by Allah’s permission,”
continuing until His words:
“They cast the Book of Allah behind their backs as though they did not know.”
Because of this:
“They incurred wrath upon wrath.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2514) and (2483).

11911 — ʿUmar said:
“The most proficient reciter among us is Ubayy, and the most knowledgeable in judgment among us is ʿAlī. Yet we leave some of what Ubayy says, because Ubayy says, ‘I will not abandon anything I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ,’ while Allah, the Exalted, has said:
‘Whatever verse We abrogate or cause to be forgotten…’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4481).
Ubayy’s statement:
“I will not abandon anything”
means that he would not leave anything he had heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
In other words, he did not consider anything from the Qur’an to have been abrogated.
ʿUmar responded by citing the words of Allah:
“Whatever verse We abrogate or cause to be forgotten,”
because this verse establishes the reality of abrogation.
As for the recitation:
“Or We delay it,”
this is the reading of Ibn Kathīr and Abū ʿAmr, and it means that Allah postpones it.
The reading:
“Or cause it to be forgotten”
is the recitation of the remaining Qur’anic readers and is derived from forgetfulness.
Allah preserves in the heart of the Prophet ﷺ whatever He wills, as He said:
“We will make you recite, and you will not forget.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿlā 87:6]
He may also cause His Prophet to forget whatever He wills and abrogate it.
This refutes the Jews who claim that abrogation cannot occur within Allah’s rulings, even though abrogation occurred in previous scriptures and earlier religious laws.
For example, Adam was permitted to marry his daughters to his sons, but this was later forbidden.
Allah also permitted Noah, after leaving the Ark, to eat all animals, after which the permissibility of some of them was abrogated.
Jesus, peace be upon him, likewise abrogated some rulings of the Torah, such as the prohibition of working on Saturday.
Likewise, the Qur’an abolished certain rulings introduced into Christianity by its scholars, such as permitting the consumption of pork and venerating the cross.
Absolute judgment belongs to Allah.
He confirms whatever He wills and abrogates whatever He wills. Allah has power over all things.

11912 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The Muslim who commits the greatest offense against the Muslims is one who asks about something that had not been prohibited, causing it to become prohibited because of his question.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7289) and Muslim (2358/132).

11913 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to prohibit us from:
“He said and she said,” excessive questioning, and wasting wealth.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1477) and Muslim (593/13).
The words of Allah:
“As Moses was questioned before”
indicate that believers should not question their Messenger stubbornly or challengingly, as the Children of Israel questioned Moses, peace be upon him, out of obstinacy and denial.
However, asking questions for the purpose of learning is praiseworthy.
The Prophet ﷺ encouraged teaching and learning in many hadiths.

11914 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would perform voluntary prayer while traveling from Makkah toward Madinah upon his riding animal, facing whichever direction it turned.
Ibn ʿUmar said:
The following verse was revealed concerning this:
“Wherever you turn, there is the Face of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (700/34).

11915 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said: ‘The son of Adam has denied Me, though he had no right to do so, and he has insulted Me, though he had no right to do so.
As for his denial of Me, it is his claim that I am unable to restore him as he was.
As for his insulting Me, it is his statement that I have a child. Glory be to Me! I am far above taking a wife or a child.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4482).
Insult means describing someone with qualities that diminish and degrade him.
Attributing a child to Allah, the Exalted, necessarily entails implications which are entirely unbefitting His majesty and greatness.

11916 — ʿUmar said:
“I agreed with Allah in three matters—or my Lord agreed with me in three matters.
I said:
‘O Messenger of Allah, perhaps you should take the Station of Abraham as a place of prayer.’
I also said:
‘O Messenger of Allah, both righteous and wicked people enter upon you. Perhaps you should command the Mothers of the Believers to observe the veil.’
Allah then revealed the verse of ḥijāb.
ʿUmar continued:
I learned that the Prophet ﷺ had reproached some of his wives, so I entered upon them and said:
‘You must cease, or Allah will replace you with wives better than you for His Messenger ﷺ.’
When I came to one of his wives, she said:
‘ʿUmar, does the Messenger of Allah ﷺ not possess sufficient ability to admonish his wives, such that you must admonish them?’
Allah then revealed:
“Perhaps, if he divorces you, his Lord will replace you with wives better than you—women who are Muslim…”
 [Sūrat al-Taḥrīm 66:5]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4483).

11917 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated concerning the pilgrimage of the Prophet ﷺ:
When the Prophet ﷺ completed ṭawāf, ʿUmar asked him:
“Is this the Station of our father Abraham?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿUmar asked:
“Should we not take it as a place of prayer?”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Take the Station of Abraham as a place of prayer.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (1196).

11918 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated in a lengthy hadith concerning the construction of the Kaʿbah:
Abraham remained away from them for as long as Allah willed. He later returned and found Ishmael sharpening arrows beneath a large tree near Zamzam.
When Ishmael saw him, he stood and went toward him. They greeted one another as a father greets his son and a son greets his father.
Abraham then said:
“Ishmael, Allah has commanded me to do something.”
Ishmael replied:
“Then do whatever your Lord has commanded you.”
Abraham asked:
“Will you help me?”
He replied:
“I will help you.”
Abraham said:
“Allah has commanded me to build a House here,”
and he pointed toward a raised mound above the surrounding ground.
They then raised the foundations of the House. Ishmael brought the stones while Abraham built.
When the structure became high, Ishmael brought this stone and placed it for Abraham. Abraham stood upon it while building, and Ishmael continued handing him the stones. Both of them said:
“Our Lord, accept this from us. You are indeed the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:127]
They continued building while moving around the House and repeating:
“Our Lord, accept this from us. You are indeed the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:127]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3364).

11919 — The Mother of the Believers, ʿĀʾishah, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you not see that your people rebuilt the Kaʿbah but stopped short of the foundations of Abraham?”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will you not restore it upon the foundations of Abraham?”
He replied:
“Were it not that your people have only recently left disbelief, I would do so.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar said:
“If ʿĀʾishah truly heard this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, then I believe the Messenger of Allah ﷺ refrained from touching the two corners adjoining the Ḥijr only because the House was not completed upon the foundations of Abraham.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (109), al-Bukhārī (4484), and Muslim (1333/399).
This noble verse indicates that the foundations existed before Abraham, peace be upon him.
However, no authentic report establishes who first built the noble Kaʿbah, for all the reports transmitted concerning that matter are weak.
It is strange that some claim these reports are taken from Israelite traditions, while failing to realize that the Israelites do not acknowledge Abraham’s migration with his wife Hājar to Makkah.
In the book Judaism and Christianity, I demonstrated from the texts of the Torah that Abraham, peace be upon him, migrated to Makkah and that the event involving the sacrifice of his son Ishmael took place in Minā. Refer to that work.

11920 — ʿAmr ibn Dīnār and ʿUbaydullāh ibn Abī Yazīd said:
During the time of the Prophet ﷺ, there was no wall surrounding the Sacred House. People would pray around it.
During the caliphate of ʿUmar, he constructed a wall around it.
ʿUbaydullāh said:
“Its wall was short, so Ibn al-Zubayr later rebuilt it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3830).
The word jadr, with the letter jīm vowelled with fatḥah and the dāl still, means a wall. Its plural is judrān.

11921 — Al-ʿIrbāḍ ibn Sāriyah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was already the servant of Allah and the Seal of the Prophets while Adam, peace be upon him, was still lying in his clay.
I will tell you the beginning of my affair: the supplication of my father Abraham, the glad tidings of Jesus concerning me, and the vision seen by my mother. The mothers of the prophets likewise see such visions.”
Another narration states:
“My mother saw in a vision that a light emerged from her by which the palaces of Syria were illuminated.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17150), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (18/629), and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2365).

11922 — Khālid ibn Maʿdān narrated from a number of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that they asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell us about yourself.”
He replied:
“I am the fulfillment of the supplication of my father Abraham, the glad tidings given by Jesus, and the vision of my mother. While she was pregnant with me, she saw a light emerge from her by which Buṣrā was illuminated.”
Buṣrā is located in the land of Syria.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/600).
Buṣrā was the largest city of the region of Ḥawrān.

11923 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent ʿUmar to collect the obligatory charity.
It was reported that Ibn Jamīl, Khālid ibn al-Walīd, and al-ʿAbbās, the paternal uncle of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, had withheld it.
The narrator then mentioned the remainder of the hadith, in which the Prophet ﷺ said:
“ʿUmar, do you not know that a man’s paternal uncle is like his father?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1468) and Muslim (983/11). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.
The phrase “like his father” means that the paternal uncle resembles the father in status.
The original meaning of ṣinw is two palm trees growing from a single root.
The hadith means that the paternal uncle should be honored and respected like the father.

11924 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The People of the Scripture used to recite the Torah in Hebrew and explain it in Arabic to the Muslims.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not affirm what the People of the Scripture tell you, and do not declare it false. Rather, say:
‘We believe in Allah and in what was revealed to us.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4485).
This hadith establishes the principle that one must refrain from issuing a judgment concerning matters and reports whose truth is uncertain.
Such matters should not be conclusively judged valid or false, lawful or unlawful, without evidence.
We have been commanded to believe in the scriptures revealed to the prophets.
However, the Jewish and Christian custodians of those books altered and changed them. We therefore have no certain way of knowing which of their reports are authentic or whether what they relate from those books is accurate.
We were therefore commanded to withhold judgment.
We do not affirm their reports, lest we agree with them concerning what they altered and changed.
Nor do we reject them outright, because the report may be true, in which case we would be denying something we were commanded to believe.
We therefore say:
“We believe in whatever scripture Allah revealed.”
Al-Khaṭṭābī explained this in Aʿlām al-Ḥadīth (3/1801).

11925 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed facing Bayt al-Maqdis for sixteen or seventeen months.
He desired that his qiblah be directed toward the Sacred House.
He performed the afternoon prayer—or one of the prayers—with a group of people.
A man who had prayed with him then left and passed by the people of another mosque while they were bowing in prayer.
He said:
“I testify by Allah that I have just prayed with the Prophet ﷺ facing Makkah.”
They immediately turned while remaining in prayer and faced the Sacred House.
Some men had died or been killed while praying toward the former qiblah before it was changed toward the Sacred House, and we did not know what to say concerning them.
Allah then revealed:
“Allah would never allow your faith to be lost. Allah is indeed Most Kind and Most Merciful to mankind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4486) and Muslim (525). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The word:
“Your faith”
means your prayers.

11926 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ was directed to face the Kaʿbah, the Companions asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what will happen to our brothers who died while praying toward Bayt al-Maqdis?”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Allah would never allow your faith to be lost.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4680), al-Tirmidhī (2964), Aḥmad (2691), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (1717) and al-Ḥākim (2/269).

11927 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
While the people were performing the dawn prayer in the mosque of Qubāʾ, a man came to them and said:
“Allah has revealed Qur’an to the Prophet ﷺ commanding him to face the Kaʿbah, so face it.”
They therefore turned toward the Kaʿbah while remaining in prayer.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4488) and Muslim (525). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11928 — Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to pray facing Bayt al-Maqdis. Then the following verse was revealed:
“We have certainly seen the turning of your face toward the sky. We will therefore surely turn you toward a direction of prayer that pleases you. So turn your face toward the Sacred Mosque, and wherever you may be, turn your faces toward it. Those who were given the Scripture certainly know that this is the truth from their Lord, and Allah is not unaware of what they do.”
A man from Banū Salamah passed by some people while they were bowing in the dawn prayer, after they had completed one rakʿah.
He called out:
“The direction of prayer has now been changed.”
They immediately turned, while remaining in prayer, toward the new qiblah.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (527).
The words of Allah, the Exalted:
“Thus We have made you a balanced and just nation, so that you may be witnesses over mankind and the Messenger may be a witness over you.”

11929 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Noah will be summoned on the Day of Resurrection, and he will say, ‘Here I am at Your service, my Lord.’
Allah will ask, ‘Did you convey the message?’
He will reply, ‘Yes.’
His nation will then be asked, ‘Did he convey the message to you?’
They will reply, ‘No warner came to us.’
Allah will ask Noah, ‘Who will testify for you?’
He will reply, ‘Muḥammad and his nation.’
They will testify that he conveyed the message, and the Messenger will be a witness over you. This is the meaning of the words of Allah, Glorious is His mention:
‘Thus We have made you a balanced and just nation, so that you may be witnesses over mankind and the Messenger may be a witness over you.’
The word ‘balanced’ means just.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4487).

11930 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The People of the Scripture used to recite the Torah in Hebrew and explain it in Arabic to the Muslims.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not affirm what the People of the Scripture tell you, and do not declare it false. Rather, say:
‘We believe in Allah and in what was revealed to us.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4485).

11931 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, the Exalted, says: ‘I am as My servant expects Me to be, and I am with him when he remembers Me.
If he remembers Me within himself, I remember him within Myself. If he remembers Me in a gathering, I remember him in a gathering better than it.
If he draws near to Me by a handspan, I draw near to him by a cubit. If he draws near to Me by a cubit, I draw near to him by an arm’s length. If he comes to Me walking, I come to him swiftly.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7405) and Muslim (2675).

11932 — Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that she heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No Muslim is afflicted by a calamity and then says what Allah commanded:
‘Indeed, we belong to Allah and to Him we will return. O Allah, reward me for my calamity and replace it for me with something better,’
except that Allah replaces it for him with something better.”
She said:
When Abū Salamah died, I said to myself:
“Which Muslim could be better than Abū Salamah, from the first household to migrate to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
Nevertheless, I recited the supplication, and Allah replaced him for me with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
She continued:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to propose marriage to me on his behalf.
I said:
“I have a daughter, and I am a woman who experiences jealousy.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“As for her daughter, we will supplicate to Allah to make her independent of her, and I will supplicate to Allah to remove her jealousy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (918).
Muslim narrated it through another route with the wording:
Umm Salamah said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No servant is afflicted by a calamity and says:
‘Indeed, we belong to Allah and to Him we will return. O Allah, reward me for my calamity and replace it for me with something better,’
except that Allah rewards him for his calamity and replaces it for him with something better.”
Umm Salamah said:
When Abū Salamah died, I said what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had commanded me to say. Allah then replaced him for me with someone better than him: the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.

11933 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah intends good for someone, He afflicts him with trials.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in al-Muwaṭṭaʾ (1752) and al-Bukhārī (5645).
Concerning the words “He afflicts him,” Abū ʿUbayd al-Harawī said:
“It means that Allah tests him with calamities in order to reward him for enduring them.”

11934 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Abū Hurayrah narrated that they heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No fatigue, illness, anxiety, sorrow, harm, or distress afflicts a Muslim—even the prick of a thorn—except that Allah expiates some of his sins through it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5641–5642) and Muslim (2573). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11935 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated that he asked ʿĀʾishah:
“What do you think about the words of Allah, the Exalted:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah. Whoever performs Ḥajj or ʿUmrah at the House incurs no sin by going between them’?
By Allah, it would appear that there is no sin upon anyone who does not go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.”
She replied:
“What a terrible interpretation you have made, son of my sister! Had the verse meant what you understood, it would have said: ‘There is no sin upon him if he does not go between them.’
Rather, it was revealed concerning the Anṣār. Before accepting Islam, they used to enter the state of pilgrimage devotion in the name of the idol Manāt, which they worshipped near al-Mushallal. Those who had entered pilgrimage devotion for Manāt considered it sinful to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.
After embracing Islam, they asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about this, saying, ‘O Messenger of Allah, we used to consider it sinful to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.’
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah…’”
ʿĀʾishah, may Allah be pleased with her, said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ established going between them as a religious practice, so no one is permitted to abandon going between them.”
ʿUrwah continued:
I informed Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān of this, and he said:
“This is knowledge I had not previously heard.
I have heard learned men mention that all people used to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, except those whom ʿĀʾishah mentioned who had entered pilgrimage devotion for Manāt.
When Allah mentioned circumambulation of the House in the Qur’an but did not mention al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, they asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, we used to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah. Allah has revealed the command to circumambulate the House but has not mentioned al-Ṣafā. Is there any sin upon us if we go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah?’
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah…’”
Abū Bakr said:
“It appears that this verse was revealed concerning both groups:
Those who, during the pre-Islamic period, considered it sinful to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah;
and those who had previously gone between them but later hesitated to do so after accepting Islam, because Allah had commanded circumambulation of the House without initially mentioning al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, until He mentioned them afterward.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1643) and Muslim (1277/261).

11936 — ʿĀṣim ibn Sulaymān narrated:
I asked Anas ibn Mālik about al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.
He replied:
“We used to regard going between them as a practice belonging to the pre-Islamic period. When Islam came, we refrained from it.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah. Whoever performs Ḥajj or ʿUmrah at the House incurs no sin by going between them. Whoever voluntarily performs additional good, then Allah is Appreciative and All-Knowing.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4496) and Muslim (1278).

11937 — Jābir narrated in the lengthy hadith describing the pilgrimage of the Prophet ﷺ:
The Prophet ﷺ then went out through the gate toward al-Ṣafā.
When he approached al-Ṣafā, he recited:
“Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah.”
He then said:
“I begin with that which Allah mentioned first.”
He began with al-Ṣafā and climbed it until he could see the Sacred House.
He faced the qiblah, declared Allah’s oneness and greatness, and said:
“There is no deity except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all sovereignty and all praise, and He has power over all things.
There is no deity except Allah alone. He fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the confederate armies by Himself.”
He then supplicated between these declarations and repeated this three times.
He afterward descended toward al-Marwah. When his feet reached the bottom of the valley, he ran. When he began ascending, he walked until he reached al-Marwah.
There he performed the same acts that he had performed upon al-Ṣafā.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

11938 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ made one statement, and I made another.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever dies while calling upon a rival besides Allah will enter the Fire.”
I said:
“Whoever dies without calling upon any rival besides Allah will enter Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4497) and Muslim (92/150).
This second statement was made by ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.
It is well known that he exercised great caution before attributing statements directly to the Prophet ﷺ.
It has therefore been said that he heard both statements from the Prophet ﷺ. He explicitly attributed the one he remembered with certainty to him, while leaving the other as his own statement because he was uncertain about its precise attribution.
Both statements are, however, authentically established from the Prophet ﷺ in the following hadith of Jābir.

11939 — Jābir narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what are the two matters that make the outcome inevitable?”
He replied:
“Whoever dies without associating anything with Allah will enter Paradise, and whoever dies associating anything with Allah will enter the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (93/151).

11940 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O people, Allah is pure and accepts only what is pure. Allah has commanded the believers with the same command He gave to the messengers.
He said:
‘O messengers, eat from the good and lawful things and perform righteous deeds. I am fully aware of what you do.’
 [Sūrat al-Muʾminūn 23:51]
And He said:
‘O you who believe, eat from the good things We have provided for you.’
He then mentioned a man who travels for a long time, disheveled and covered in dust, raising his hands toward heaven and saying, ‘My Lord! My Lord!’
Yet his food is unlawful, his drink is unlawful, his clothing is unlawful, and he has been nourished with what is unlawful. How, then, could his supplication be answered?”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1015).

11941 — Ibn ʿUmar said:
“Two kinds of carrion and two kinds of blood have been made lawful for us: locusts and fish, and the liver and spleen.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī (1/254).
Ibn al-Qayyim said in Zād al-Maʿād (3/392):
“This is a ḥasan hadith. Although it is formally a statement of a Companion, it carries the ruling of a prophetic report, because when a Companion says, ‘Such-and-such was made lawful for us,’ or, ‘Such-and-such was forbidden to us,’ this refers to the declaration of lawfulness or prohibition by the Prophet ﷺ.”
End quote.

11942 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which charity brings the greatest reward?”
He replied:
“That you give charity while you are healthy and reluctant to part with your wealth, fearing poverty and hoping for wealth. Do not delay until your soul reaches your throat and then say, ‘So-and-so should receive this much, and so-and-so should receive this much,’ when it has already become the property of so-and-so.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1419) and Muslim (1032). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The Words of Allah: “And Those Who Ask”
This contains encouragement not to send a person who asks away empty-handed, as stated in the following hadith.

11943 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Bujayd narrated from his grandmother, Umm Bujayd, who was among the women who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
She said:
“O Messenger of Allah, a poor person may stand at my door, but I find nothing to give him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to her:
“Even if you find nothing to give him except a burned hoof, place it in his hand.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1666), al-Tirmidhī (665), al-Nasāʾī (2574), and Ibn Khuzaymah (2473).
The Words of Allah: “And Those Who Remain Patient during Hardship, Affliction, and at the Time of Battle”

11944 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
“By Allah, whenever the battle became fierce, we would seek protection behind him. The bravest among us was the one who stood alongside him.”
He meant the Prophet ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1776/79).

11945 — Anas narrated:
Al-Rubayyiʿ, the daughter of al-Naḍr, broke the front tooth of a young woman.
Her family offered financial compensation and asked the injured party to pardon her, but they refused. They came to the Prophet ﷺ, and he ordered legal retaliation.
Anas ibn al-Naḍr said:
“O Messenger of Allah, will al-Rubayyiʿ’s tooth be broken? No, by the One who sent you with the truth, her tooth will not be broken.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Anas, the ruling of Allah is legal retaliation.”
The injured family then became satisfied and granted pardon.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Among the servants of Allah are those who, if they swear by Allah that something will occur, Allah fulfills their oath.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2703).

11946 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
During the year of the conquest of Makkah, the tribe of Khuzāʿah killed a man from Banū Layth in retaliation for one of their men who had been killed during the pre-Islamic period.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood and said:
“Allah restrained the elephant from Makkah and gave authority over it to His Messenger and the believers. It was not made lawful for anyone before me, and it will not be made lawful for anyone after me. It was made lawful for me only for one hour of one day, and at this very hour it has returned to being sacred.
Its thorny plants may not be cut, its trees may not be felled, and its lost property may not be picked up except by one who will publicly announce it. Whoever has a relative murdered has the choice between two options: either blood money is paid to him or the killer is subjected to legal retaliation.”
The remainder of the hadith follows.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6880) and Muslim (1355). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11947 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Among the Children of Israel, there was legal retaliation, but there was no provision for blood money.
Allah, the Exalted, therefore said to this nation:
“O you who believe, legal retaliation has been prescribed for you concerning those murdered.”
The meaning of pardon is that blood money is accepted in a case of intentional killing.
The words:
“Then there should be a fair demand and payment made with kindness”
mean that the victim’s family should seek payment reasonably and the offender should pay it graciously.
The words:
“That is a concession and mercy from your Lord”
refer to a concession from what had been prescribed for those before you.
The words:
“Whoever transgresses after that will have a painful punishment”
refer to one who kills the offender after having already accepted the blood money.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4498).

11948 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“It is not proper for a Muslim who has something concerning which he should make a bequest to spend two nights without having his will written and kept with him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Waṣiyyah (1), al-Bukhārī (2738), and Muslim (1627).

11949 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ came to visit me while I was ill in Makkah. Saʿd disliked the possibility of dying in the land from which he had migrated.
He said:
“May Allah have mercy upon Ibn ʿAfrāʾ.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, may I bequeath all my wealth?”
He replied:
“No.”
I asked:
“Then half of it?”
He replied:
“No.”
I asked:
“Then one-third?”
He replied:
“One-third, and one-third is a great deal. It is better for you to leave your heirs wealthy than to leave them poor and begging from people.
Whatever you spend is charity for you, even the morsel of food you place in your wife’s mouth. Perhaps Allah will preserve you so that some people benefit through you while others are harmed through you.”
At that time, Saʿd had only one daughter.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2742) and Muslim (1628). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11950 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had commanded the people to fast on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ.
When fasting Ramadan was made obligatory, whoever wished continued fasting ʿĀshūrāʾ, and whoever wished left it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2001) and Muslim (1125/115).

11951 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ narrated:
When the verse:
“Those who are able to fast only with great difficulty may give a ransom by feeding a poor person”
was revealed, whoever wished could refrain from fasting and instead offer the ransom.
This remained the case until the following verse was revealed and abrogated that option.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4507) and Muslim (1145/149).
The meaning of the hadith is that when Allah revealed:
“Those who are able to fast only with great difficulty may give a ransom by feeding a poor person,”
whoever wished could fast, and whoever wished could instead feed a poor person.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed the following verse:
“The month of Ramadan is that in which the Qur’an was revealed,”
continuing until His words:
“Whoever among you witnesses the month must fast it.”
Allah thereby made fasting obligatory upon the healthy resident, permitted the ill person and traveler to make up the fast later, and retained the provision of feeding a poor person for the elderly person who is unable to fast.

11952 — ʿAṭāʾ narrated that he heard Ibn ʿAbbās recite:
“Those who are able to fast only with great difficulty may give a ransom by feeding a poor person.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“This verse has not been abrogated. It applies to an elderly man and an elderly woman who are unable to fast. They must feed one poor person for each day.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4505).
The established abrogation applies to a healthy resident, upon whom fasting became obligatory through the words of Allah:
“Whoever among you witnesses the month must fast it.”
As for a very elderly person who is unable to fast, he may refrain from fasting and is not required to make up the days.
The statement:
“They must feed one poor person for each day”
is the opinion of Ibn ʿAbbās and the majority of scholars.
When Anas became elderly, he would refrain from fasting and feed a poor person bread and meat for each day.
Another opinion, which is one of the two views attributed to al-Shāfiʿī, holds that feeding is not obligatory upon such a person, just as no obligation applies to a child who has not reached puberty, because Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity.
Refer to the Book of Fasting for further details.

11953 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Some of us fasted and others did not fast, and neither group criticized the other.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1117).
The Words of Allah: “So That You May Complete the Prescribed Number”

11954 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ mentioned Ramadan and said:
“Do not begin fasting until you see the crescent, and do not end the fast until you see it. If it is obscured from you, then calculate the month accordingly.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (633), al-Bukhārī (1906), and Muslim (1080).

11955 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Begin fasting when you see the crescent, and end the fast when you see it. If the month is obscured from you, then count thirty days.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1909) and Muslim (1081/19). The wording cited here is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī narrated a similar wording.

11956 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
We were traveling with the Prophet ﷺ, and the people began raising their voices while declaring Allah’s greatness.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O people, be gentle with yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. You are calling upon One who is All-Hearing and Near, and He is with you.”
The remainder of the hadith follows.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2704).

11957 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
When fasting Ramadan was first prescribed, the Muslims would not approach their wives throughout the entire month of Ramadan.
Some men nevertheless betrayed themselves, so Allah revealed:
“Allah knew that you were betraying yourselves, so He accepted your repentance and pardoned you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4508).

11958 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
When one of the Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ was fasting and the time for breaking the fast arrived, if he fell asleep before eating, he would not eat during that night or the following day until sunset.
Qays ibn Ṣirmah al-Anṣārī was fasting. When the time of breaking the fast came, he went to his wife and asked:
“Do you have any food?”
She replied:
“No, but I will go and look for some for you.”
He had been working throughout the day, and sleep overcame him.
When his wife returned and saw him asleep, she said:
“What a loss for you!”
By midday the following day, he lost consciousness.
The matter was mentioned to the Prophet ﷺ, and this verse was revealed:
“It has been made lawful for you to be intimate with your wives during the nights of fasting.”
The Muslims rejoiced greatly at its revelation.
The following words were also revealed:
“Eat and drink until the white thread becomes distinct to you from the black thread.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Fasting (1915), through ʿUbaydullāh ibn Mūsā, from Isrāʾīl, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ.

11959 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“O you who believe, fasting has been prescribed for you as it was prescribed for those before you”:
During the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, once the people performed the night prayer, food, drink, and marital relations became forbidden to them, and they continued fasting until the following night.
A man betrayed himself by having relations with his wife after performing the night prayer, even though he had not yet broken his fast.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, wished to make the matter easy for those who remained, as a concession and a benefit, so He said:
“Allah knew that you were betraying yourselves.”
This was among the things through which Allah benefited the people, granted them a concession, and made matters easy for them.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2313).

11960 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Before the relevant ruling concerning fasting was revealed, people were permitted to eat, drink, and approach their wives. However, once one of them fell asleep, he could no longer eat, drink, or approach his wife until breaking the fast on the following night.
It reached us that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, after falling asleep and thereby entering the state in which fasting became binding upon him, had relations with his wife.
He then came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“I complain to Allah and to you about what I have done.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“What did you do?”
He replied:
“My soul enticed me, and I approached my wife after I had slept, even though I intended to fast.”
They claimed that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“It was not fitting for you to do that.”
Then the following revelation came:
“It has been made lawful for you to be intimate with your wives during the nights of fasting.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mūsā ibn ʿUqbah, as mentioned by al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr (1/511). Its chain is authentic, except for the wording, “It was not fitting for you to do that,” which is unusual.

11961 — Mūsā ibn Jubayr, the freedman of Banū Salamah, narrated that he heard ʿAbdullāh ibn Kaʿb ibn Mālik relate from his father:
During Ramadan, if a fasting man reached the evening and then fell asleep, food, drink, and marital relations became forbidden to him until he broke his fast the following day.
One night, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb returned from the Prophet ﷺ after having remained with him late into the night.
He found that his wife had fallen asleep and desired her.
She said:
“I have already slept.”
He replied:
“You have not slept.”
He then had relations with her.
Kaʿb ibn Mālik did the same thing.
The following morning, ʿUmar went to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him, so Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Allah knew that you were betraying yourselves, so He accepted your repentance and pardoned you.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15795).

11962 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“It has been made lawful for you to be intimate with your wives during the nights of fasting”:
During Ramadan, once the Muslims performed the night prayer, women and food became forbidden to them until the same time on the following night.
Some Muslims nevertheless ate food and approached their wives after the night prayer during Ramadan. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was among them.
They complained about this to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so Allah revealed:
“Allah knew that you were betraying yourselves, so He accepted your repentance and pardoned you. Now, therefore, approach them,”
meaning: have marital relations with them.
And He revealed:
“Eat and drink until the white thread becomes distinct to you from the black thread of dawn.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī (3/235–236).

11963 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“Until the white thread becomes distinct to you from the black thread of dawn,”
I took a black cord and a white cord and placed them beneath my pillow.
I continued looking at them during the night, but I could not distinguish one from the other.
The following morning, I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and mentioned this to him.
He said:
“It refers only to the darkness of the night and the whiteness of the day.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1916) and Muslim (1090). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
In another narration recorded by both of them, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him:
“Your pillow must indeed be very broad if the white thread and black thread are beneath it.”
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4509) and Muslim (1090).
In another wording narrated by al-Bukhārī (4510), he said:
“You must indeed have a very broad neck if you can see the two threads.”
He then explained:
“No. Rather, it means the darkness of the night and the whiteness of the day.”

11964 — Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
The following words were first revealed:
“Eat and drink until the white thread becomes distinct to you from the black thread,”
but the words:
“Of dawn”
had not yet been revealed.
