When some men intended to fast, they would tie a white thread and a black thread to their legs and continue eating until they could distinguish between them.
Allah later revealed the words:
“Of dawn,”
and they then understood that the verse referred to the night and the day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Fasting (1917) and Muslim (1091/35).
The Words of Allah: “Then Complete the Fast until Nightfall”

11965 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the night approaches from this direction, the day withdraws from that direction, and the sun has set, the fasting person has reached the time of breaking his fast.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1954) and Muslim (1100). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The Words of Allah: “Do Not Approach Them while You Are Remaining in Seclusion in the Mosques”

11966 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
I would enter the house only for a necessary purpose. If there was an ill person inside, I would not ask about him except while passing by.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would extend his head to me while he remained in the mosque, and I would comb his hair.
When he was observing iʿtikāf, he would not enter the house except for a necessary purpose.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2029) and Muslim (297/7). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

11967 — Umm Salamah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I am only a human being, and you bring your disputes before me. Perhaps one of you is more eloquent and persuasive in presenting his argument than another, so I judge in his favor according to what I hear.
If I award someone anything that rightfully belongs to his brother, he must not take it, for I have only cut out for him a piece of the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6967) and Muslim (1713). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11968 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
This verse was revealed concerning us.
When the Anṣār performed Ḥajj and returned home, they would not enter their houses through the doors. Instead, they would enter from the rear.
A man from the Anṣār entered through his door and was apparently criticized for doing so.
Allah then revealed:
“Righteousness is not that you enter houses from the rear, but righteousness belongs to the one who fears Allah. Enter houses through their doors.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1803) and Muslim (3026).

11969 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Quraysh were known as al-Ḥums, and while in the state of iḥrām they would enter houses through their doors.
The Anṣār and the other Arabs, however, would not enter through doors while in iḥrām.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was once inside a garden and came out through its gate. Quṭbah ibn ʿĀmir al-Anṣārī came out through the gate with him.
The people said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Quṭbah ibn ʿĀmir is a sinful man. He came out through the gate with you.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked him:
“What caused you to do that?”
He replied:
“I saw you do it, so I did the same.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I am from al-Ḥums.”
The man replied:
“Your religion is my religion.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Righteousness is not that you enter houses from the rear, but righteousness belongs to the one who fears Allah. Enter houses through their doors.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/483) and al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 48).
Al-Wāḥidī then said:
“The commentators said that during the pre-Islamic period and at the beginning of Islam, when a man entered the state of iḥrām for Ḥajj or ʿUmrah, he would not enter an enclosure, house, or dwelling through its door.
If he was from the people of Madinah, he would make an opening in the rear of his house through which he entered and exited, or he would set up a ladder and climb over it.
If he lived in tents, he would leave from the rear of his tent or pavilion and would not enter through the front opening until he completed his state of iḥrām. They considered this a religious practice.
The only exception was one of al-Ḥums. These included Quraysh, Kinānah, Khuzāʿah, Thaqīf, Khathʿam, Banū ʿĀmir ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿah, and Banū al-Naḍr ibn Muʿāwiyah. They were called al-Ḥums because of their strictness in religion.
They said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ once entered a house belonging to one of the Anṣār. A man from the Anṣār followed him through the door while in iḥrām, and the people objected to him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked him, ‘Why did you enter through the door while in iḥrām?’
He replied, ‘I saw you enter through the door, so I followed you.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘I am from al-Ḥums.’
The man replied, ‘If you are from al-Ḥums, then I am also from al-Ḥums. Our religion is one. I am pleased with your guidance, manner, and religion.’
Allah then revealed this verse.”
End quote.

11970 — Buraydah reported:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed a commander over an army or military detachment, he would specifically advise him to fear Allah and to treat the Muslims accompanying him well.
He would then say:
“Fight in the name of Allah, in the path of Allah. Fight those who disbelieve in Allah. Go forth, but do not misappropriate the spoils, do not act treacherously, do not mutilate, and do not kill a child…”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1731/3).

11971 — Nāfiʿ narrated from Ibn ʿUmar:
Two men came to him during the turmoil involving Ibn al-Zubayr and said:
“The people have fallen into disorder. You are Ibn ʿUmar and a Companion of the Prophet ﷺ. What prevents you from going out to fight?”
He replied:
“What prevents me is that Allah has forbidden me from shedding the blood of my Muslim brother.”
They said:
“Did Allah not say:
‘Fight them until there is no more persecution’?”
He replied:
“We fought until there was no more persecution and religion belonged to Allah. You wish to fight until persecution returns and religion belongs to other than Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4513).
Al-Bukhārī (4514) said:
ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ added, narrating from Wahb, who said: So-and-so and Ḥaywah ibn Shurayḥ informed me, from Bakr ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, that Bukayr ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated to him from Nāfiʿ:
A man came to Ibn ʿUmar and asked:
“Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, what causes you to perform Ḥajj one year and ʿUmrah another year while abandoning fighting in the cause of Allah, although you know the encouragement Allah has given concerning it?”
He replied:
“Son of my brother, Islam is built upon five: belief in Allah and His Messenger, the five prayers, fasting Ramadan, paying zakāh, and pilgrimage to the House.”
The man said:
“Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, do you not hear what Allah mentioned in His Book:
‘If two groups of believers fight one another, reconcile between them. If one of them transgresses against the other, fight the transgressing group until it returns to Allah’s command’
 [Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:9],
and:
‘Fight them until there is no more persecution’?”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“We did that during the time of the Prophet ﷺ, when Islam was weak in numbers. A man would be persecuted because of his religion: they would either kill or torture him. When Islam became widespread, there was no longer persecution.”
The man asked:
“What is your opinion concerning ʿAlī and ʿUthmān?”
He replied:
“As for ʿUthmān, Allah pardoned him, but you disliked pardoning him. As for ʿAlī, he was the cousin and son-in-law of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He pointed with his hand and said:
“That is his house, where you can see it.”
End of the report.

11972 — Ḥudhayfah said concerning the words of Allah:
“Spend in the cause of Allah and do not throw yourselves into destruction”:
“It was revealed concerning spending.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4516).

11973 — Aslam Abū ʿImrān al-Tujībī narrated:
We were in a city of the Byzantine lands. The Byzantines brought out against us an enormous battle formation, and an equal or larger number of Muslims advanced against them.
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir commanded the troops from Egypt, while Faḍālah ibn ʿUbayd commanded the entire force.
A Muslim man charged into the Byzantine ranks until he entered among them.
The people shouted:
“Glory be to Allah! He is throwing himself into destruction!”
Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī stood and said:
“People, you are interpreting this verse in this manner, but it was revealed concerning us, the Anṣār.
When Allah strengthened Islam and increased its supporters, some of us secretly said to one another, without the Messenger of Allah ﷺ being present:
‘Our wealth has been neglected. Allah has strengthened Islam and increased its supporters. Perhaps we should remain with our property and repair what has been lost.’
Allah then revealed to His Prophet ﷺ, refuting what we had said:
‘Spend in the cause of Allah and do not throw yourselves into destruction.’
Destruction meant remaining with our property, restoring it, and abandoning military expeditions.”
Abū Ayyūb continued going forth in the cause of Allah until he was buried in Byzantine territory.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2512), al-Tirmidhī (2972), Ibn Abī Ḥātim (1/330–331), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4711).

11974 — Abū Jabīrah ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk narrated:
The Anṣār used to give charity and spend whatever Allah willed.
When a year of hardship afflicted them, they withheld their spending.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Spend in the cause of Allah and do not throw yourselves into destruction. Do good, for Allah loves those who do good.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (1/332) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (22/390).
The majority of scholars interpreted destruction in this verse as abandoning expenditure in the cause of Allah.
A second meaning of destruction is that a sinner assumes there can be no repentance for him. Reports have also been transmitted concerning this interpretation.

11975 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib said concerning the words of Allah:
“Spend in the cause of Allah and do not throw yourselves into destruction”:
“It refers to a man who commits sins and then throws himself into destruction by saying, ‘There is no repentance for me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī (3/319).

11976 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated:
A man would commit a sin and then say:
“Allah will never forgive me.”
Allah then revealed:
“Spend in the cause of Allah and do not throw yourselves into destruction.”
Good: Narrated by al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 51) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan (9/45).

11977 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Maʿqil narrated:
I sat beside Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah in this mosque—meaning the mosque of Kūfah—and asked him about the compensation involving fasting.
He replied:
I was carried to the Prophet ﷺ while lice were falling across my face.
He said:
“I did not realize that your suffering had reached this degree. Can you find a sheep?”
I replied:
“No.”
He said:
“Fast for three days, or feed six poor people, giving each poor person half a ṣāʿ of food, and shave your head.”
This verse was revealed specifically concerning me, but its ruling applies generally to all of you.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4517) and Muslim (1201/85).
The Words of Allah: “Whoever Performs ʿUmrah Followed by Ḥajj…”

11978 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn narrated:
The verse concerning tamattuʿ was revealed in the Book of Allah, and we practiced it with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
No Qur’anic verse was revealed prohibiting it, and the Prophet ﷺ did not forbid it before his death.
A man later expressed whatever opinion he wished concerning it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4518) and Muslim (1226/172). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The statement:
“A man later expressed whatever opinion he wished”
has been interpreted as referring to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who discouraged tamattuʿ so that people would continue journeying to the Sacred House separately as pilgrims for Ḥajj and as performers of ʿUmrah.

11979 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“There must be no sexual speech, sin, or quarreling during Ḥajj”:
“Sexual speech means explicit references to intercourse and approaching women concerning it. Sin includes every act of disobedience, and quarreling means a man arguing with his companion.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/22).
The Words of Allah: “Take Provisions, but the Best Provision Is God-Consciousness”

11980 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The people of Yemen used to perform Ḥajj without taking provisions, saying:
“We are those who place their trust in Allah.”
When they reached Makkah, they would beg from the people.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Take provisions, but the best provision is God-consciousness.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1523).

11981 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿUkāẓ, Majannah, and Dhū al-Majāz were markets during the pre-Islamic period.
The Muslims later feared that conducting trade during the pilgrimage season might be sinful.
Allah then revealed:
“There is no sin upon you in seeking bounty from your Lord,”
concerning trade during the seasons of Ḥajj.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4519).

11982 — Abū Umāmah al-Taymī narrated:
I used to hire out my riding animal and provide transportation along this route.
Some people told me:
“You will receive no Ḥajj.”
I met Ibn ʿUmar and said:
“Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, I hire out my services along this route, and some people tell me that I will receive no Ḥajj.”
Ibn ʿUmar asked:
“Do you not enter iḥrām, recite the talbiyah, circumambulate the House, depart from ʿArafāt, and stone the pillars?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then you have performed Ḥajj.
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked him the same question you asked me. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent and did not answer until this verse was revealed:
‘There is no sin upon you in seeking bounty from your Lord.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sent for the man, recited the verse to him, and said:
‘You have performed Ḥajj.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1733), Aḥmad (6434), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (3051, 3052) and al-Ḥākim (1/449).

11983 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Quraysh and those who followed their religion used to remain at Muzdalifah. They were known as al-Ḥums.
The rest of the Arabs would stand at ʿArafāt.
When Islam came, Allah commanded His Prophet ﷺ to go to ʿArafāt, remain there, and then depart from it.
This is the meaning of the words of Allah:
“Then depart from where the people depart.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4520) and Muslim (1219).
Al-Ḥums were Quraysh and those whose mothers were from Quraysh.
They were called al-Ḥums because of their intense strictness in religious practice.

11984 — ʿUrwah narrated:
During the pre-Islamic period, the people used to circumambulate the Kaʿbah naked, except for al-Ḥums. Al-Ḥums were Quraysh and those born to women of Quraysh.
Al-Ḥums considered themselves responsible for providing garments to the people.
A man from al-Ḥums would give another man clothing in which to perform ṭawāf, and a woman from al-Ḥums would give another woman clothing in which to perform ṭawāf.
Whoever was not given clothing by al-Ḥums would circumambulate the House naked.
The general population would depart from ʿArafāt, while al-Ḥums would depart from Jamʿ, meaning Muzdalifah.
Hishām said:
My father narrated to me from ʿĀʾishah that this verse was revealed concerning al-Ḥums:
“Then depart from where the people depart.”
They had previously departed from Jamʿ, so they were commanded to proceed to ʿArafāt.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1665) and Muslim (1219/152). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11985 — Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to say:
“O Allah, our Lord, grant us good in this world, good in the Hereafter, and protect us from the punishment of the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4522). He also narrated it (6389) through his chain with the wording:
“The supplication most frequently made by the Prophet ﷺ was: ‘O Allah, our Lord, grant us good in this world, good in the Hereafter, and protect us from the punishment of the Fire.’”
Muslim (2690) narrated through his chain that Qatādah asked Anas:
“Which supplication did the Prophet ﷺ make most frequently?”
He replied:
“The supplication he made most frequently was: ‘O Allah, our Lord, grant us good in this world, good in the Hereafter, and protect us from the punishment of the Fire.’”

11986 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yaʿmar al-Dīlī narrated:
I witnessed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was standing at ʿArafah. Some people from Najd came to him and asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how is Ḥajj performed?”
He replied:
“Ḥajj is ʿArafah. Whoever reaches ʿArafah before the dawn prayer on the night of Muzdalifah has completed his Ḥajj. The days of Minā are three:
‘Whoever hastens to leave after two days incurs no sin, and whoever remains longer incurs no sin.’”
He then seated a man behind him on his mount, and the man continued proclaiming these words.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1949), al-Tirmidhī (889), Aḥmad (18774), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3892).

11987 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The most hated of men in the sight of Allah is the fiercely argumentative opponent.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4523) and Muslim (2668).
The word al-aladd means one who is intensely quarrelsome.
The word al-khaṣim means one who is skilled in argumentation.
What is condemned is arguing through falsehood in order to repel a right or establish a false claim.

11988 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The following verse was revealed to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Among mankind is one who sells himself seeking Allah’s pleasure.”
When the Prophet ﷺ saw him—meaning Ṣuhayb—he said:
“Abū Yaḥyā, the transaction has been profitable.”
He then recited the verse to him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/398).
The report includes a story narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/398) through Sulaymān ibn Ḥarb, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Ayyūb, from ʿIkrimah, who said:
When Ṣuhayb departed as an emigrant, the people of Makkah pursued him.
He emptied his quiver and brought out forty arrows, saying:
“You will not reach me until I place an arrow in each one of you. After that, I will turn to my sword, and you know that I am a capable fighter. I have left behind two female slaves in Makkah, and they are yours.”

11989 — Usāmah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I stood at the gate of Paradise and found that most of those who entered it were the poor, while the people of wealth and worldly fortune were being held back, although the people destined for the Fire had already been ordered toward it.
I then stood at the gate of the Fire and found that most of those who entered it were women.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5196) and Muslim (2736). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

11990 — Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated:
A man passed by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and the Prophet ﷺ asked a man sitting with him:
“What is your opinion of this man?”
He replied:
“He is one of the noble people. By Allah, if he proposes marriage, he deserves to be accepted, and if he intercedes, his intercession deserves to be accepted.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent.
Another man then passed by, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What is your opinion of this man?”
He replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, he is one of the poor Muslims. If he proposes marriage, he is unlikely to be accepted; if he intercedes, his intercession is unlikely to be accepted; and if he speaks, people are unlikely to listen to him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“This man is better than an earth full of men like the first.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6447).

11991 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I looked into Paradise and saw that most of its inhabitants were the poor. I looked into the Fire and saw that most of its inhabitants were women.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6449).

11992 — Abū Umāmah narrated:
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, was Adam a prophet?”
He replied:
“Yes, a prophet to whom Allah spoke.”
The man asked:
“How much time was there between him and Noah?”
He replied:
“Ten generations.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6190).
The word qarn may mean one hundred years, which is the immediately apparent meaning.
It has also been said to mean a generation of people.
The generations before Noah lived for very long periods, just as Noah, peace be upon him, remained among his people for one thousand years minus fifty. On this interpretation, ten qurūn means ten generations, spanning thousands of years.

11993 — Khabbāb ibn al-Aratt narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was reclining upon his cloak in the shade of the Kaʿbah. We had suffered severely at the hands of the polytheists.
I said:
“Will you not supplicate to Allah for us?”
He sat up, his face reddened, and said:
“Among those before you, a man would be combed with iron combs until the flesh and sinews were stripped from his bones, yet that would not turn him away from his religion.
A saw would be placed upon the center of his head and he would be cut into two halves, yet that would not turn him away from his religion.
Allah will certainly complete this matter until a rider travels from Ṣanʿāʾ to Ḥaḍramawt fearing none but Allah.”
Bayān added:
“And fearing only the wolf for his sheep.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3852).
The claim that this verse was revealed concerning the Battle of the Confederates is not established through an authentic report.
Al-Wāḥidī mentioned in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 60), from Qatādah and al-Suddī, that the verse was revealed during the Battle of the Trench, when the Muslims suffered exhaustion, severe hardship, heat, fear, cold, restricted living conditions, and many forms of harm.
Their condition was as Allah, the Exalted, said:
“The hearts reached the throats.”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:10]
ʿAbd al-Razzāq narrated in his Tafsīr (1/332) from Qatādah concerning the words of Allah:
“The example of those who passed before you has not yet come to you; hardship and affliction touched them,”
that he said:
“It was revealed on the Day of the Confederates,”
and he mentioned a similar explanation.
This is the view of most commentators, although no connected authentic report has been established concerning it.

11994 — Jundub ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated from the Prophet ﷺ:
The Prophet ﷺ dispatched a group and appointed Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ—or ʿUbaydah—as their commander.
When he prepared to depart, he wept out of intense longing to remain with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so he stayed behind.
The Prophet ﷺ appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaḥsh in his place and gave him a written letter. He ordered him not to open it until he reached a specified location and said:
“Do not compel any of your companions to continue traveling with you.”
When ʿAbdullāh read the letter, he said:
“Indeed, we belong to Allah and to Him we will return.”
He then said:
“We hear and obey Allah and His Messenger.”
He informed his companions of the instructions and read the letter to them.
Two men returned, while the rest continued.
They encountered Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī and killed him, not knowing whether that day belonged to Rajab or Jumādā.
The polytheists said to the Muslims:
“You have killed during the sacred month.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“They ask you about fighting during the sacred month. Say: Fighting during it is a grave matter…”
Some people then said:
“Even if they did not incur sin, they will receive no reward.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Indeed, those who believe, emigrate, and strive in the cause of Allah may hope for Allah’s mercy. Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1534), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (2/174), and al-Bayhaqī (9/11–12).

11995 — ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb narrated:
The people asked Anas ibn Mālik about faḍīkh, and he said:
“We had no wine other than this faḍīkh of yours which you call faḍīkh.
I was standing in our house serving it to Abū Ṭalḥah, Abū Ayyūb, and a number of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when a man arrived and asked, ‘Has the news reached you?’
We replied:
‘No.’
He said:
‘Intoxicants have now been prohibited.’
Abū Ṭalḥah said:
‘Anas, pour out these jars.’
After the man’s report, they neither questioned him further nor returned to drinking it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4617) and Muslim (1980/4).

11996 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked about bitʿ, a drink made from honey.
He replied:
“Every drink that causes intoxication is forbidden.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (1595), al-Bukhārī (5585), and Muslim (2001).

11997 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every intoxicant is wine, and every intoxicant is forbidden. Whoever drinks wine in this world and dies while persistently addicted to it without repenting will not drink it in the Hereafter.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2003).
The Words of Allah: “They Ask You What They Should Spend. Say: What Is beyond Your Needs”
The word al-ʿafw here means whatever exceeds a person’s needs.

11998 — Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The upper hand is better than the lower hand. Begin with those for whom you are responsible. The best charity is that which is given while one remains self-sufficient. Whoever seeks to remain chaste and independent, Allah will preserve his chastity, and whoever seeks freedom from need, Allah will enrich him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1427) and Muslim (1034).

11999 — Abū Umāmah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Son of Adam, giving away what is surplus to your needs is better for you, while withholding it is worse for you. You will not be blamed for retaining what is sufficient for your needs. Begin with those for whom you are responsible, and the upper hand is better than the lower hand.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1036).
The words:
“Withholding it is worse for you”
mean that after a person has fulfilled the obligatory rights upon his wealth, such as zakāh and other duties, retaining the remainder may be worse for him in the Hereafter because it reduces his potential reward.
As for the worldly ruling, keeping it remains permissible, and something permissible is not intrinsically evil.
The words:
“You will not be blamed for retaining what is sufficient for your needs”
mean that if a person retains enough for himself after paying zakāh, he is not blameworthy.
Likewise, if his wealth becomes insufficient after fulfilling the obligation of zakāh, he may receive zakāh and charity from others to secure his sufficiency and meet his needs.

12000 — Anas narrated:
When a Jewish woman menstruated, the Jews would not eat with her and would not live together with her in the houses.
The Companions of the Prophet ﷺ asked about this, so Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“They ask you about menstruation. Say: It is harm, so keep away from women during menstruation…”
until the end of the verse.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do everything except sexual intercourse.”
When this reached the Jews, they said:
“This man does not wish to leave any of our practices without opposing us in it.”
Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr and ʿAbbād ibn Bishr came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, the Jews say such-and-such. Should we not, therefore, have intercourse with menstruating women?”
The face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ changed, until we thought he had become angry with them.
They left, and a gift of milk intended for the Prophet ﷺ met them on the way. He sent someone after them and gave them the milk to drink, so they understood that he was not angry with them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (302).
The words:
“They would not live together with them in the houses”
mean that they would not associate with them at home. Rather, they would send them to a separate room.
The first occurrence of al-maḥīḍ refers to menstrual blood, while the second refers to the period of menstruation.
The words:
“Except sexual relations”
mean intercourse.
The expression:
“He had found something against them”
means that he had become angry with them.
The expression:
“He had not found anything against them”
means that he was not angry with them.

12001 — Jābir narrated:
The Jews used to say:
“If a man has intercourse with his wife from behind, the child will be born cross-eyed.”
Allah then revealed:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation as you wish.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4528) and Muslim (1435/117). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
In Muslim’s wording:
“If a man approaches his wife from behind while intercourse occurs in the vagina, the child will be born cross-eyed.”

12002 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sābiṭ narrated:
I entered upon Ḥafṣah bint ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and said:
“I wish to ask you about something, but I feel embarrassed to ask.”
She replied:
“Do not be embarrassed, son of my brother.”
He asked about approaching women from behind.
She said:
Umm Salamah narrated to me that the Anṣār did not place women in a bent-forward position during intercourse.
The Jews used to claim:
“If a man places his wife in that position, his child will be born cross-eyed.”
When the Emigrants arrived in Madinah, they married women of the Anṣār and approached them in that position.
One woman refused to obey her husband and said:
“You will not do that until I go to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She entered upon Umm Salamah and mentioned the matter to her.
Umm Salamah said:
“Remain here until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrives.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came, the Anṣārī woman became too embarrassed to question him and left.
Umm Salamah then informed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He said:
“Call the Anṣārī woman.”
She was called, and he recited to her:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation as you wish,”
meaning intercourse must occur in the single lawful opening.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26601) and al-Tirmidhī (2979).
The verb jabbā–yujabbī refers to placing a woman in a position resembling prostration.
Another reason for the revelation of this verse is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās, as follows.

12003 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿUmar came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I am ruined.”
He asked:
“What has ruined you?”
ʿUmar replied:
“I changed the position of my saddle last night.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ initially gave him no answer.
Then the following verse was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation as you wish.”
That is, approach from the front or from behind, but avoid anal intercourse and intercourse during menstruation.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2980), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10973), Aḥmad (2703), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Mushkil al-Āthār (6127).
Another cause of revelation is also reported from Ibn ʿAbbās, as follows.

12004 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A group from Ḥimyar came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked him about several matters.
One man said:
“I have a strong desire for women. What is your ruling?”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (2/404).
These hadiths establish the prohibition of approaching women anywhere other than the vagina.
This ruling has been transmitted from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ through many routes.

12005 — Bahz ibn Ḥakīm ibn Muʿāwiyah ibn Ḥaydah al-Qushayrī narrated from his father, from his grandfather, who said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, regarding our wives, what may we approach and what must we avoid?”
He replied:
“Approach your place of cultivation however you wish.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2143) and Aḥmad (20030).
The words:
“However you wish”
mean from whichever bodily position, provided intercourse occurs in the vagina.
It has also been claimed that the words mean:
“Wherever you wish,”
including both the vagina and anus.
This interpretation was attributed to Ibn ʿUmar in the following report.

12006 — Nāfiʿ narrated:
When Ibn ʿUmar recited the Qur’an, he would not speak.
One day he recited the verse:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation as you wish.”
He asked:
“Do you know concerning what this verse was revealed?”
I replied:
“No.”
He said:
“It was revealed concerning approaching women from behind.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr (3/751).

12007 — Abū al-Naḍr said to Nāfiʿ:
“People have extensively attributed to you the statement that Ibn ʿUmar permitted women to be approached anally.”
Nāfiʿ replied:
“They have lied about me. I will tell you what actually happened.
One day Ibn ʿUmar was reviewing the Qur’an while I was with him. When he reached the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
‘Your wives are a place of cultivation for you,’
he asked, ‘Nāfiʿ, do you know the matter concerning which this verse was revealed?’
I replied:
‘No.’
He said:
‘We, the people of Quraysh, used to place women in a bent-forward position during intercourse. When we entered Madinah and married women of the Anṣār, we wished to do with them what we had previously done, but they disliked it and regarded it as a serious matter.
The women of the Anṣār had adopted the custom of the Jews, who approached women only while they lay on their sides.
Allah, the Exalted, therefore revealed:
“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation as you wish.”’”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭaḥāwī in Mushkil al-Āthār (15/424).

12008 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated the account of the pilgrimage of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He continued the report until mentioning the sermon of the Prophet ﷺ on the Day of ʿArafah, in which he spoke concerning women and said:
“Fear Allah concerning women. You have taken them under Allah’s protection, and marital relations with them became lawful to you through the word of Allah.
Your right over them is that they do not allow anyone whom you dislike to sit upon your furnishings. If they do that, discipline them in a manner that is not severe.
Their right over you is that you provide their food and clothing according to what is reasonable.”
The remainder of the hadith follows.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

12009 — Maʿqil ibn Yasār narrated:
I had a sister whose marriage proposals were brought to me.
A cousin of mine came to me, and I married her to him.
He later divorced her with a revocable divorce and then left her until her waiting period ended.
When proposals were made for her again, he returned and asked to marry her.
I said:
“By Allah, I will never marry her to him again.”
Maʿqil said:
The following verse was revealed concerning me:
“When you divorce women and they complete their waiting period, do not prevent them from marrying their former husbands.”
He continued:
“I expiated my oath and married her to him again.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4529) and Abū Dāwūd (2087). The wording cited here is that of Abū Dāwūd.
Al-Bukhārī said:
Ibrāhīm narrated from Yūnus, from al-Ḥasan. Abū Maʿmar narrated to us: ʿAbd al-Wārith narrated to us: Yūnus narrated to us from al-Ḥasan that the husband of Maʿqil ibn Yasār’s sister divorced her and left her until her waiting period ended. He then proposed marriage to her again, but Maʿqil refused. Thereupon the following verse was revealed:
“Do not prevent them from marrying their former husbands.”
Al-Bukhārī narrated the report through three routes, but he provided the wording of the hadith only through the third route. This third route is disconnected, because al-Ḥasan was not present during the event. However, the other routes explicitly state that al-Ḥasan heard it from Maʿqil ibn Yasār.
Al-Bukhārī’s statement, “Ibrāhīm said,” is connected in the Book of Marriage (5130). He cited it here to establish that al-Ḥasan heard from Maʿqil ibn Yasār.
Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī also narrated several reports in his Tafsīr stating that the verse was revealed concerning Maʿqil ibn Yasār and his sister.
Al-Tirmidhī (2981) narrated it through another route from al-Ḥasan, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār, with a similar wording. He said:
“This is an authentic hadith, and it has been narrated from al-Ḥasan through more than one route.”
Al-Tirmidhī derived a legal ruling from the hadith and said:
“This hadith proves that a marriage may not be contracted without a guardian. Maʿqil ibn Yasār’s sister had previously been married. Had the matter belonged solely to her rather than to her guardian, she could have married herself and would not have needed her guardian, Maʿqil ibn Yasār.
Allah addressed the guardians in the verse, saying:
‘Do not prevent them from marrying their former husbands.’
This verse therefore proves that the authority to contract the marriage belongs to the guardians, provided the women themselves consent.”
It has also been said that the verse was revealed concerning Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī.
He had a female cousin whose husband divorced her once. After her waiting period ended, the husband returned wishing to remarry her. Jābir refused and said:
“You divorced our cousin, and now you wish to marry her a second time?”
The woman wished to return to her former husband, so this verse was revealed. However, this report is not authentic.
The apparent meaning of the verse nevertheless supports the ruling mentioned.
A man may divorce his wife once or twice, after which her waiting period ends. He then wishes to marry her again, and she is willing to return to him, but her guardians prevent her out of pride or indignation.
Allah therefore addressed such guardians, saying:
“Do not prevent them,”
meaning: do not prevent them from returning to their former husbands through a new marriage contract.
The words:
“When they mutually agree between themselves according to what is reasonable”
mean that when the woman agrees to return to her former husband, her guardians are forbidden from harming her by obstructing the marriage and preventing her from marrying a former husband who wishes to remarry her.
The original meaning of ʿaḍl is restriction or constriction.

12010 — Zaynab bint Abī Salamah narrated:
I entered upon Umm Ḥabībah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, when her father, Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, died.
Umm Ḥabībah called for perfume containing yellow-colored khalūq or another fragrance. She applied some of it to a young girl and then touched her own cheeks with it.
She said:
“By Allah, I have no need for perfume, except that I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘It is not lawful for a woman who believes in Allah and the Last Day to mourn for a deceased person for more than three nights, except for her husband, for whom she must mourn for four months and ten days.’”
Zaynab continued:
I then entered upon Zaynab bint Jaḥsh, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, when her brother died.
She called for perfume and applied some of it. She then said:
“By Allah, I have no need for perfume, except that I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘It is not lawful for a woman who believes in Allah and the Last Day to mourn for a deceased person for more than three nights, except for her husband, for whom she must mourn for four months and ten days.’”
Zaynab said:
I heard my mother, Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, say:
A woman came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, my daughter’s husband has died, and she is suffering pain in her eyes. May she apply kohl to them?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“No.”
She asked him two or three times, and each time he answered:
“No.”
He then said:
“It is only four months and ten days. During the pre-Islamic period, one of you would throw a piece of dung at the completion of an entire year.”
Ḥumayd ibn Nāfiʿ said:
I asked Zaynab:
“What does it mean that she would throw a piece of dung at the completion of a year?”
Zaynab replied:
When a woman’s husband died, she would enter a small, wretched enclosure, wear her worst clothing, and avoid perfume and all forms of grooming until a full year had passed.
She would then be brought an animal—a donkey, sheep, or bird—and rub herself against it. Seldom would she rub herself against an animal without it dying.
She would then emerge and be given a piece of dung, which she would throw away. After that she could resume using whatever perfume or other adornment she wished.
Mālik said:
The word ḥifsh means a wretched or inferior dwelling.
The expression taftaḍḍu bihi means that she rubbed the animal against her skin as a ritual intended to remove the effects of mourning.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Divorce (1266–1270), al-Bukhārī (5334–5336), and Muslim (1486–1488).

12011 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd was asked concerning a man who married a woman and then died before consummating the marriage and before specifying her bridal gift.
He said:
“She is entitled to the complete bridal gift, she must observe the waiting period, and she is entitled to inheritance.”
Maʿqil ibn Sinān said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ issue the same judgment concerning Barwaʿ bint Wāshiq.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2114), al-Nasāʾī (3356), and Ibn Mājah (1891), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4098).
The words:
“Four months and ten days”
mean that the waiting period of a woman whose husband dies, provided she is not pregnant, is four months and ten days.
A female slave is excluded from this ruling according to the majority of scholars. Her waiting period is half that of a free woman: two months and five nights.
Some scholars disagreed and held that there is no difference between a free woman and a female slave regarding the waiting period.
They argued that the purpose of the waiting period is to confirm that the womb is free of pregnancy, and free women and female slaves are equal in that respect.
Among the rulings governing a widow’s mourning period are the following:
1. She should remain in the house in which she was living when her husband died. An exception applies if the house was rented or belonged to someone other than the husband, in which case she may move to her family’s home.
2. She should not leave the house except for a need or necessity, such as attending a hospital appointment or fulfilling employment duties. Once her need is completed, she should return home. The ruling applies equally during the day and night.
3. She must avoid perfume and similar fragrances. However, after becoming pure from menstruation, there is no harm in using a small amount of incense or another fragrance.
There is also no harm in her offering perfume to members of her household or guests, provided she does not personally use it with them.
4. She must avoid jewelry made of gold, silver, diamonds, or other materials, whether necklaces, bracelets, rings, or anything similar.
5. She must avoid using henna, kohl, and similar cosmetics, as well as every other form of adornment.
6. She must avoid beautiful or decorative clothing and wear ordinary clothing instead.
As for a pregnant woman whose husband dies, according to the strongest scholarly opinion, her waiting period ends when she gives birth.

12012 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah narrated:
Subayʿah al-Aslamiyyah gave birth only a few nights after her husband died.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to her:
“Your waiting period has ended, so marry whomever you wish.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (58) and al-Bukhārī (5320).

12013 — ʿAlī narrated that on the Day of the Trench, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“They prevented us from performing the middle prayer until the sun had set. May Allah fill their graves and their houses—or their insides—with fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4533) and Muslim (627/202). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12014 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The polytheists prevented the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from performing the ʿAṣr prayer until the sun became red or yellow.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“They distracted us from the middle prayer, the ʿAṣr prayer. May Allah fill their insides and their graves with fire.”
Or he said:
“May Allah stuff their insides and their graves with fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (628).

12015 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that on the Day of the Trench, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“May Allah fill their houses and their graves with fire, just as they distracted us from the middle prayer until the sun had set.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (390).

12016 — Kuhayl ibn Ḥarmalah al-Numayrī narrated from Abū Hurayrah:
Abū Hurayrah came and stayed with Abū Kulthūm al-Dawsī. They began discussing which prayer was the middle prayer.
Abū Hurayrah said:
“We differed concerning it just as you have differed, while we were in the courtyard of the house of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Among us was the righteous man Abū Hāshim ibn ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah ibn ʿAbd Shams.
He said:
‘I will find out the answer for you.’
He went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He was sufficiently close and confident with him to ask him directly. He sought permission, entered, and then came back out to us and informed us that it was the ʿAṣr prayer.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Āḥād wa-al-Mathānī (557) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (391).

12017 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
The following verse was revealed:
“Guard strictly the prayers and the ʿAṣr prayer.”
We recited it for as long as Allah willed.
Allah then abrogated it, and the following wording was revealed:
“Guard strictly the prayers and the middle prayer.”
A man who was sitting beside one of his relatives said:
“Then it must be the ʿAṣr prayer.”
Al-Barāʾ replied:
“I have told you how it was revealed and how Allah abrogated it. Allah knows best.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (630).
This means that the wording:
“And the ʿAṣr prayer”
was recited as part of the Qur’an for a period of time. Allah, the Exalted, then abrogated that wording and revealed:
“Guard strictly the prayers and the middle prayer.”
This is also indicated by the reports from ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah, for they heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite that wording and did not know that it had later been abrogated.

12018 — Abū Yūnus, the freedman of ʿĀʾishah, narrated:
ʿĀʾishah instructed me to write a copy of the Qur’an for her.
She said:
“When you reach this verse, notify me:
‘Guard strictly the prayers and the middle prayer.’”
When I reached it, I informed her.
She dictated to me:
“Guard strictly the prayers, the middle prayer, and the ʿAṣr prayer, and stand before Allah in devout obedience.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“I heard it from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Congregational Prayer (26) and Muslim (629).
The words:
“Stand before Allah in devout obedience”
mean: stand obediently and submissively before Him.

12019 — Zayd ibn Arqam narrated:
We used to speak during prayer. A person would speak to his companion about something he needed until the following verse was revealed:
“Guard strictly the prayers, especially the middle prayer, and stand before Allah in devout obedience.”
We were then commanded to remain silent and were prohibited from speaking during prayer.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4534) and Muslim (539/35).
The meaning of qunūt is worship and obedience.

12020 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Which prayer is best?”
He replied:
“The prayer with the longest qunūt.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (756/165).
The words:
“The longest qunūt”
mean prolonging the prayer—its standing, bowing, and prostration—while maintaining humility and refraining from looking around.

12021 — Nāfiʿ narrated:
Whenever Ibn ʿUmar was asked about the prayer performed during danger, he would describe the manner of the fear prayer and then say:
“If the danger is even more severe than that, they should pray on foot while standing upon their feet, or while riding, whether facing the qiblah or not.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of the Fear Prayer (3), al-Bukhārī (4535), and Muslim (839/306).
The word:
“On foot”
means that they should pray while walking. It is the plural of rājil, just as qiyām is the plural form associated with qāʾim.
The words:
“Or while riding”
mean that they should pray while mounted. Rukbān is the plural of rākib.

12022 — Ibn al-Zubayr said:
I asked ʿUthmān:
“This verse in Sūrat al-Baqarah:
‘Those among you who die and leave wives behind…’
continuing until:
‘Without expulsion,’
was abrogated by the other verse. Why, then, do you still write it?”
He replied:
“Leave it, son of my brother. I will not alter anything from its established place.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4536).
Similar interpretations were reported from Mujāhid, al-Ḥasan, ʿIkrimah, Qatādah, al-Ḍaḥḥāk, al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas, and Muqātil ibn Ḥayyān.
They held that it was abrogated by the words:
“Four months and ten days.”
Ibn Abī Ḥātim mentioned this in his Tafsīr (2/452).
Refer to the Book of Marriage for further discussion.

12023 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿah narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb departed for Syria.
When he reached Sargh, news reached him that an epidemic had broken out in Syria.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf informed him that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had said:
“If you hear that an epidemic has occurred in a land, do not enter it. If it occurs in a land while you are there, do not leave it while fleeing from it.”
ʿUmar therefore returned from Sargh.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of General Matters (24), al-Bukhārī (5730), and Muslim (2219/100).

12024 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
We, the Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ, used to say that the number of those who fought at Badr was the same as the number of Ṭālūt’s companions who crossed the river with him.
Only believers crossed with him: slightly more than three hundred and ten men.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3958).

12025 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every prophet was given signs through which people believed in him. What I was given was revelation that Allah revealed to me. I therefore hope to have the greatest number of followers on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4981) and Muslim (152). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12026 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been favored above the prophets in six matters: I was given concise and comprehensive speech; I was granted victory through awe; the spoils of war were made lawful for me; the earth was made for me a means of purification and a place of prayer; I was sent to all creation; and the line of prophets was sealed with me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (523).

12027 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood among us and stated four matters:
“Allah does not sleep, and it is not befitting for Him to sleep. He raises the scale and lowers it. The deeds of the day are raised to Him during the night, and the deeds of the night are raised to Him during the day.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (179/295).

12028 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“There is no compulsion in religion”:
A woman from the Anṣār whose children rarely survived would vow:
“If a child of mine survives, I will make him Jewish.”
When Banū al-Naḍīr were expelled, some of the sons of the Anṣār were among them.
The Anṣār said:
“O Messenger of Allah, they are our sons.”
Allah then revealed:
“There is no compulsion in religion.”
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said:
“Whoever wished joined Banū al-Naḍīr, and whoever wished entered Islam.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (140).
Abū Dāwūd (2682) also narrated it from al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī al-Ḥulwānī through a similar chain, with the wording:
“A woman whose children did not survive would make a vow that if one of her children lived, she would make him Jewish.”
He did not explicitly attribute it to the Prophet ﷺ, but it carries the ruling of a prophetic report.
Regarding the word miqlāt, Abū Dāwūd said:
“It refers to a woman whose children do not survive.”

12029 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“We would have more right to doubt than Abraham, when he said:
‘My Lord, show me how You give life to the dead.’ Allah said, ‘Do you not believe?’ He replied, ‘Yes, but so that my heart may become reassured.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4537) and Muslim (151).
The hadith does not contain an admission that either the Prophet ﷺ or Abraham doubted.
Rather, it denies doubt from both of them.
Its meaning is:
“If I do not doubt Allah’s power to give life to the dead, Abraham is even more deserving of being free from doubt.”
The Prophet ﷺ said this out of humility and self-effacement.
Al-Khaṭṭābī explained this interpretation.
It is also said that Abraham, peace be upon him, did not ask whether resurrection itself was possible, because he knew with certainty that Allah possessed the power to do it.
Rather, he asked to see the manner in which resurrection occurs. This was a request to increase certain knowledge through direct observation.
The words:
“Cut them and draw them close to yourself”
mean: cut and slaughter them.
It is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās that he said concerning the word:
“Faṣurhunna”
that it is a Nabataean expression meaning:
“Cut them into pieces.”
The words:
“Then place a portion of them upon every mountain”
mean that after slaughtering the birds, Abraham was to mix their parts together and place a portion of that mixture upon each mountain.
The verse does not specify the number of mountains.
Rather, Abraham was left to distribute the portions of the birds among a number of mountains.
The words:
“They will come to you swiftly”
mean that every part would return to its corresponding body, after which the birds would come to Abraham with their bodies completely restored.
It has also been said that Abraham retained the head of each bird.
The remaining parts of each bird came to him and joined their respective heads until the birds returned to their original condition.
When Abraham witnessed this, he knew through direct observation that Allah is Almighty and All-Wise.

12030 — Sulaymān ibn Yasār narrated:
After the people dispersed from Abū Hurayrah, Nātil, a man from Syria, said to him:
“Elder, narrate to us a hadith that you heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Abū Hurayrah replied:
“Yes. I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘The first person whose case will be judged on the Day of Resurrection will be a man who was martyred. He will be brought forward, and Allah will remind him of His blessings, which he will acknowledge.
Allah will ask, “What did you do with them?”
He will reply, “I fought for Your sake until I was martyred.”
Allah will say, “You have lied. You fought only so that people would say, ‘He is courageous,’ and that was said.” He will then be commanded to be dragged upon his face until he is thrown into the Fire.
Then a man who acquired knowledge, taught it, and recited the Qur’an will be brought forward. Allah will remind him of His blessings, which he will acknowledge.
Allah will ask, “What did you do with them?”
He will reply, “I acquired knowledge, taught it, and recited the Qur’an for Your sake.”
Allah will say, “You have lied. You acquired knowledge only so that people would say, ‘He is a scholar,’ and you recited the Qur’an so that they would say, ‘He is a reciter,’ and that was said.” He will then be commanded to be dragged upon his face until he is thrown into the Fire.
Then a man upon whom Allah had bestowed abundance and every type of wealth will be brought forward. Allah will remind him of His blessings, which he will acknowledge.
Allah will ask, “What did you do with them?”
He will reply, “I did not leave any cause in which You love wealth to be spent except that I spent in it for Your sake.”
Allah will say, “You have lied. You did that only so that people would say, ‘He is generous,’ and that was said.” He will then be commanded to be dragged upon his face and thrown into the Fire.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1905/152).
The expression “Nātil, the Syrian” refers to Nātil ibn Qays al-Khuzāʿī, who was a leader among his people.

12031 — Maḥmūd ibn Labīd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ came out and said:
“O people, beware of hidden shirk.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is hidden shirk?”
He replied:
“A man stands to pray and strives to beautify his prayer because he notices that people are watching him. That is hidden shirk.”
In another narration, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“What I fear most for you is minor shirk.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is minor shirk?”
He replied:
“Showing off. On the Day of Resurrection, when people are recompensed for their deeds, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say to them: ‘Go to those before whom you used to show off in the world and see whether you can find any reward with them.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Khuzaymah (937), Ibn Abī Shaybah (8489), Aḥmad (23631), and al-Baghawī in Sharḥ al-Sunnah (4135).

12032 — ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr narrated:
One day, ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with him, asked the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ:
“Concerning what do you think this verse was revealed:
‘Would any of you wish to have a garden…’?”
They replied:
“Allah knows best.”
ʿUmar became displeased and said:
“Say either that you know or that you do not know.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Commander of the Believers, I have an understanding of it.”
ʿUmar replied:
“Son of my brother, speak and do not belittle yourself.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“It is a parable for a deed.”
ʿUmar asked:
“What kind of deed?”
Ibn ʿAbbās repeated:
“For a deed.”
ʿUmar then said:
“It is the example of a wealthy man who acts in obedience to Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Allah then allows Satan to overcome him, so he commits acts of disobedience until he destroys all his deeds.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4583).
The word:
“Garden”
means an orchard.
The word:
“Whirlwind”
means a powerful wind.
The words:
“Containing fire”
mean a wind containing intensely scorching heat.
The words:
“And it burned”
refer to the destruction of the fruits that the man needed in his old age.
This is a parable of an elderly man who destroys his deeds through shirk and religious innovation near the end of his life, precisely when he most needs faith in Allah and righteous deeds to lead him to Paradise.
The words:
“So that you may reflect”
mean that you should avoid returning to shirk, disbelief, and misguidance, for toward the end of life you are in greater need than ever of faith and righteous deeds.

12033 — Al-Barāʾ said concerning the words of Allah:
“Do not deliberately select what is inferior from it to give in charity”:
This verse was revealed concerning us, the Anṣār.
We owned date palms, and each man would bring dates according to the abundance or scarcity of his crop. A man would bring one or two clusters of dates and hang them in the mosque.
The People of the Bench had no food. When one of them became hungry, he would come to a cluster and strike it with his staff so that unripe and ripe dates would fall, and he would eat them.
Some people who had little desire for goodness would bring clusters containing poor, dried, or defective dates, sometimes even broken clusters, and hang them there.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“O you who believe, spend from the good things you have earned and from what We have produced for you from the earth. Do not deliberately select what is inferior from it to give away, when you yourselves would not accept it except with closed eyes.”
They said:
“Were one of you given something similar to what he had donated, he would not accept it except by overlooking its defects or out of embarrassment.”
Al-Barāʾ said:
“After that, each one of us would bring the best of what he possessed.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2987), Ibn Mājah (1822), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (2803), and al-Ḥākim (2/285).
The words:
“Do not deliberately select”
mean: do not intentionally choose.
Concerning the words:
“When you yourselves would not accept it except with closed eyes,”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“If someone owed you a right and brought you something inferior to what was due, you would not accept it at the value of something good without reducing its valuation.
This is the meaning of:
‘Except that you overlook its defects.’
How, then, can you be pleased to offer Allah what you would not accept for yourselves, when His right upon you is that you offer from the finest and most valuable of your wealth?”
Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr through an authentic chain.

12034 — Jābir narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ prescribed one ṣāʿ of dates as Zakāt al-Fiṭr.
A man brought poor-quality dates, so the Prophet ﷺ said to ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah:
“Do not assess these dates.”
The following Qur’anic verse was then revealed:
“O you who believe, spend from the good things you have earned and from what We have produced for you from the earth, and do not deliberately select what is inferior from it to give away.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/283–284) and al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 81).

12035 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to buy inexpensive food and give it in charity.
Allah then revealed to His Prophet:
“O you who believe, spend from the good things you have earned.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (2790).

12036 — Al-Miqdām ibn Maʿdīkarib narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No one has ever eaten food better than that earned through the work of his own hands. The Prophet of Allah, David, peace be upon him, used to eat from the work of his own hands.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2072).

12037 — Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“Spend and do not count everything you give, lest Allah count His provision against you. Do not hoard, lest Allah withhold from you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2591) and Muslim (1029). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12038 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: ‘Spend, and I will spend upon you.’”
He also said:
“The hand of Allah is full and is not diminished by continuous spending, generously bestowing by night and day.”
He further said:
“Have you considered all that He has spent since creating the heavens and the earth? It has not diminished what is in His hand. His Throne was upon the water, and in His hand is the scale, which He lowers and raises.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4684) and Muslim (993). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12039 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are seven whom Allah will shade beneath His shade on a Day when there will be no shade except His.”
Among them he mentioned:
“A man who gives charity so secretly that his left hand does not know what his right hand has spent.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (660) and Muslim (1031). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12040 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Muslims disliked giving small amounts of charity to their polytheist relatives.
They asked about this and were granted permission, so the following verse was revealed:
“You are not responsible for their guidance, but Allah guides whomever He wills. Whatever good you spend is for your own benefit, and you should spend only seeking the face of Allah. Whatever good you spend will be repaid to you in full, and you will not be wronged.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10986), Ibn al-Mundhir (1), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (2852).
Ibn Jarīr also narrated it in his Tafsīr through another route from Sufyān.
That narration states:
Some of the Anṣār had relatives and family connections among Banū Qurayẓah and Banū al-Naḍīr. They refrained from giving them charity because they wanted them to accept Islam.
This verse was then revealed.

12041 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The truly poor person is not the one who can be turned away with one or two dates, or one or two morsels of food. The truly poor person is the one who remains dignified and refrains from asking.”
He then said:
“Recite, if you wish, the words of Allah: ‘They do not persistently ask people.’”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4539) and Muslim (1039). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The word:
“Persistently”
means that they do not insist upon asking.
Whoever asks despite already possessing enough to avoid asking has persisted improperly in begging. This is burdensome and blameworthy solicitation.

12042 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb narrated from his father, from his grandfather, that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever begs while possessing forty dirhams has asked importunately, and it is like consuming the dust of begging.”
The word mentioned here means dust or sand.
Good:
Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (2375).

12043 — Qabīṣah ibn Mukhāriq al-Hilālī narrated:
I undertook a financial obligation to reconcile between people, so I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to ask him for assistance in paying it.
He said:
“Remain here until charity comes to us, and we will order that something be given to you from it.”
He then said:
“Qabīṣah, begging is not lawful except for one of three people:
A man who has undertaken a financial obligation to reconcile between people. Begging is lawful for him until he obtains enough to fulfill it, after which he must stop.
A man whose wealth has been destroyed by a calamity. Begging is lawful for him until he acquires enough to maintain a reasonable standard of living—or he said, enough to meet his needs.
A man afflicted by poverty, until three intelligent men from his people testify, ‘So-and-so has truly been afflicted by poverty.’ Begging is lawful for him until he acquires enough to maintain a reasonable standard of living—or he said, enough to meet his needs.
Any begging other than these cases, Qabīṣah, is unlawful wealth which its recipient consumes unlawfully.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (1044).

12044 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When the verses concerning usury in Sūrat al-Baqarah were revealed, the Prophet ﷺ went out to the mosque and recited them to the people.
He then prohibited trade in intoxicants.
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (459) and Muslim (1580/70). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The Words of Allah: “Allah Has Permitted Trade and Forbidden Usury”

12045 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ cursed the one who consumes usury, the one who pays it, the one who records it, and its two witnesses.
He said:
“They are all equal.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (1598).

12046 — Abū Juḥayfah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ prohibited the price received for selling a dog and the price received for blood.
He also prohibited tattooing and requesting tattoos, consuming usury and paying it, and he cursed image-makers.
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2086).

12047 — Samurah ibn Jundub reported:
Whenever the Prophet ﷺ completed a prayer, he would turn toward us and ask:
“Did any of you see a dream last night?”
If someone had seen one, he would relate it, and the Prophet ﷺ would say:
“Whatever Allah wills.”
One day he asked us:
“Did any of you see a dream?”
We replied:
“No.”
He said:
“But I saw a dream last night…”
He then related his dream, in which he said:
“We proceeded until we came to a river of blood. A man was standing in the middle of the river, while another man stood on its bank with stones before him. The man in the river approached, and whenever he tried to come out, the man on the bank threw a stone into his mouth and sent him back to where he had been. Every time he came forward intending to leave, a stone was thrown into his mouth and he returned to his former place.”
Jibrīl said:
“The man you saw in the river is one who consumes usury.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1386) and Muslim (2275). This is the wording of al-Bukhārī.

12048 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one accumulates a great amount of wealth through usury except that his affair ultimately ends in scarcity.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2279), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (10/275), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/37).
The phrase:
“It ends in scarcity”
means that although usury may appear to increase wealth immediately, it ultimately leads to reduction and loss.
The words:
“And He causes charities to grow”
mean that Allah increases and develops their reward.

12049 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever gives in charity the equivalent of a single date from lawful earnings—and Allah accepts only what is pure—Allah accepts it with His right hand and then nurtures it for its giver just as one of you raises his young foal, until it becomes like a mountain.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1410) and Muslim (1014/64).
The word falū, with either a still or rounded pronunciation of the letter lām, means a young foal.
It is called this because it has been separated and weaned from its mother.
The word qalūṣ means a young female camel and is not used for a male.

12050 — Ḥudhayfah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The angels received the soul of a man from among those who lived before you and asked him, ‘Did you perform any good deed?’
He replied, ‘No.’
They said, ‘Try to remember.’
He said, ‘I used to lend money to people and instruct my servants to grant additional time to those in hardship and to overlook the faults of those able to pay.’
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, ‘Overlook his faults.’”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2077) and Muslim (1560). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12051 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated:
My father and I went out seeking knowledge among this community of the Anṣār before its scholars passed away.
The first person we met was Abū al-Yusr, a Companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. A servant accompanied him carrying a bundle of documents.
Abū al-Yusr wore a cloak and a Maʿāfirī garment, and his servant wore similar clothing.
My father said to him:
“Uncle, I see traces of anger upon your face.”
He replied:
“Yes. So-and-so, the son of so-and-so from the tribe of Judhām, owed me money. I went to his family, greeted them, and asked, ‘Is he here?’
They replied:
‘No.’
A young son of his came out to me, so I asked:
‘Where is your father?’
He replied:
‘He heard your voice and entered my mother’s enclosed room.’
I said:
‘Come out to me, for I know where you are.’
He came out, and I asked:
‘What caused you to hide from me?’
He replied:
‘By Allah, I will speak truthfully to you and will not lie. I feared that if I spoke to you I might lie, and if I made you a promise I might break it. You were a Companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and by Allah, I am in hardship.’
I asked:
‘By Allah?’
He replied:
‘By Allah.’
I asked again:
‘By Allah?’
He replied:
‘By Allah.’
I asked a third time:
‘By Allah?’
He replied:
‘By Allah.’
Abū al-Yusr then brought out the debt document and erased it with his own hand.
He said:
‘If you later find the means to repay me, then repay me. Otherwise, you are released from it.’
Then he said:
‘These two eyes of mine witnessed it’—placing his fingers upon his eyes—‘these two ears of mine heard it, and this heart of mine retained it’—pointing toward his heart—‘from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who said:
“Whoever grants additional time to one in hardship or cancels his debt, Allah will shade him beneath His shade.”’”
The remainder of the hadith follows.
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (3006).

12052 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Qatādah narrated:
Abū Qatādah searched for a debtor of his, but the man hid from him.
When he eventually found him, the man said:
“I am in financial hardship.”
Abū Qatādah asked:
“By Allah?”
He replied:
“By Allah.”
Abū Qatādah said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘Whoever wishes Allah to save him from the hardships of the Day of Resurrection should grant relief to an insolvent debtor or cancel his debt.’”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (1563).

12053 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever grants additional time to an insolvent debtor receives the reward of charity equal to the debt for every day.”
I later heard him say:
“Whoever grants additional time to an insolvent debtor receives the reward of charity equal to twice the debt for every day.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, I first heard you say, ‘Whoever grants additional time to an insolvent debtor receives the reward of charity equal to the debt for every day.’ Then I heard you say, ‘He receives the reward of charity equal to twice the debt for every day.’”
He replied:
“Before the debt becomes due, he receives the reward of charity equal to the debt for every day. Once the debt becomes due and he grants further time, he receives the reward of charity equal to twice the debt for every day.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Aḥmad (23046), authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/29), and narrated by al-Bayhaqī (5/357).

12054 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The last portion of the Qur’an to be revealed was:
“Fear a Day when you will be returned to Allah.”
Good:
Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10/39–40).

12055 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
“The last verse revealed to the Prophet ﷺ was the verse concerning usury.”
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4544).
The expression:
“The verse concerning usury”
means the collection of verses concerning usury beginning with:
“Those who consume usury will not rise except as one rises…”
and ending with:
“Fear a Day when you will be returned to Allah. Then every soul will be repaid in full for what it earned, and they will not be wronged.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:275–281]
Under this interpretation, Ibn ʿAbbās’s statement is reconciled with the other reports, which should likewise be understood in this manner.

12056 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
ʿUmar delivered a sermon from the pulpit of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Among his statements was:
“There are three matters concerning which I wished that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not left us until he had given us a decisive instruction: the inheritance of the grandfather, the inheritance of one who leaves neither parents nor children, and certain categories of usury.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5588) and Muslim (3032).

12057 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī recited:
“O you who believe, when you contract a debt for a fixed term…”
until he reached the words:
“But if one of you entrusts another…”
He then said:
“This abrogated what preceded it.”
Good:
Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2365), Ibn al-Mundhir in his Tafsīr (74), and al-Bayhaqī (10/145).

12058 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ purchased food from a Jewish man on deferred payment and gave him his armor as a pledge.
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Sales (2096) and Muslim in the Book of Sharecropping and Agricultural Transactions (1603), both through Abū Muʿāwiyah, from al-Aʿmash, from Ibrāhīm, from al-Aswad, from ʿĀʾishah.
The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim narrated a similar wording.

12059 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the following verse was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“To Allah belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it, Allah will call you to account for it. He forgives whomever He wills and punishes whomever He wills, and Allah has power over all things,”
it weighed heavily upon the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
They came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, knelt down upon their knees, and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we have been charged with deeds within our ability: prayer, fasting, striving, and charity. Now this verse has been revealed to you, and we are unable to bear it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you wish to say as the People of the two Scriptures before you said, ‘We hear and disobey’? Rather, say: ‘We hear and obey. Grant us Your forgiveness, our Lord, and to You is the final return.’”
They said:
“We hear and obey. Grant us Your forgiveness, our Lord, and to You is the final return.”
When the people recited these words and their tongues became accustomed and submissive to them, Allah revealed immediately afterward:
“The Messenger believes in what has been revealed to him from his Lord, as do the believers. All of them believe in Allah, His angels, His Books, and His messengers. We make no distinction between any of His messengers. They say, ‘We hear and obey. Grant us Your forgiveness, our Lord, and to You is the final return.’”
When they submitted in this manner, Allah, the Exalted, abrogated the former ruling and revealed:
“Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. It will receive the good it has earned and bear the evil it has acquired. Our Lord, do not hold us accountable if we forget or make a mistake.”
Allah said:
“Yes.”
They said:
“Our Lord, do not place upon us a burden like that which You placed upon those before us.”
Allah said:
“Yes.”
They said:
“Our Lord, do not burden us with what we have no strength to bear.”
Allah said:
“Yes.”
They said:
“Pardon us, forgive us, and have mercy upon us. You are our Protector, so grant us victory over the disbelieving people.”
Allah said:
“Yes.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (125).

12060 — A man from among the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ—whom the narrator believed to be Ibn ʿUmar—said concerning the words of Allah:
“Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it, Allah will call you to account for it”:
“The verse that follows it abrogated it.”
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4546).
The word iṣr means a binding covenant, as Ibn ʿAbbās explained.

12061 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the verse:
“Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it, Allah will call you to account for it”
was revealed, something entered the hearts of the Companions more intensely than anything else had entered them.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Say: ‘We hear, we obey, and we submit.’”
Allah then placed firm faith within their hearts and revealed:
“Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. It will receive the good it has earned and bear the evil it has acquired. Our Lord, do not hold us accountable if we forget or make a mistake.”
Allah said:
“I have done so.”
They said:
“Our Lord, do not place upon us a burden like that which You placed upon those before us.”
Allah said:
“I have done so.”
They said:
“Our Lord, do not burden us with what we have no strength to bear. Pardon us, forgive us, and have mercy upon us. You are our Protector.”
Allah said:
“I have done so.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (126).

12062 — Saʿīd ibn Marjānah narrated:
While he was sitting with ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar, Ibn ʿUmar recited the verse:
“To Allah belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it…”
He then said:
“By Allah, if Allah holds us accountable according to this, we will surely perish.”
Ibn ʿUmar then wept until his sobbing could be heard.
Ibn Marjānah said:
I stood and went to Ibn ʿAbbās. I told him what Ibn ʿUmar had recited and how he had reacted upon reciting it.
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“May Allah forgive Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. By my life, when this verse was revealed, the Muslims experienced the same distress that ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar experienced.”
Allah then revealed:
“Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity,”
until the end of the sūrah.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“These involuntary whisperings were among the things the Muslims had no ability to control. The final ruling became that each soul receives the reward of what it deliberately earns and bears the burden of what it deliberately acquires through speech and action.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī (5/132).

12063 — Mujāhid narrated:
I entered upon Ibn ʿAbbās and said:
“Abū ʿAbbās, I was with Ibn ʿUmar when he recited a verse and began to weep.”
He asked:
“Which verse?”
I replied:
