“Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it, Allah will call you to account for it.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“When this verse was revealed, it caused the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intense grief and great distress.
They said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, we are ruined. We may be held accountable for what we speak and what we do, but our hearts are not under our control.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to them:
‘Say: We hear and obey.’
They said, ‘We hear and obey.’
The following verse then abrogated it:
‘The Messenger believes in what has been revealed to him from his Lord, as do the believers…’
continuing until:
‘Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. It will receive the good it has earned and bear the evil it has acquired.’
They were therefore pardoned for involuntary thoughts of the soul and were held accountable for their deliberate deeds.”
Good:
Narrated by Aḥmad (3070) and Ibn Jarīr (5/133), both from the narration of ʿAbd al-Razzāq. It is also found in his Tafsīr (1/113–114).
It has been transmitted through numerous reports from the Companions, the Successors, and those who followed them that the words of Allah:
“Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it”
were abrogated by the verse that follows.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has pardoned the inner thoughts of the soul so long as a person neither speaks of them nor acts upon them, as established in authentic hadiths.

12064 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has pardoned my nation for what their souls whisper to them, so long as they do not act upon it or speak of it.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5269) and Muslim (127/333).
The words of Allah:
“Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity”
mean that Allah does not impose upon a person anything beyond his ability.
This verse abrogates the earlier words:
“Whether you disclose what is within yourselves or conceal it, Allah will call you to account for it,”
because a servant does not control the involuntary whisperings of his soul, so how could he be punished for them?
The words:
“You are our Protector”
mean:
“You are our Guardian and Supporter, and upon You we place our trust.”
Regarding the words:
“Grant us victory over the disbelieving people,”
Muʿādh would say:
“Āmīn,”
whenever he completed the recitation of this sūrah.

12065 — The Mother of the Believers, ʿĀʾishah, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited:
“He is the One Who revealed to you the Book. In it are clear and decisive verses which are the foundation of the Book, while others are ambiguous. As for those in whose hearts is deviation, they follow what is ambiguous from it, seeking tribulation and seeking its interpretation. None knows its true interpretation except Allah. Those firmly grounded in knowledge say, ‘We believe in it; all of it is from our Lord.’ None takes heed except people of understanding.”
ʿĀʾishah said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“When you see those who pursue its ambiguous passages, they are the ones whom Allah has named, so beware of them.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4547) and Muslim (2665).

12066 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān al-Kilābī narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“There is no heart except that it lies between two fingers of the Lord of all worlds. If He wills to keep it upright, He keeps it upright, and if He wills to cause it to deviate, He causes it to deviate.”
He used to supplicate:
“O Turner of hearts, keep our hearts firm upon Your religion.”
He also said:
“The scale is in the hand of the Most Merciful, Mighty and Majestic. He lowers it and raises it.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Aḥmad (17630), Ibn Mājah (199), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (943), and al-Ḥākim (1/525).

12067 — Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The heart of the son of Adam changes more rapidly than a cooking pot when it reaches a full boil.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (226) and al-Ḥākim (2/289). The wording cited here is that of al-Ḥākim.

12068 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
A man sought permission to enter upon the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allow him to enter. What an evil son of his clan he is,”
or:
“What an evil brother of his clan he is.”
When the man entered, the Prophet ﷺ spoke gently to him.
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, you said what you said about him, yet you then spoke gently to him?”
He replied:
“ʿĀʾishah, the worst people in rank before Allah are those whom people abandon or avoid out of fear of their obscene behavior.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6131) and Muslim (2591).

12069 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There is no newborn except that Satan touches him at the moment of birth, causing him to cry out because of Satan’s touch, except Mary and her son.”
Abū Hurayrah would then say:
“Recite, if you wish:
‘I seek Your protection for her and her descendants from Satan, the accursed.’”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4548) and Muslim (2366).
The words:
“Allah knew best what she had delivered, and the male is not like the female”
form a parenthetical statement from Allah.
Allah declares that He does not need to be informed by her or anyone else. He knew that the child would be female before her mother knew and before she gave birth.
Allah knew that this female child would be better than the male child whom the wife of ʿImrān had hoped for because a male would ordinarily be stronger, more capable, and more suited to serving Bayt al-Maqdis.
She had vowed that the child in her womb would be dedicated to that service.
This newborn girl was better than the male child she had hoped for because she would become the mother of a great prophet and one of the foremost women of Paradise.
Thus, the statement is from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and is not an expression of regret from the wife of ʿImrān over giving birth to a girl.

12070 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Only three spoke while still in the cradle: Jesus, a child who lived during the time of Jurayj, and another child.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3436) and Muslim (2550).

12071 — Ḥudhayfah narrated:
Al-ʿĀqib and al-Sayyid, the two leaders of Najrān, came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intending to invoke Allah’s curse against him.
One of them said to the other:
“Do not do it. By Allah, if he is truly a prophet and we invoke Allah’s curse against him, neither we nor our descendants after us will prosper.”
They said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“We will give you what you requested from us. Send with us a trustworthy man, and do not send anyone except one who is truly trustworthy.”
He said:
“I will certainly send with you a man who is truly and completely trustworthy.”
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ eagerly hoped to be chosen.
He said:
“Stand, Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ.”
When he stood, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“This is the trustworthy man of this nation.”
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4380).

12072 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
When the verse:
“Say: Come, let us call our sons and your sons…”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called ʿAlī, Fāṭimah, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn and said:
“O Allah, these are my family.”
Authentic:
Narrated by Muslim (2404/32).

12073 — Jābir narrated:
The delegation of Najrān came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“What do you say concerning Jesus, son of Mary?”
He replied:
“He is the Spirit of Allah, His word, the servant of Allah, and His messenger.”
They asked:
“Will you engage in mutual invocation with us that he is not as you have described?”
He asked:
“Is that what you prefer?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then whenever you wish.”
The Prophet ﷺ came and gathered his two grandsons, al-Ḥasan and al-Ḥusayn.
Their leader said:
“Do not invoke Allah’s curse against this man. By Allah, if you do, one of the two parties will certainly be swallowed by the earth.”
They withdrew and said:
“Abū al-Qāsim, only our foolish people wished to invoke Allah’s curse against you. We would like you to excuse us.”
He said:
“I have excused you.”
He then said:
“The punishment had already drawn near over Najrān.”
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/593–594).
Scholars differed concerning whether mutual invocation remains legislated after the Prophet ﷺ.
The majority held that it was not exclusive to him and may also be practiced by others, because Allah did not state that it was forbidden to his nation after him.
At the same time, they warned against making frequent or casual use of mutual invocation.
It should be restricted to an important religious matter involving confusion and stubborn opposition that cannot be resolved except through such an invocation.
This is because mutual invocation may produce harmful consequences, such as ordinary people becoming fanatically attached to one of the disputants or falsehood gaining publicity that it would not otherwise have received.
The person who undertakes mutual invocation must be knowledgeable, God-fearing, and capable of establishing the proof against his opponent in order to uphold the truth.

12074 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported:
Abū Sufyān told me directly, saying:
I set out during the period of truce that existed between me and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He said:
While I was in Syria, a letter from the Prophet ﷺ was brought to Heraclius. Diḥyah al-Kalbī had delivered it…
He then related the story in detail. The letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ contained:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.
From Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, to Heraclius, the ruler of the Romans. Peace be upon whoever follows guidance.
To proceed: I invite you with the call of Islam. Embrace Islam and you will be safe. Embrace Islam and Allah will give you your reward twice. But if you turn away, then upon you will be the sin of the Arīsiyyīn.
‘O People of the Scripture, come to a common word between us and you: that we worship none but Allah…’
to His words:
‘Bear witness that we are Muslims.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4553) and Muslim (1773). 
This contains a refutation of the view of Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq and others that the beginning of Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, extending to more than eighty verses, was revealed concerning the delegation of Najrān.
Even if his statement is accepted, the noble verse that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote to Heraclius was certainly revealed before the delegation of Najrān.

12075 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever takes a binding oath in order to unlawfully seize the wealth of a Muslim will meet Allah while He is angry with him.”
Allah then revealed confirmation of this:
“Indeed, those who exchange Allah’s covenant and their oaths for a small price will have no share in the Hereafter…”
until the end of the verse.
Al-Ashʿath ibn Qays entered and asked:
“What is Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrating to you?”
They informed him.
He said:
“This verse was revealed concerning me. I had a well situated in the land of one of my cousins.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Produce your evidence, or else he must take an oath.”
I said:
“Then he will simply take an oath, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Whoever takes a binding oath in order to unlawfully seize a Muslim’s property while deliberately lying in that oath will meet Allah while He is angry with him.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4549–4550) and Muslim (138). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12076 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā narrated:
A man displayed merchandise in the marketplace and swore that he had been offered a price for it that no one had actually offered, intending to deceive a Muslim into purchasing it.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Indeed, those who exchange Allah’s covenant and their oaths for a small price…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4551).

12077 — Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
Two women were sewing leather in a house—or in a room—when one of them came out with an awl driven through her palm and accused the other woman.
The matter was brought before Ibn ʿAbbās.
Ibn ʿAbbās said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stated:
“If people were given whatever they claimed, the blood and wealth of others would be lost.”
He said:
“Remind her of Allah and recite to her:
‘Indeed, those who exchange Allah’s covenant…’”
They reminded her of Allah, and she confessed.
Ibn ʿAbbās then said that the Prophet ﷺ stated:
“The oath is required from the defendant.”
Agreed upon:
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4552) and Muslim (1711).

12078 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A man from the Anṣār apostatized from Islam and joined the polytheists.
Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“How can Allah guide a people who disbelieved after having believed…”
until the end of the verse.
His people sent the verse to him, so he returned in repentance.
The Prophet ﷺ accepted his repentance and released him.
Authentic:
Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (4068), Aḥmad (2218), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4477) and al-Ḥākim (2/142).

12079 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Abū Ṭalḥah possessed more date-palm property than any other Anṣārī in Madinah.
The property he loved most was Bayruḥāʾ, which faced the mosque. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to enter it and drink from its pleasant water.
Anas said:
When the verse:
“You will never attain righteousness until you spend from what you love”
was revealed, Abū Ṭalḥah went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, says:
‘You will never attain righteousness until you spend from what you love.’
The property I love most is Bayruḥāʾ. I give it in charity for Allah, hoping for its righteousness and reward stored with Allah. Place it wherever you wish, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Excellent! That is profitable wealth. That is profitable wealth. I have heard what you said concerning it, and I think you should distribute it among your close relatives.”
Abū Ṭalḥah said:
“I will do so, O Messenger of Allah.”
He then distributed it among his relatives and paternal cousins.
Agreed upon:
Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Charity (2), al-Bukhārī (4554), and Muslim (998).

12080 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A group of Jews came before the Prophet of Allah ﷺ one day and said:
“Abū al-Qāsim, tell us about certain matters that we will ask you about, matters known only to a prophet.”
He said:
“Ask me whatever you wish, but give me the covenant of Allah and the covenant Jacob, peace be upon him, took from his sons: if I tell you something and you recognize it as true, you will follow me in Islam.”
They replied:
“You have that covenant.”
He said:
“Then ask me whatever you wish.”
They said:
“Tell us about four matters. Which food did Israel forbid for himself before the Torah was revealed?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I ask you by the One Who revealed the Torah to Moses: Do you know that Israel—Jacob, peace be upon him—became severely ill and his illness lasted for a long time? He made a vow to Allah that if Allah cured him, he would forbid himself the drink and food he loved most. The food he loved most was camel meat, and the drink he loved most was camel milk.”
They replied:
“By Allah, yes.”
The remainder of the hadith was then mentioned.
Good:
Narrated by Aḥmad (2483, 2514).
I say: Israel is Jacob, peace be upon him, after whom the Children of Israel were named.
The sons of Jacob and their descendants continued refraining from camel meat and milk until the time of Moses, peace be upon him.
As for the Torah that Allah, the Exalted, revealed to Moses, it did not prohibit these things.
This is indicated by the words of Allah:
“Before the Torah was revealed.”

12081 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Israel suffered from sciatica and would spend the night in pain.
He vowed to Allah that if Allah cured him, he would no longer eat meat containing veins or sinews.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“All food was lawful for the Children of Israel except what Israel had made unlawful for himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (1/402) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim (3/705), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/292).

12082 — Abū Dharr narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which mosque was first established on earth?”
He replied:
“The Sacred Mosque.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“Al-Aqṣā Mosque.”
I asked:
“How much time was between them?”
He replied:
“Forty years. Wherever the time for prayer reaches you, pray there, for it is a place of prayer.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3366) and Muslim (520).
The words:
“The first House”
indicate that Adam, peace be upon him, descended in Makkah, because the first house of worship was established for the first human being.
The word:
“At Bakkah”
means Makkah, which has several names, including Bakkah.
It was said to have been named Bakkah because it breaks the necks and power of oppressors and tyrants.
It was also said that the name comes from people pressing and crowding against one another there.
It is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās that Makkah extends from the mountain pass to al-Tanʿīm, while Bakkah extends from the Sacred House to al-Baṭḥāʾ.
Other explanations have also been given.
Among the names of Makkah are:
The Secure City, Mother of Cities, the Sacred City, al-Ḥāṭimah, and al-Qādis.
It is called al-Qādis because it purifies people from sins, along with other explanations.
The Words of Allah: “Whoever Enters It Shall Be Safe”
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Whoever enters it shall be safe”:
Whoever seeks sanctuary in the Sacred House is granted sanctuary.
However, he is not to be given shelter, food, or drink. When he leaves the sanctuary, he may be seized for his crime.
The Words of Allah: “Pilgrimage to the House Is a Duty Owed to Allah by Whoever Is Able to Find a Way”

12083 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ addressed us and said:
“O people, Allah has made Ḥajj obligatory upon you, so perform Ḥajj.”
A man asked:
“Every year, O Messenger of Allah?”
The Prophet ﷺ remained silent until the man repeated the question three times.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Had I said yes, it would have become obligatory, and you would not have been able to fulfill it.”
He then said:
“Leave me as long as I leave you without further instruction. Those before you were destroyed only because of their excessive questioning and disagreement with their prophets. When I command you to do something, do as much of it as you are able, and when I prohibit you from something, abandon it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1337).

12084 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
During the pre-Islamic period, warfare had existed between al-Aws and al-Khazraj.
One day, while they were sitting together, they began recalling the conflicts that had occurred between them until they became angry. They rose against one another carrying weapons.
The following verse was then revealed:
“How could you disbelieve while the verses of Allah are being recited to you and His Messenger is among you?”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/720) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12/99).

12085 — Mujāhid narrated:
The people were circumambulating the House while Ibn ʿAbbās sat holding a hooked staff. He said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stated:
“‘O you who believe, fear Allah as He should truly be feared and do not die except as Muslims.’ If a single drop from the Tree of Zaqqūm were to fall upon the earth, it would ruin the livelihood of all its inhabitants. What, then, of those whose only food will be Zaqqūm?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2585), Ibn Mājah (4325), Aḥmad (2735), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim (3/723).
The words:
“Do not die except as Muslims”
mean that you must preserve your Islam in belief and practice so that when death arrives, you remain firmly established upon it.

12086 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Rabb al-Kaʿbah narrated:
I entered the mosque and found ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ sitting in the shade of the Kaʿbah with people gathered around him while he narrated from the Prophet ﷺ.
Among what he narrated was the statement of the Prophet ﷺ:
“Whoever wishes to be removed far from the Fire and admitted into Paradise must let death come to him while he believes in Allah and the Last Day, and he must treat people as he would love them to treat him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1844).

12087 — Jābir narrated:
Three days before the Prophet ﷺ died, I heard him say:
“None of you should die except while holding a good expectation of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2877).

12088 — Bahz ibn Ḥakīm narrated from his father, from his grandfather, that he heard the Prophet ﷺ say concerning the words of Allah:
“You are the best nation raised for mankind”:
“You complete seventy nations. You are the best of them and the most honored of them before Allah.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3001) and Ibn Mājah (4287, 4288).

12089 — Abū Hurayrah said concerning the words of Allah:
“You are the best nation raised for mankind”:
“You are the best people for other people. You bring them with chains around their necks until they enter Islam.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4557).
The meaning of the report is that when disbelievers are captured during warfare, they remain prisoners, as was practiced in earlier and later times.
If they then freely choose Islam and enter it willingly, without coercion—for Allah has prohibited compulsion—they are released and become free, because Islam encourages the emancipation of slaves.

12090 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
One night, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ delayed the ʿIshāʾ prayer.
He then came out to the mosque and found the people waiting for the prayer. He said:
“Behold, no one among the followers of these religions is remembering Allah at this hour except you.”
Allah then revealed:
“They are not all alike; among the People of the Scripture is an upright community who recite the verses of Allah throughout the night while they prostrate.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (3760), Abū Yaʿlā (5306), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (375), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (1530).

12091 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ delayed the ʿIshāʾ prayer until ʿUmar called out:
“The prayer! The women and children have fallen asleep.”
The Prophet ﷺ came out and said:
“No one among the inhabitants of the earth is waiting for this prayer except you.”
At that time, it was not being performed anywhere except in Madinah.
They used to perform it between the disappearance of twilight and the end of the first third of the night.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (569) and Muslim (638).

12092 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah never sent a prophet or appointed a ruler except that he had two groups of close advisers: one group commanded him to do good and encouraged him toward it, while the other commanded him to do evil and encouraged him toward it. The protected person is the one whom Allah protects.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6611).
The words:
“You believe in all the Scripture”
mean that Muslims believe in all the Books Allah revealed to His prophets, such as the Scrolls of Abraham, the Torah of Moses, and the Gospel of Jesus.
They, however, do not believe in the Book that Allah revealed to the Prophet of mercy and the final prophet, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him.

12093 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The following verse was revealed concerning us:
“When two groups among you nearly lost courage, but Allah was their Protector.”
They were Banū Salamah and Banū Ḥārithah.
We would not wish that the verse had not been revealed because Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“Allah was their Protector.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4051) and Muslim (2505).

12094 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wished to supplicate against someone or for someone, he would perform qunūt after bowing.
Sometimes, after saying:
“Allah hears whoever praises Him,”
he would say aloud:
“O Allah, our Lord, to You belongs all praise. O Allah, rescue al-Walīd ibn al-Walīd, Salamah ibn Hishām, and ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Rabīʿah. O Allah, intensify Your punishment upon Muḍar and make their years like the years of Joseph.”
During some of his dawn prayers, he would also say:
“O Allah, curse so-and-so and so-and-so,”
referring to certain Arab tribes, until Allah revealed:
“You have no authority in the matter.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4560) and Muslim (675). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12095 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
He heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, after raising his head from bowing in the final rakʿah of the dawn prayer, say:
“O Allah, curse so-and-so, so-and-so, and so-and-so,”
after saying:
“Allah hears whoever praises Him. Our Lord, to You belongs all praise.”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“You have no authority in the matter…”
continuing until:
“For they are wrongdoers.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4069).

12096 — Anas narrated:
At the Battle of Uḥud, one of the front teeth of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was broken and his head was wounded.
As he wiped the blood from himself, he said:
“How can a people succeed when they have wounded their prophet and broken his tooth while he calls them to Allah?”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“You have no authority in the matter.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1791/104).

12097 — Abū Isḥāq said:
I heard al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrate:
On the Day of Uḥud, the Prophet ﷺ appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn Jubayr as commander over the archers, who numbered fifty men. He said:
“Even if you see birds snatching us away, do not leave this position until I send for you. And even if you see us defeat and trample the enemy, do not leave until I send for you.”
The Muslims defeated them.
Al-Barāʾ said:
“By Allah, I saw their women running swiftly with their garments raised, so that their anklets and lower legs were exposed.”
The companions of ʿAbdullāh ibn Jubayr said:
“The spoils, people, the spoils! Your companions have prevailed, so what are you waiting for?”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Jubayr said:
“Have you forgotten what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told you?”
They replied:
“By Allah, we will go to the people and take our share of the spoils.”
When they went to them, the tide of battle was turned against the Muslims, and they began retreating in defeat. That was when the Messenger was calling them from behind.
Only twelve men remained with the Prophet ﷺ, and the enemy killed seventy of the Muslims.
On the Day of Badr, the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions had inflicted losses upon 140 of the polytheists: seventy prisoners and seventy killed.
Abū Sufyān called out three times:
“Is Muḥammad among the people?”
The Prophet ﷺ forbade them from answering him.
He then called out three times:
“Is the son of Abū Quḥāfah among the people?”
He then called out three times:
“Is the son of al-Khaṭṭāb among the people?”
He returned to his companions and said:
“These men have been killed.”
ʿUmar could no longer restrain himself and said:
“You have lied, by Allah, enemy of Allah! All those whom you named are alive, and there still remains for you that which will grieve you.”
Abū Sufyān said:
“This day is in exchange for the Day of Badr, and warfare alternates between opponents. You will find mutilation among your dead. I did not order it, yet it does not displease me.”
He then began chanting:
“Exalted be Hubal! Exalted be Hubal!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Will you not answer him?”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what should we say?”
He replied:
“Say: Allah is higher and more majestic.”
Abū Sufyān said:
“We have al-ʿUzzā, while you have no ʿUzzā.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Will you not answer him?”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what should we say?”
He replied:
“Say: Allah is our Protector, and you have no protector.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3039).

12098 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated:
Drowsiness overcame us while we stood in our battle ranks on the Day of Uḥud. My sword repeatedly fell from my hand; I would pick it up, then it would fall again, and I would pick it up once more.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4562).

12099 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated:
I raised my head on the Day of Uḥud and looked around. Every one of them was swaying beneath his leather shield because of drowsiness.
That is the meaning of the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“Then, after the distress, He sent down upon you security in the form of drowsiness.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3007), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11134), and Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah (19892).
Drowsiness also occurred during the Battle of Badr, as will appear in the commentary on Sūrat al-Anfāl, verse 11.

12100 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ met me and said:
“Jābir, why do I see you broken-hearted?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, my father was martyred and left dependents and debt behind.”
He said:
“Shall I not give you glad tidings of how Allah received your father?”
I replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Allah never spoke to anyone except from behind a veil, but He brought your father to life and spoke to him directly. He said: ‘My servant, ask Me for whatever you wish and I will grant it to you.’
Your father said: ‘My Lord, bring me back to life so that I may be killed for Your sake a second time.’
The Lord, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘It has already been decreed by Me that they will not return.’”
Jābir said:
The following verse was then revealed:
“Do not think of those who were killed in the cause of Allah as dead.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3010), Ibn Mājah (190), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7022) and al-Ḥākim (3/203–204).
There is no contradiction between the different narrations. One version is detailed while another is abbreviated, and this difference goes back to Jābir himself, who narrated it once in detail and another time concisely.
Jābir’s father was ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām al-Anṣārī, who was martyred on the Day of Uḥud.
Jābir said:
I began weeping and uncovering the cloth from his face. The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ tried to stop me, but the Prophet ﷺ did not stop me.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not weep over him—or why do you weep over him? The angels continued shading him with their wings until he was lifted away.”
Narrated by the two Shaykhs: al-Bukhārī (4080) and Muslim (2471).

12101 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When your brothers were struck down at Uḥud, Allah placed their souls inside green birds. They visit the rivers of Paradise, eat from its fruits, and return to golden lamps suspended beneath the Throne.
When they experienced the delight of their food, drink, and resting places, they said: ‘Who will convey to our brothers that we are alive in Paradise and receiving provision, so that they will not lose interest in striving or retreat during battle?’
Allah, the Exalted, said: ‘I will convey it to them on your behalf.’
Then Allah revealed: ‘Do not think of those who were killed in the cause of Allah as dead…’ until the end of the verse.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2520).

12102 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said concerning the words of Allah:
“Do not think of those who were killed in the cause of Allah as dead. Rather, they are alive with their Lord, receiving provision”:
“We asked about this, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
‘Their souls are inside green birds that have lamps suspended from the Throne. They roam freely throughout Paradise wherever they wish and then return to those lamps.
Their Lord looked upon them and said: “Do you desire anything?”
They replied: “What could we desire when we roam freely throughout Paradise wherever we wish?”
He asked them this three times. When they realized that they would continue to be asked, they said: “Our Lord, we wish You would return our souls to our bodies so that we may be killed in Your cause once again.”’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Leadership (1887), through several routes from al-Aʿmash, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Murrah, from Masrūq, who said: “We asked ʿAbdullāh about this verse,” and he narrated the report.

12103 — ʿĀʾishah said to ʿUrwah:
“Son of my sister, your two forefathers were among them: al-Zubayr and Abū Bakr.
After the Prophet of Allah ﷺ suffered what he suffered on the Day of Uḥud and the polytheists withdrew, he feared that they might return. He said:
‘Who will pursue them?’
Seventy men volunteered. Abū Bakr and al-Zubayr were among them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4077) and Muslim (2418/51).

12104 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the polytheists withdrew from Uḥud and reached al-Rawḥāʾ, they said:
“You neither killed Muḥammad nor captured the young women. What a terrible thing you have done! Return!”
News of this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so he called the people to set out. They responded until they reached Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad and the well of Abū ʿInabah.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Those who responded to Allah and the Messenger after injury had afflicted them…”
Abū Sufyān had said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Our appointment with you will be at the next season of Badr, where you killed our companions.”
The cowardly turned back, while the courageous prepared themselves for both fighting and trade.
When they reached Badr, they found no enemy there, so they conducted trade in the market.
Allah then revealed:
“They returned with favor from Allah and bounty, untouched by harm, and they pursued the pleasure of Allah. Allah is the Possessor of tremendous bounty.”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:174]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11017) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/247).
In summary, after the polytheists inflicted what they did upon the Muslims, they initially intended to return to their homeland. They then regretted withdrawing and resolved to return to Madinah and destroy it.
When news of this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he called the Muslims to pursue them in order to frighten them and demonstrate the strength and endurance of the Muslims.
He did not permit anyone to join except those who had attended the Battle of Uḥud, with the exception of Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh.
Jābir explained that his father had left him behind to care for his seven sisters, so the Prophet ﷺ permitted him to accompany them.

12105 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs”:
Abraham, peace be upon him, said them when he was thrown into the fire, and Muḥammad ﷺ said them when the people were told:
“The people have gathered against you, so fear them.” But this only increased them in faith, and they said, “Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4563).

12106 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The final words spoken by Abraham when he was thrown into the fire were:
“Allah is sufficient for me, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4564).
Scholars differed concerning when the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.”
The strongest report is that transmitted from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq:
The Prophet ﷺ said it while at Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad after Uḥud, when news reached him that Abū Sufyān had gathered forces to march against the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions in order to annihilate those who remained.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.”
Ibn Isḥāq mentioned this in his Sīrah, as preserved in the Sīrah of Ibn Hishām, in a detailed report from ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm.

12107 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is given wealth by Allah but does not pay its zakāh will have his wealth represented for him on the Day of Resurrection as a bald-headed, venomous male snake with two dark markings above its eyes. It will be placed around his neck, seize him by the sides of his mouth, and say: ‘I am your wealth. I am your treasure.’”
He then recited:
“Let not those who withhold what Allah has given them from His bounty think that it is good for them…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4565).

12108 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Jews came to Muḥammad ﷺ when Allah revealed:
“Who is it that will lend Allah a goodly loan?”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:245]
They said:
“Is your Lord poor, asking His servants for a loan?”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Allah has certainly heard the statement of those who said: ‘Allah is poor and we are rich.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/828).

12109 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A place in Paradise equal to the length of one of your whips is better than the world and everything within it.”
He then recited:
“Whoever is moved away from the Fire and admitted into Paradise has truly succeeded. The life of this world is nothing but the enjoyment of delusion.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3013), Aḥmad (9651), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7417) and al-Ḥākim (2/299).

12110 — Usāmah ibn Zayd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ rode upon a donkey with a cloak from Fadak beneath him, while Usāmah ibn Zayd rode behind him. He was going to visit Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah among Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj before the Battle of Badr.
He passed by a gathering in which ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl was present. This was before ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy outwardly accepted Islam. The gathering included a mixture of Muslims, idol-worshipping polytheists, and Jews. ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah was also among them.
When the dust raised by the animal reached the gathering, ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy covered his nose with his cloak and said:
“Do not cover us with dust.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ greeted them, stopped, dismounted, called them to Allah, and recited the Qur’an to them.
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl said:
“Man, nothing could be better than what you say, provided that it is true. But do not disturb us with it in our gathering. Return to your dwelling, and relate it to whoever comes to you.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah said:
“Rather, O Messenger of Allah, come to us with it in our gatherings, for we love that.”
The Muslims, polytheists, and Jews began insulting one another until they nearly attacked each other. The Prophet ﷺ continued calming them until they became quiet.
The Prophet ﷺ then mounted his animal and proceeded until he entered upon Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah. He said:
“Saʿd, did you not hear what Abū Ḥubāb said?”
He meant ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy, and then mentioned what he had said.
Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, pardon and overlook him. By the One Who revealed the Book to you, Allah has brought the truth that He revealed to you. The people of this settlement had agreed to crown him and place the royal headband upon him. When Allah prevented that through the truth He granted you, he was choked with resentment. That is what caused him to behave as you saw.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ pardoned him.
The Prophet ﷺ and his Companions used to pardon the polytheists and the People of the Scripture, as Allah had commanded them, and they patiently endured their abuse.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says:
“You will certainly hear much abuse from those who were given the Scripture before you and from those who associate partners with Allah.”
Allah also says:
“Many of the People of the Scripture wish that they could turn you back to disbelief after your faith, out of envy from within themselves.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:109]
The Prophet ﷺ continued practicing the pardon Allah had commanded until Allah permitted him to fight them.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ fought at Badr and Allah destroyed through him the leading disbelievers of Quraysh, Ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl and the polytheists and idol worshippers with him said:
“This matter has clearly become established.”
They then pledged allegiance to the Messenger ﷺ upon Islam and outwardly became Muslims.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4566) and Muslim (1798/116).

12111 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
During the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, certain hypocrites would remain behind whenever the Prophet ﷺ departed for battle. They rejoiced in sitting behind in opposition to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
When the Prophet ﷺ returned, they would present excuses, swear oaths, and desire to be praised for what they had not done.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Do not think that those who rejoice in what they have done and love to be praised for what they have not done will escape the punishment.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4567) and Muslim (2777).

12112 — Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf narrated:
Marwān said to his gatekeeper:
“Rāfiʿ, go to Ibn ʿAbbās and say: If every person among us who rejoices in what he has done and loves to be praised for what he has not done is to be punished, then all of us will certainly be punished.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“What connection do you have with this verse? It was revealed only concerning the People of the Scripture.”
Ibn ʿAbbās then recited:
“When Allah took the covenant of those who were given the Scripture: You must make it clear to the people and not conceal it.”
He also recited:
“Do not think that those who rejoice in what they have done and love to be praised for what they have not done…”
Ibn ʿAbbās explained:
“The Prophet ﷺ asked them about something, but they concealed it from him and informed him of something else. They departed after making him believe that they had answered what he asked. They sought his praise for that and rejoiced in having concealed what he had asked them about.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4568) and Muslim (2778).

12113 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
I spent the night at the home of my maternal aunt Maymūnah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke with his family for a while and then went to sleep.
During the final third of the night, he sat up, looked toward the sky, and recited:
“Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth and the alternation of night and day are signs for people of understanding.”
He then stood, performed ablution, cleaned his teeth with the siwāk, and prayed eleven rakʿahs.
Bilāl then called the adhān. The Prophet ﷺ prayed two rakʿahs and then went out and led the dawn prayer.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4569) and Muslim (763/190).

12114 — ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ narrated:
ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr and I entered upon ʿĀʾishah.
She said to ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr:
“It is time that you visited us.”
He replied:
“Mother, I say as the earlier person said: ‘Visit at intervals and your love will increase.’”
She said:
“Spare us this foreign manner of speech of yours.”
Ibn ʿUmayr said:
“Tell us about the most remarkable thing you ever saw from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She remained silent for a while and then said:
One night, he said:
“ʿĀʾishah, allow me to worship my Lord tonight.”
I replied:
“By Allah, I love being close to you, and I love whatever pleases you.”
He stood, purified himself, and began praying.
He continued weeping until his lap became wet. He then continued weeping until his beard became wet. He continued weeping until the ground became wet.
Bilāl came to inform him that it was time for prayer. When he saw him weeping, he asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, why are you weeping when Allah has forgiven your past and future sins?”
He replied:
“Should I not be a grateful servant? A verse was revealed to me tonight. Woe to the one who recites it but does not reflect upon it:
‘Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth…’”
He recited the entire verse.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (620).

12115 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
He spent one night at the home of Maymūnah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, who was his maternal aunt.
He said:
I lay across the width of the cushion, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his wife lay along its length.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ slept until approximately midnight—slightly before or slightly after it. He awoke, sat up, and wiped the sleep from his face with his hand.
He then recited the final ten verses of Sūrat Āl ʿImrān. Afterward, he went to a hanging water skin, performed ablution from it thoroughly, and stood to pray.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
I stood and did as he had done, then went and stood beside him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ placed his right hand upon my head, took hold of my right ear, and gently twisted it.
He prayed two rakʿahs, then two, then two, then two, then two, then two, and then performed Witr.
He lay down until the caller to prayer came. He prayed two brief rakʿahs and then went out and led the dawn prayer.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (11), al-Bukhārī (4570), and Muslim (763/182).

12116 — Umm Salamah said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we do not hear Allah mentioning women in connection with emigration.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Their Lord answered them: I will never allow the deed of any worker among you, male or female, to be lost…”
until the end of the verse.
The Anṣār said concerning Umm Salamah:
“She was the first woman traveling in a litter who came to us.”
Good: Narrated by Saʿīd ibn Manṣūr (552), al-Tirmidhī (3023), and al-Ḥākim (2/300).
The words:
“With Allah is the finest reward”
mean that whoever performs righteous deeds has a fine reward with Allah, whether granted sooner or later.
Shaddād ibn Aws said:
“People, do not suspect Allah of injustice in His decree, for He does not wrong a believer. When something a believer loves comes to him, let him praise Allah. When something he dislikes comes to him, let him remain patient and seek the reward, for with Allah is the finest reward.”
Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/844).

12117 — Anas narrated:
When news of the death of the Negus reached them, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Perform the funeral prayer for him.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we pray over an Abyssinian slave?”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Indeed, among the People of the Scripture are those who believe in Allah, in what was revealed to you, and in what was revealed to them, humbly submitting to Allah. They do not exchange the verses of Allah for a small price. Those will have their reward with their Lord. Allah is swift in reckoning.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11022) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (832).

12118 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Shall I direct you to that by which Allah erases sins and raises ranks?”
They replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Performing ablution thoroughly despite difficulty, taking many steps to the mosques, and waiting for one prayer after another. That is steadfast guarding and readiness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (251).
The second meaning is the stationing of fighters opposite the enemy and guarding the borders of Muslim lands against the entrance of the enemies of Allah.

12119 — Salmān al-Fārisī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Guarding the frontier for one day and one night is better than fasting and praying for an entire month. If the one guarding it dies, the reward of the deed he used to perform continues for him, his provision continues to be granted to him, and he is protected from the trial of the grave.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1913).
Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ wrote to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb informing him of the large Roman forces and the danger he feared from them.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb wrote back:
“To proceed: Whenever a believing servant is afflicted by a severe hardship, Allah places relief after it. One hardship will never overcome two eases. Allah, the Exalted, says in His Book:
‘O you who believe, be patient, persevere, remain steadfast, and fear Allah so that you may succeed.’”
Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Jihād (6).
The most appropriate combined meaning is:
Remain patient in obeying Allah and abandoning prohibited things. Outlast the harm of the polytheists and the enemies of Islam. Remain constantly prepared and guard the borders of the Muslim lands.
Along with these righteous deeds, do not neglect the fear of Allah, for it is the key to success in this world and the Hereafter.
End of the Tafsīr of Sūrat Āl ʿImrān.

12120 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The woman was created from a rib. She will not remain completely straight according to your way. If you enjoy companionship with her, you will do so while some curvature remains in her. But if you attempt to straighten her completely, you will break her, and breaking her means divorcing her.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1468/60), whose wording is cited here, and al-Bukhārī (5184).
Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī narrated in his Tafsīr (6/341–342), from Ibn Ḥumayd, who said: Salamah narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said:
“Drowsiness was cast upon Adam, peace and blessings be upon him, according to what has reached us from the People of the Scripture who possessed the Torah and from other people of knowledge, from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās and others.
A rib was then taken from the left side of Adam, and its place was closed while Adam remained asleep and did not awaken. Allah, Blessed and Exalted, created from that rib his wife Eve and formed her as a woman so that he might find tranquility with her.
When the drowsiness was lifted from him and he awoke, he saw her beside him and said—according to what they claim, and Allah knows best—‘My flesh, my blood, and my wife!’ He then found tranquility with her.”
Its chain of transmission is disconnected.
The words:
“From the people of the Torah”
are accurate in this respect, for a similar account appears in Genesis (2:21–22).

12121 — ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words of Allah:
“If you fear that you will not deal justly with the orphan girls…”
This verse was revealed concerning a man who had an orphan girl under his guardianship. He was both her guardian and her heir, and she possessed wealth, while no one was available to defend her rights against him.
He would marry her because of her wealth and then harm her and treat her badly.
Allah therefore said:
“If you fear that you will not deal justly with the orphan girls, then marry whatever women are lawful and pleasing to you…”
Meaning:
“Marry those whom I have made lawful for you and leave this orphan girl whom you would harm.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4573) and Muslim (3018/7).

12122 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr asked ʿĀʾishah concerning the words of Allah:
“If you fear that you will not deal justly with the orphan girls…”
She replied:
“Son of my sister, this refers to an orphan girl who is under the guardianship of a man and shares in his property. He is attracted to her wealth and beauty and wishes to marry her without dealing justly regarding her bridal gift by giving her the amount that another man would give her.
They were therefore forbidden from marrying orphan girls unless they treated them justly and granted them the highest customary bridal gift appropriate for women like them. They were instead commanded to marry other women who were lawful and pleasing to them.”
ʿUrwah continued:
ʿĀʾishah said:
“The people later asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for a ruling concerning women after this verse, so Allah revealed:
‘They ask you for a ruling concerning women.’
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:127]”
ʿĀʾishah said:
The words of Allah in the other verse:
“Whom you are unwilling to marry”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:127]
refer to a guardian’s lack of desire to marry an orphan girl under his care when she possesses little wealth and beauty.
She said:
“They were forbidden from marrying orphan girls whose wealth and beauty attracted them unless they acted justly, because they would otherwise avoid marrying those who possessed little wealth and beauty.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4574) and Muslim (3018). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The words:
“Two, three, or four”
mean that a man may marry two, three, or four women. If he fears that he will not act justly, he must be content with one.
The scholars of Islam unanimously agreed that a Muslim man may not marry more than four women at one time.
An opposing view has been attributed to a group among the Shīʿah who permitted marrying up to nine women, while some of them claimed there was no fixed limit.
All of these views contradict the guidance of the Prophet ﷺ, for permission to have more than four wives was among the exclusive privileges of the Prophet ﷺ.

12123 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
Ghaylān ibn Salamah al-Thaqafī accepted Islam while he had ten wives.
The Prophet ﷺ ordered him to retain four of them.
Good: Narrated by al-Dāraquṭnī (3/271) and al-Bayhaqī (7/183).
The words:
“That is more likely that you will not commit injustice”
mean that you will not act unjustly or oppressively.
This is how the majority of scholars explained the verse.
This explanation has been reported from ʿĀʾishah as a prophetic narration, but the correct view is that it is her own statement.
Some scholars said that the phrase means:
“So that you do not incline unjustly.”
The first interpretation is more correct.

12124 — ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words of Allah:
“Whoever is wealthy should refrain, and whoever is poor may consume according to what is reasonable”:
This verse was revealed concerning the guardian of an orphan. If the guardian is poor, he may take a reasonable amount from the orphan’s wealth in compensation for caring for and managing his affairs.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4575) and Muslim (3019).

12125 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb narrated from his father, from his grandfather:
A man asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“I possess no wealth, but I have an orphan under my care. What may I do?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Eat reasonably from the wealth of your orphan without extravagance.”
Or he said:
“Do not preserve your own wealth by spending his wealth instead.”
Ḥusayn, one of the narrators, was uncertain regarding the exact wording.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2872), al-Nasāʾī (6/256), Ibn Mājah (2718), and Aḥmad (6747).
The words:
“Do not preserve your wealth by means of his wealth”
mean that you must not save your own property by spending the orphan’s property in situations where you ought to spend from your own wealth.
Scholars differed over whether an orphan’s guardian must repay what he reasonably took if he later becomes wealthy.
The correct view is that he is not obligated to repay it because he took it as compensation for his work. However, he may repay it voluntarily if he wishes.
It is reported that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“I have placed myself in relation to this public wealth in the position of an orphan’s guardian. If I am financially independent, I refrain from taking anything. If I am in need, I borrow from it, and when I become financially able, I repay it.”
Narrated by al-Bayhaqī.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“The orphan’s guardian may take from the orphan’s wealth enough for his food and clothing, may drink surplus milk, and may ride a spare mount. If he later becomes wealthy, he should repay it, but if he remains poor, he is absolved.”
Narrated by al-Bayhaqī.
Requiring repayment in every case could discourage guardians from managing the orphan’s interests and thereby cause those interests to be neglected.

12126 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Some people claim that this verse was abrogated. By Allah, it was not abrogated, but it is one of the rulings that people have neglected.
There are two kinds of guardians present at the division: a guardian who inherits, and he should give something to those present; and a guardian who does not inherit, and he should speak kindly, saying, ‘I do not possess the authority to give you anything.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2759).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated it in the Book of Tafsīr (4576) through another route, limiting the wording to:
“It remains legally effective and has not been abrogated.”
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, ʿIkrimah, and others likewise narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās that this verse remains in force and was not abrogated.
Some of Ibn ʿAbbās’s students narrated from him that this verse applied before the prescribed inheritance shares were revealed.
When the inheritance shares were revealed, Allah assigned each eligible person a fixed portion of what parents and close relatives left behind, whether little or much.
This is the position of the majority of jurists, including the four imams and their followers.
Even if the obligation was abrogated, the act remains recommended and encouraged.
If those entitled to fixed inheritance shares willingly wish to give something to orphans, poor people, and relatives who do not inherit, they may do so.

12127 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Avoid the seven destructive sins.”
Among them, he mentioned:
“Unlawfully consuming the wealth of an orphan.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2766) and Muslim (89).

12128 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The wife of Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with her two daughters by Saʿd and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, these are the two daughters of Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ. Their father was martyred while fighting beside you on the Day of Uḥud. Their paternal uncle has taken their wealth and left them nothing, and they will not be able to marry unless they possess wealth.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah will issue a judgment concerning this matter.”
The verse of inheritance was then revealed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for their paternal uncle and said:
“Give the two daughters of Saʿd two-thirds, give their mother one-eighth, and whatever remains belongs to you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2092), whose wording is cited here, Ibn Mājah (2720), Abū Dāwūd (2892), and Aḥmad (14798).

12129 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Previously, the estate belonged to the children, while parents received through a bequest. Allah then abrogated from that whatever He willed.
He assigned the male the share of two females. He assigned the parents one-sixth or one-third according to the circumstances. He assigned the wife one-eighth or one-fourth, and the husband one-half or one-fourth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4578).
These verses, together with the verse at the end of Sūrat al-Nisāʾ, are the verses governing inheritance. They contain most of the rulings concerning prescribed inheritance shares, except for the inheritance of the grandmother, who receives one-sixth, as established in the Sunnah and unanimously agreed upon by the scholars.
The word kalālah in the verse:
“If a man or woman leaves an inheritance as kalālah…”
is derived from iklīl, meaning a crown that surrounds the head from its sides.
Here, it refers to a deceased person whose heirs are collateral relatives rather than direct ascendants or descendants.
Thus, the deceased has no surviving direct ascendant, such as a father, grandfather, or anyone above them, whether male or female; and no direct descendant, such as a child, grandchild, or anyone below them, whether male or female.
Concerning the words:
“After any bequest that has been made or any debt,”
the earlier and later scholars unanimously agreed that debts must be paid before carrying out a bequest.
Although the bequest is mentioned first in the verse, it is carried out after the debts are paid. Allah did not intend the sequence of mention to establish their legal order. Rather, He intended to show that both must be settled before the estate is divided among the heirs.
A bequest may only be made from one-third of the estate or less, whereas debts may consume the entire estate.
A bequest must be made justly and must not be intended to harm the heirs, for such harm is a form of wrongdoing.
It has been authentically attributed as a statement of Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Causing harm through a bequest is among the major sins.”
It has also been narrated as a statement of the Prophet ﷺ, but that version is not authentic.

12130 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Take this ruling from me; take this ruling from me. Allah has now appointed a way for them: an unmarried person who commits fornication with an unmarried person is to receive one hundred lashes and be exiled for one year, while a previously married person who commits adultery with a previously married person is to receive one hundred lashes and be stoned.”
In another narration:
Whenever revelation descended upon the Prophet ﷺ, he would experience distress and his face would become darkened.
One day, revelation descended upon him and he experienced this condition. When it was lifted from him, he said:
“Take this ruling from me, for Allah has now appointed a way for them: the previously married person with the previously married person, and the unmarried person with the unmarried person. The previously married person is to receive one hundred lashes and then be stoned, while the unmarried person is to receive one hundred lashes and then be exiled for one year.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1690/12). The second version was also narrated by Muslim through another chain.
The scholarly disagreement regarding combining flogging and stoning for a previously married adulterer is well known.
The majority held that a previously married adulterer is only to be stoned and is not flogged beforehand. This is because the Prophet ﷺ stoned Māʿiz, the Ghāmidiyyah woman, and the two Jewish adulterers without first flogging them. They therefore considered flogging to have been abrogated in the case of previously married adulterers.

12131 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah accepts the repentance of His servant so long as the soul has not reached the throat.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3537), Ibn Mājah (4253), Aḥmad (6160), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (628) and al-Ḥākim (4/257).

12132 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Satan said: ‘By Your might, my Lord, I will continue misleading Your servants so long as their souls remain within their bodies.’
The Lord said: ‘By My might and majesty, I will continue forgiving them so long as they seek My forgiveness.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (11237, 11244, 11729).

12133 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When a man died, his male relatives considered themselves more entitled to his widow than her own family.
One of them could marry her if he wished, they could marry her to someone else if they wished, or they could prevent her from marrying altogether.
This verse was revealed concerning that practice.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4579).

12134 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“It is not lawful for you to inherit women against their will, nor should you constrain them in order to take back part of what you gave them, unless they commit a clearly proven immorality”:
A man would inherit the widow of one of his relatives and prevent her from marrying until she died or returned her bridal gift to him.
Allah firmly prohibited this practice.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2090).
The words:
“Unless they commit a clearly proven immorality”
refer to adultery.
That is, if her adultery is legally established, the husband may recover from her the bridal gift he gave her.

12135 — Muḥammad ibn Abī Umāmah narrated from his father:
When Abū Qays ibn al-Aslat died, his son wished to marry his father’s widow, for this had been their practice during the pre-Islamic period.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“It is not lawful for you to inherit women against their will.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11029) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/902).

12136 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A believing man should not hate a believing woman. If he dislikes one quality in her, he will be pleased with another.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1469).
The word yafrak means to hate or detest, and fark means hatred.

12137 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
The people of the pre-Islamic period generally regarded as unlawful the same marriages that Islam prohibited, except that they married their fathers’ widows and combined two sisters in marriage.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Do not marry women whom your fathers married, except what has already occurred. It was indeed a shameful act, a hateful practice, and an evil way.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (6/549).

12138 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Do not marry women whom your fathers married”:
Every woman whom your father or son married is permanently forbidden to you, whether or not the marriage was consummated.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (6/550).

12139 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
On the Day of Ḥunayn, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent an army to Awṭās. They encountered the enemy, fought them, defeated them, and took female captives.
Some of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ felt uneasy about having relations with them because their polytheist husbands were still alive.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Also forbidden to you are married women, except those whom your right hands possess.”
That is, such captive women became lawful for them after completing the required waiting period.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Breastfeeding (1456/33).

12140 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent him on the expedition of Dhāt al-Salāsil, he experienced a wet dream during an intensely cold night. He feared that if he performed the ritual bath, he would perish, so he performed dry ablution and then led his companions in the dawn prayer.
When they returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, ʿAmr mentioned what had happened.
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“ʿAmr, did you lead your companions in prayer while you were in a state of major ritual impurity?”
He replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah. I experienced a wet dream during a bitterly cold night, and I feared that if I bathed, I would perish. I remembered the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
‘Do not kill yourselves. Indeed, Allah is ever Merciful to you.’
So I performed dry ablution and then prayed.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and said nothing.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (334), Aḥmad (17812), and al-Ḥākim (1/177–178).
Ibn Mardawayh narrated through another route from Ibn ʿAbbās that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ led the people in prayer while in a state of major ritual impurity.
When they returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they informed him of it. He summoned ʿAmr and questioned him.
ʿAmr said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I feared that the cold would kill me, and Allah, the Exalted, has said:
‘Do not kill yourselves. Indeed, Allah is ever Merciful to you.’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not rebuke him.
Concerning the words:
“Whoever does that through aggression and wrongdoing, We will cast him into a Fire,”
Allah warns that whoever deliberately and wrongfully kills himself will be admitted into the Fire.

12141 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever throws himself from a mountain and kills himself will be in the Fire of Hell, repeatedly throwing himself down within it, remaining therein perpetually.
Whoever drinks poison and kills himself will have his poison in his hand and will continue drinking it in the Fire of Hell, remaining therein perpetually.
Whoever kills himself with an iron weapon will have that weapon in his hand and will continue stabbing himself in the abdomen with it in the Fire of Hell, remaining therein perpetually.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5778) and Muslim (109/175).

12142 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Avoid the seven destructive sins.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what are they?”
He replied:
“Associating partners with Allah, sorcery, killing a soul whom Allah has forbidden to be killed except by legal right, consuming usury, consuming an orphan’s wealth, fleeing on the day the armies meet, and falsely accusing chaste, believing, unsuspecting women of adultery.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Bequests (2766) and Muslim in the Book of Faith (89).

12143 — Umm Salamah said:
“Men participate in military expeditions, whereas women do not, and we receive only half the inheritance.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Do not desire what Allah has given some of you over others.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3022), Aḥmad (26736), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (4/935).

12144 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Do not desire what Allah has given some of you over others. Men will have a share of what they earn, and women will have a share of what they earn”:
A woman came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Prophet of Allah, a male receives the share of two females, and the testimony of two women equals that of one man. Is the same true concerning our deeds? If a woman performs one good deed, will only half a good deed be recorded for her?”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Do not desire…”
Meaning:
“This is justice from Me, and I am the One Who ordained it.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/935) and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (10/116–117).
The words:
“This is justice from Me, and I am the One Who ordained it”
affirm that Allah, the Exalted, is the One Who assigned a male the share of two females, and that this ruling proceeds from His perfect justice.
Some scholars held that the reward of a woman’s deeds was half that of a man’s deeds because Allah prohibited women from desiring what He had granted men over them.
Likewise, a man was forbidden from saying:
“If only I possessed the wealth and family of so-and-so.”
Instead, he should ask Allah from His bounty.
Ibn ʿAbbās stated this in his explanation of the words:
“Do not desire what Allah has given some of you over others.”

12145 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“For everyone We have appointed heirs”:
It means inheritors.
Regarding the words:
“Those with whom your oaths have established a bond,”
when the Emigrants first arrived in Madinah, an Emigrant would inherit from an Anṣārī instead of the Anṣārī’s blood relatives because of the bond of brotherhood that the Prophet ﷺ had established between them.
When the words:
“For everyone We have appointed heirs”
were revealed, that form of inheritance was abrogated.
The words:
“Those with whom your oaths have established a bond, give them their due share”
then referred to support, assistance, and sincere counsel. Their right to inherit had ended, although a bequest could still be made for them.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4580).

12146 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated in his description of the pilgrimage of the Prophet ﷺ that during the Farewell Pilgrimage, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Fear Allah regarding women, for you have taken them under the protection of Allah and made intimacy with them lawful through the word of Allah.
Your right over them is that they do not allow anyone whom you dislike onto your bedding. If they do that, strike them in a manner that is not severe or injurious.
Their right over you is that you provide their food and clothing according to what is reasonable.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).
The words:
“Not severe or injurious”
mean, as the jurists explained, that it must not break any part of her body or leave any mark upon her.

12147 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Zamʿah narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ delivering a sermon in which he mentioned women and said:
“One of you beats his wife as a slave is beaten, yet he may then lie intimately with her at the end of the same day.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4942) and Muslim (2855).

12148 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never struck anything with his hand—neither a woman nor a servant.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2328).

12149 — Muʿādh ibn Jabal reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“O Muʿādh, do you know Allah’s right over His servants?”
He replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“That they worship Him and do not associate anything with Him. Do you know their right upon Him?”
He replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“That He not punish them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7373) and Muslim (50/30).

12150 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“Jibrīl continued to advise me concerning the neighbor until I thought that he would make him an heir.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6015) and Muslim in al-Birr wa al-Ṣilah (2625).

12151 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ had a sheep slaughtered for him by his household. When he arrived, he said:
“Did you give some as a gift to our Jewish neighbor? Did you give some as a gift to our Jewish neighbor? I heard the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ say:
‘Jibrīl continued to advise me concerning the neighbor until I thought that he would make him an heir.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5152), al-Tirmidhī (1943), and Aḥmad (6496).

12152 — Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said to his Companions:
“What do you say concerning adultery?”
They replied, “Allāh and His Messenger have forbidden it, so it is forbidden until the Day of Resurrection.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ then said to his Companions:
“For a man to commit adultery with ten women would be less serious for him than to commit adultery with his neighbor’s wife.”
He then said:
“What do you say concerning theft?”
They replied, “Allāh and His Messenger have forbidden it, so it is forbidden until the Day of Resurrection.”
He said:
“For a man to steal from ten houses would be less serious for him than to steal from his neighbor.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23854), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (103), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (20/256–257).
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar described Abū Ẓabyah al-Kalāʿī as “acceptable” (maqbūl). However, Ibn Maʿīn and al-Dārimī declared him trustworthy, Ibn Ḥibbān included him among the trustworthy narrators, and al-Dāraquṭnī said, “There is no problem with him.” Thus, at the very least, his status is that of a truthful narrator (ṣadūq), and his ḥadīth is therefore graded ḥasan.
Regarding His saying, {“and the companion at your side”}: according to ʿAlī and Ibn Masʿūd, it refers to one’s wife.
According to Ibn ʿAbbās, it refers to one’s companion on a journey—that is, the person who remains by one’s side.
I say: this “companion” includes both men and women, whether during travel or while residing at home. A companion has many rights over his or her companion. Among these rights is that one should love for one’s companion what one loves for oneself and dislike for one’s companion what one dislikes for oneself.
Regarding His saying, {“and those whom your right hands possess”}, it means slaves—that is, treating them with kindness.

12153 — Al-Maʿrūr ibn Suwayd said:
“I met Abū Dharr at al-Rabadhah. He was wearing a suit of clothing, and his servant was also wearing a suit of clothing. I asked him about that, and he said:
‘I once exchanged insults with a man and reproached him because of his mother. The Prophet ﷺ said to me:
“O Abū Dharr! Did you reproach him because of his mother? You are indeed a man in whom there remains a trait of Jāhiliyyah. Your servants are your brothers whom Allāh has placed under your authority. Whoever has his brother under his authority should feed him from what he himself eats and clothe him from what he himself wears. Do not burden them with what overwhelms them; and if you do burden them, then help them.”’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Īmān (30) and Muslim in al-Aymān (1661:40), both through Shuʿbah, from Wāṣil al-Aḥdab, from al-Maʿrūr, who narrated it. The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
The phrase “khawalukum” means: your servants.
And regarding His saying:
{“Indeed, Allāh does not love anyone who is self-conceited and boastful.”} [Qurʾān 4:36]
The Words of Allah: “Allah Does Not Love Anyone Who Is Arrogant and Boastful”

12154 — Muṭarrif narrated:
A hadith from Abū Dharr used to reach me, and I longed to meet him. When I met him, I said:
“Abū Dharr, I have been informed that you claim the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told you that Allah loves three people and hates three.”
He replied:
“Yes. Do you think I would tell three lies against my beloved companion?”
I asked:
“Who are the three whom He hates?”
He replied:
“The arrogant boaster. Do you not find this in the Book of Allah revealed among you?”
He then recited:
“Indeed, Allah does not love anyone who is arrogant and boastful.”
I asked:
“Who else?”
He replied:
“The arrogant person who continually reminds others of what he has given them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/950).

12155 — A man from Balhujaym narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, to what are you calling?”
He replied:
“I call to Allah alone—the One Who, if harm touches you and you call upon Him, removes it from you; the One Who, if you become lost in a barren land and call upon Him, returns you to the way; and the One Who, if drought afflicts you and you call upon Him, causes vegetation to grow for you.”
I said:
“Then advise me.”
He said:
“Never insult anyone. Never regard any good deed as insignificant, even if it is only meeting your brother with a cheerful face or pouring water from your bucket into the vessel of someone seeking water.
Wear your lower garment to the middle of your shin; but if you refuse, then wear it down to the ankles.
Beware of letting the lower garment hang beneath the ankles, for letting it hang low is a form of arrogance, and Allah does not love arrogance.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20636).
The unnamed Companion in this report was Jābir ibn Sulaym, as identified in the preceding chain of transmission.

12156 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah loves to see the effect of His blessing upon His servant.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2819).
The word disbeliever in the words:
“We have prepared a humiliating punishment for the disbelievers”
may here mean one who conceals and covers the blessing of Allah, rather than a disbeliever who has left the religion, because failing to display Allah’s blessing does not remove a person from Islam.
Alternatively, the concealment may refer to the Jews and Christians hiding the knowledge they possessed concerning the prophethood of the Prophet ﷺ. In that case, disbelief bears its usual meaning as the opposite of Islam.

12157 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the lengthy hadith of intercession:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say: ‘Go, and whoever you find in his heart the weight of a dīnār of faith, bring him out.’”
Abū Saʿīd said:
“If you do not believe me, then recite:
‘Indeed, Allah does not wrong even by the weight of an atom. If there is a good deed, He multiplies it.’”
In another narration:
“Bring out whoever has in his heart the weight of a mustard seed of faith.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7439) and Muslim (183).
The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī. Muslim’s wording states:
“Whoever you find in his heart the weight of an atom of goodness.”

12158 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“Recite the Qur’an to me.”
I asked:
“Should I recite it to you when it was revealed to you?”
He replied:
“I desire to hear it from someone other than myself.”
I recited Sūrat al-Nisāʾ until I reached:
“How will it be when We bring a witness from every nation and bring you as a witness over these?”
I raised my head—or a man beside me nudged me, so I raised my head—and saw the tears of the Prophet ﷺ flowing.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5055) and Muslim (800).

12159 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated:
Before wine was prohibited, a man from the Anṣār invited us. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf went forward and led them in the sunset prayer.
He recited:
“Say: O disbelievers…”
but became confused in its recitation.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Do not approach prayer while you are intoxicated.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3671), al-Tirmidhī (3026), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/958), and al-Ḥākim (4/142).

12160 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
