Four verses were revealed concerning me. He then mentioned among them:
I came upon a group of the Anṣār and the Emigrants. They said:
“Come, and we will feed you and give you wine to drink.”
This occurred before wine was prohibited.
I went to them in an orchard. They had a roasted camel’s head and a skin filled with wine.
I ate and drank with them. The Anṣār and the Emigrants were mentioned, and I said:
“The Emigrants are better than the Anṣār.”
A man took one of the jawbones from the camel’s head and struck me with it, injuring my nose.
I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed concerning me and the matter of wine:
“Wine, gambling, stone altars, and divining arrows are only filth from the work of Satan.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1748/43).

12161 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
When the initial ruling concerning wine was revealed, ʿUmar said:
“O Allah, give us a clear and decisive ruling concerning wine.”
The verse in Sūrat al-Baqarah was then revealed:
“They ask you about wine and gambling. Say: In them is great sin…”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:219]
ʿUmar was summoned, and the verse was recited to him. He said:
“O Allah, give us a clear and decisive ruling concerning wine.”
The verse in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ was then revealed:
“O you who believe, do not approach prayer while you are intoxicated.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:43]
Thereafter, whenever the prayer was established, the caller of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would announce:
“No intoxicated person is to approach the prayer.”
ʿUmar was summoned, and the verse was recited to him. He again said:
“O Allah, give us a clear and decisive ruling concerning wine.”
Then the verse was revealed:
“Will you not then desist?”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:91]
ʿUmar said:
“We have desisted.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3669), al-Tirmidhī (3039), al-Nasāʾī (5540), Aḥmad (378), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (4/143).
The words:
“Nor while in a state of major ritual impurity, except when merely passing through, until you bathe”
mean that a person in a state of major ritual impurity may not remain inside the mosque. There is no harm, however, in passing through it.

12162 — Abū Dharr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Clean earth is the purification of a Muslim, even if he does not find water for ten years. When he finds water, let him apply it to his skin, for that is better.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (332), al-Tirmidhī (124), al-Nasāʾī (322), and Aḥmad (21568).

12163 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn al-Khuzāʿī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw a man standing apart who had not prayed with the people.
He asked:
“So-and-so, what prevented you from praying with the people?”
The man replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I became ritually impure and there was no water.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Use clean earth, for it is sufficient for you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (344) and Muslim (682).

12164 — The Mother of the Believers, ʿĀʾishah, narrated:
We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on one of his journeys. When we reached al-Baydāʾ or Dhāt al-Jaysh, one of my necklaces broke and was lost.
She then narrated the incident, which included the following:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ slept until morning while no water was available. Allah, Blessed and Exalted, then revealed the verse of dry ablution, and the people performed tayammum.
Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr said:
“This is not the first blessing you have brought, family of Abū Bakr.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
We then made the camel I had been riding stand, and we found the necklace beneath it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Purification (92), al-Bukhārī (334), and Muslim (367).

12165 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever dies while associating anything with Allah will enter the Fire.”
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“And I say: Whoever dies without associating anything with Allah will enter Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1238) and Muslim (92).

12166 — Muʿāwiyah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah may forgive every sin except that of a man who dies as a disbeliever, or a man who deliberately kills a believer.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (7/81), Aḥmad (16907), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (4/351).

12167 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
We used to refrain from seeking forgiveness for those who committed major sins until we heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite:
“Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.”
He also said:
“I have reserved my supplication as an intercession for those among my nation who committed major sins.”
Ibn ʿUmar said:
“We then abandoned many of the severe thoughts we had held within ourselves, began speaking with hope, and hoped for them.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (5840), Abū Yaʿlā (5813), and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (854).

12168 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The molar tooth—or canine tooth—of a disbeliever will be like Mount Uḥud, and the thickness of his skin will equal a journey of three days.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2851).
The words:
“We will admit them into deep and abundant shade”
mean a vast, deep, and continuous shade.

12169 — Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is a tree in Paradise beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2827) and al-Bukhārī (6552).

12170 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is a tree in Paradise beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2826).
Another narration through a different chain adds:
“Without crossing it.”

12171 — Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered Makkah and the people had become settled and secure, he went to the Sacred House and circumambulated it seven times while mounted, touching the Corner with a hooked staff in his hand.
When he completed his circumambulation, he summoned ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah and took from him the key of the Kaʿbah. It was opened for him, and he entered.
Inside, he found a wooden image of a dove. He broke it with his hand and cast it away. He then stood at the door of the Kaʿbah while the people had gathered around him in the mosque.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sat in the mosque. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib approached him holding the key of the Kaʿbah and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless you, combine for us the custodianship of the Kaʿbah with the duty of providing water to the pilgrims.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Where is ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah?”
He was summoned, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Take your key, ʿUthmān. Today is a day of righteousness and faithfulness.”
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as recorded in the Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/411).

12172 — Abū Yūnus Sulaym ibn Jubayr, the freedman of Abū Hurayrah, narrated:
I heard Abū Hurayrah recite the verse:
“Indeed, Allah commands you to return trusts to their rightful owners…”
until the words:
“All-Hearing, All-Seeing.”
He said:
“I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ place his thumb upon his ear and the finger beside it upon his eye.”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite the verse while placing his two fingers in this manner.”
Ibn Yūnus said that al-Muqriʾ explained:
“The words, ‘Indeed, Allah is All-Hearing, All-Seeing,’ mean that Allah truly possesses hearing and sight.”
Abū Dāwūd said:
“This is a refutation of the Jahmiyyah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4728) and al-Ḥākim (1/24).

12173 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Obey Allah, obey the Messenger, and those in authority among you”:
This verse was revealed concerning ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah ibn Qays ibn ʿAdī when the Prophet ﷺ appointed him as commander of a military detachment.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4584) and Muslim (1834).

12174 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Burdah was a soothsayer who judged between the Jews in the disputes they brought before him.
Some people from the tribe of Aslam brought a dispute to him, so Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Have you not seen those who claim that they believe…”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/295) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/991).

12175 — Yazīd ibn al-Aswad narrated:
We performed the Farewell Pilgrimage with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in the morning prayer. He then turned while seated and faced the people. He saw two men behind the congregation who had not prayed with the people and said:
“Bring those two men to me.”
They were brought to him with their bodies trembling.
He asked:
“What prevented you from praying with the people?”
They replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, we had already prayed at our camp.”
He said:
“Do not do that. When one of you has prayed at his camp and then reaches the congregational prayer with the imam, he should pray with him, for it will count as a voluntary prayer for him.”
One of the two men said:
“Seek forgiveness for me, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ sought forgiveness for him.
The people then rushed toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I went with them. At that time, I was among the youngest and strongest of the men.
I continued pushing through the people until I reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. I took his hand and placed it upon either my face or my chest.
I had never felt anything more fragrant or cooler than the hand of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
This occurred in the Mosque of al-Khayf.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17476), whose wording is cited here, Abū Dāwūd (575), al-Tirmidhī (219), and al-Nasāʾī (858).

12176 — ʿUrwah narrated:
Al-Zubayr disputed with a man from the Anṣār concerning an irrigation channel in the stony land of Madinah.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Zubayr, irrigate your land, then release the water to your neighbor.”
The Anṣārī said:
“O Messenger of Allah, is this because he is your cousin?”
The face of the Prophet ﷺ changed color, and he then said:
“Zubayr, irrigate your land and retain the water until it reaches the boundary wall, then release it to your neighbor.”
The Prophet ﷺ then granted al-Zubayr his complete legal right after the Anṣārī angered him. Before that, he had suggested a lenient arrangement that provided ease for both of them.
Al-Zubayr said:
“I believe that these verses were revealed concerning that incident:
‘No, by your Lord, they will not truly believe until they make you judge in whatever dispute arises between them.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4585), as well as (2359–2360), and Muslim (2357).

12177 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No prophet becomes ill except that he is given the choice between this world and the Hereafter.”
During the illness in which he died, he became extremely hoarse. I heard him say:
“With those upon whom Allah has bestowed His favor: the prophets, the steadfast affirmers of truth, the martyrs, and the righteous.”
I then knew that he had been given the choice.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4586) and Muslim (2444/86).

12178 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, you are dearer to me than my own self. You are dearer to me than my family and dearer to me than my children.
I may be sitting in my home and remember you, but I cannot remain patient until I come and look at you.
When I remember that both you and I will die, I know that when you enter Paradise, you will be raised among the prophets. If I enter Paradise, I fear that I will not see you.”
The Prophet ﷺ did not answer him until Gabriel descended with the verse:
“Whoever obeys Allah and the Messenger will be with those upon whom Allah has bestowed His favor: the prophets and the steadfast affirmers of truth…”
until the end of the verse.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (480) and Abū Nuʿaym in Ḥilyat al-Awliyāʾ (4/239–240).

12179 — Rabīʿah ibn Kaʿb al-Aslamī narrated:
I used to spend the night near the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and bring him the water for his ablution and whatever else he needed.
He said to me:
“Ask.”
I replied:
“I ask you for your companionship in Paradise.”
He asked:
“Anything other than that?”
I replied:
“That is what I desire.”
He said:
“Then help me regarding yourself by performing many prostrations.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (489).
The words:
“Help me regarding yourself by performing many prostrations”
mean that if you desire to accompany me in Paradise, then increase your prayers.
Accompanying the Prophet ﷺ does not require equality in rank, so the hadith does not need to be interpreted away from its apparent meaning.
It also provides evidence for those who hold that performing a greater number of bowings and prostrations is better than prolonging individual bowings and prostrations.

12180 — Ḥudhayfah narrated:
On the night of the Battle of the Confederates, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Is there a man who will bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?”
He repeated this three times, but no one responded.
He then said:
“Stand, Ḥudhayfah, and bring us news of the enemy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1788).

12181 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“My mother and I were among the oppressed. I was among the children, and my mother was among the women.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1357).

12182 — Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
Ibn ʿAbbās recited:
“Except the oppressed among men, women, and children.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:98]
He then said:
“My mother and I were among those whom Allah excused.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4588).

12183 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf and some of his companions came to the Prophet ﷺ in Makkah and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we were powerful and honored when we were polytheists, but after we believed, we became humiliated.”
He replied:
“I have been commanded to pardon and refrain.”
When Allah moved us to Madinah, we were commanded to fight, but they then held back. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Have you not seen those who were told, ‘Restrain your hands, establish prayer, and give zakāh’? Yet when fighting was prescribed for them, a group of them began fearing people…”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3086), al-Ḥākim (2/66–67), al-Bayhaqī through him (9/11), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/1005).
Concerning the words:
“Then a group of them began fearing people as they should fear Allah, or even more intensely, and said: ‘Our Lord, why have You prescribed fighting for us? If only You had delayed us for a short time.’”
This was the statement of some among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ who had initially desired that fighting be prescribed. When it was made obligatory, however, it became difficult for them, and they feared having to fight the people.
They said:
“Why have You prescribed fighting for us? If only You had delayed us for a short time.”
Meaning: until they died naturally upon their beds.
Allah, the Exalted, reproached them and admonished them that the enjoyment of this world is little and that the Hereafter is better for them.

12184 — Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A place in Paradise equal to the length of one of your whips is better than the world and everything within it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2892) and Muslim (1881). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12185 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever obeys me has obeyed Allah, and whoever disobeys me has disobeyed Allah. Whoever obeys the commander I appoint has obeyed me, and whoever disobeys the commander I appoint has disobeyed me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7137) and Muslim (1835/33).

12186 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb narrated from his father, from his grandfather:
My brother and I once attended a gathering that I would not exchange for the finest red camels.
My brother and I approached and found several elderly Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sitting near one of his doors. We disliked separating them, so we sat to one side.
They mentioned a verse of the Qur’an and began disputing about it until their voices rose.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out angrily, his face having become red. He threw dust toward them and said:
“Take it easy, people. This is how the nations before you were destroyed: by disagreeing with their prophets and setting parts of their scriptures against other parts.
The Qur’an was not revealed so that some of it would contradict other parts. Rather, its parts confirm one another. Whatever you understand from it, act upon it, and whatever you do not understand, refer it to the One Who knows it.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6702).
The Prophet ﷺ also warned against disputing over the Noble Qur’an, as stated in the following hadith.

12187 — Abū ʿImrān al-Jawnī narrated:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Rabāḥ al-Anṣārī wrote to me that ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr said:
I came early to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ one day.
He heard the voices of two men disputing over a verse. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out to us, and anger could be seen upon his face. He said:
“Those before you were destroyed only because of their disagreements concerning the Scripture.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2666).

12188 — Abū Mūsā narrated:
Whenever someone came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ seeking assistance, he would turn toward those sitting with him and say:
“Intercede, and you will be rewarded. Allah will decree through the tongue of His Prophet whatever He wills.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1432) and Muslim (2627).
Mujāhid said:
“This verse was revealed concerning people interceding for one another.”
The word:
“Muqīt”
means one who watches over and preserves all things.
It has also been derived from qūt, meaning sustenance. According to this interpretation, Allah alone is the Sustainer and Provider, while intercession is merely a means.

12189 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Peace be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ returned his greeting, and the man sat down. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Ten.”
Another man came and said:
“Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ returned his greeting, and he sat down. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Twenty.”
Another man came and said:
“Peace, the mercy of Allah, and His blessings be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ returned his greeting, and the man sat down. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Thirty.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5195), al-Tirmidhī (2689), Aḥmad (19948), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa al-Laylah (337).

12190 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man passed by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was sitting in a gathering and said:
“Peace be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Ten good deeds.”
Another man passed and said:
“Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Twenty good deeds.”
Another man passed and said:
“Peace, the mercy of Allah, and His blessings be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Thirty good deeds.”
A man then stood up from the gathering without giving the greeting of peace. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“How quickly your companion has forgotten! When one of you comes to a gathering, let him give the greeting of peace. If he then wishes to sit, let him sit. When he rises to leave, let him also give the greeting of peace, for the first greeting is no more deserving than the last.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (986) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (493).

12191 — Zayd ibn Thābit narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ set out for Uḥud, some people who had accompanied him turned back.
One group of Muslims said:
“We should kill them.”
Another group said:
“We should not kill them.”
The following verse was then revealed:
“Why are you divided into two groups concerning the hypocrites?”
The Prophet ﷺ said concerning Madinah:
“It expels corrupt people just as fire removes impurities from iron.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1884) and Muslim (1384). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The words:
“Allah has cast them back because of what they earned”
mean that Allah caused them to fall back into error. It has also been interpreted as meaning that He destroyed them.
The words:
“Because of what they earned”
refer to their disobedience and opposition to the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.

12192 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
The people of Kūfah disagreed concerning a verse, so I traveled to Ibn ʿAbbās and asked him about it.
He replied:
“The verse:
‘Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell…’
was the last verse revealed, and nothing abrogated it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4590) and Muslim (3023).

12193 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abzā instructed me:
“Ask Ibn ʿAbbās about these two verses and how they are to be understood:
‘Do not kill the soul that Allah has made sacred’ [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:33],
and:
‘Whoever deliberately kills a believer…’”
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās, and he replied:
When the verse in Sūrat al-Furqān was revealed:
“Those who do not invoke another deity with Allah, do not kill the soul that Allah has made sacred except by legal right, and do not commit fornication. Whoever does that will meet a penalty.”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]
the polytheists of Makkah said:
“We have killed souls that Allah made sacred, invoked another god with Allah, and committed immoral acts.”
Allah then revealed:
“Except those who repent and believe…”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:70]
That verse applies to those people.
As for the verse in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ:
“Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining therein, and Allah is angry with him, has cursed him, and has prepared for him a tremendous punishment,”
it concerns a man who learns Islam and its laws and then deliberately kills.
When I mentioned this to Mujāhid, he said:
“Except the one who sincerely regrets and repents.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3855).

12194 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abzā instructed me to ask Ibn ʿAbbās about the two verses.
Regarding:
“Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining therein,”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Nothing abrogated it.”
Regarding:
“Those who do not invoke another deity with Allah and do not kill the soul that Allah has made sacred except by legal right…”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]
he said:
“It was revealed concerning the polytheists.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4766) and Muslim (3023/18). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12195 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Is there repentance for someone who deliberately kills a believer?”
He replied:
“No.”
I then recited to him the verse in Sūrat al-Furqān:
“Those who do not invoke another deity with Allah and do not kill the soul that Allah has made sacred except by legal right…”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]
until the end of the passage.
He replied:
“That is a Makkan verse. It was abrogated by the Madinan verse:
‘Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining therein.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4762) and Muslim (3023/20). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12196 — Zayd ibn Thābit narrated:
When the verse in Sūrat al-Furqān was revealed:
“Those who do not invoke another deity with Allah and do not kill the soul that Allah has made sacred except by legal right…”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]
we were amazed by its leniency.
Six months later, the verse in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ was revealed:
“Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining therein.”
That is, the severe verse was revealed after the lenient verse and abrogated it. By the severe verse, he meant the verse in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ, and by the lenient verse, the verse in Sūrat al-Furqān.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (7/87), Abū Dāwūd (4272), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (5/136–137).
The majority of earlier and later scholars held that a murderer may repent in what is between him and his Lord, Mighty and Majestic.
If he repents, returns to Allah, humbles and submits himself, and performs righteous deeds, Allah will replace his evil deeds with good deeds. Allah will also compensate the murdered person for the wrong committed against him and satisfy him concerning his claim.
This was stated by Ibn Kathīr.
Allah, the Exalted, says:
“Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills. Whoever associates partners with Allah has indeed fabricated a tremendous sin.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:48]
This verse is general and includes every sin other than shirk.
As for the noble verse:
“Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining therein, and Allah is angry with him, has cursed him, and has prepared for him a tremendous punishment,”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“That is his deserved recompense if Allah chooses to punish him accordingly.”
Ibn Abī Ḥātim mentioned this in his Tafsīr.
The word:
“Remaining therein”
means remaining for an extremely long period, because recurrently transmitted hadiths establish that everlasting residence in the Fire applies only to a polytheist or disbeliever.

12197 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some Muslims encountered a man who was tending a small flock of sheep.
He said:
“Peace be upon you.”
They seized him, killed him, and took his flock.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Do not say to one who offers you peace, ‘You are not a believer.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4591) and Muslim (3025).

12198 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ḥadrad narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us toward Iḍam. I set out with a group of Muslims that included Abū Qatādah al-Ḥārith ibn Ribʿī and Muḥallim ibn Jaththāmah ibn Qays.
When we reached the valley of Iḍam, ʿĀmir al-Ashjaʿī passed us while riding a young camel. He had some personal belongings and a leather container of milk with him.
When he passed us, he greeted us with peace, so we refrained from harming him.
Muḥallim ibn Jaththāmah then attacked and killed him because of a previous matter between them. He also took his camel and belongings.
When we returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what had happened, the following Qur’anic verse was revealed concerning us:
“O you who believe, when you go forth in the cause of Allah, investigate carefully. Do not say to one who offers you peace, ‘You are not a believer,’ seeking the temporary gains of worldly life, for with Allah are abundant spoils. You yourselves were like that before, then Allah bestowed His favor upon you. Therefore, investigate carefully. Allah is fully aware of what you do.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23881), Ibn Abī Shaybah (38168), and Ibn al-Jārūd (777).

12199 — Al-Barāʾ narrated:
When the verse:
“Those believers who remain behind are not equal…”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Zayd and had him write it down.
Ibn Umm Maktūm then came and complained of his disability, so Allah revealed:
“Except those with disabilities.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4593) and Muslim (1898).
The words:
“Except those with disabilities”
refer to any deficiency or incapacity, whether blindness, lameness, illness, or another condition that renders a person unable to participate.

12200 — Zayd ibn Thābit narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dictated to me:
“Those believers who remain behind are not equal to those who strive in the cause of Allah.”
Ibn Umm Maktūm came while the Prophet ﷺ was dictating it to me and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, if I were able to fight, I would certainly do so.”
He was blind.
Revelation then descended upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while his thigh was resting upon mine. It became so heavy that I feared it would crush my thigh.
When the revelation was lifted from him, Allah had revealed:
“Except those with disabilities.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4592) and Muslim (1898).

12201 — Miqsam, the freedman of ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ḥārith, narrated that Ibn ʿAbbās informed him:
The words:
“Those believers who remained behind are not equal…”
refer to those who remained behind from Badr and those who went out to participate at Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4595).

12202 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Those believers who remain behind—except those with disabilities—are not equal…”
They were people during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who did not accompany him in battle because of sicknesses, illnesses, and bodily pain, while others were healthy but still did not accompany him.
Those who were ill were excused, unlike those who were healthy.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12/165).

12203 — Al-Faltān ibn ʿĀṣim narrated:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ when revelation descended upon him. Whenever revelation came to him, his gaze would become fixed while his eyes remained open, and he would devote his hearing and heart completely to what came to him from Allah.
We recognized this condition in him.
He said to the scribe:
“Write: ‘Those believers who remain behind are not equal to those who strive in the cause of Allah.’”
A blind man stood and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is our fault?”
We said to the blind man:
“Revelation is descending upon the Prophet ﷺ.”
He feared that something concerning him might be revealed, so he remained standing and said:
“I seek refuge in Allah from the anger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“Write: ‘Except those with disabilities.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1583), Ibn Ḥibbān through him (4712), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (18/280).
The words:
“Above those who remain behind by one degree”
refer to believers who remained behind because of valid excuses. They did not strive with their wealth and lives, but they possessed sincere intention, so they receive the reward of that intention.
Those who actually strove are therefore raised one degree above them.
The words:
“Allah has favored those who strive over those who remain behind with a tremendous reward”
refer to those who remained behind without a valid excuse. This interpretation prevents repetition in the verse.
The words:
“Allah has promised the best reward to each”
refer to both those who strive and those who remain behind because of disability.
The best reward means Paradise.

12204 — Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
A military expedition was imposed upon the people of Madinah, and I enrolled in it.
I met ʿIkrimah, the freedman of Ibn ʿAbbās, and informed him. He strongly forbade me from joining it.
He then said:
Ibn ʿAbbās informed me that some Muslims remained among the polytheists during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and increased the apparent size of the polytheist army.
An arrow would be shot and strike one of them, killing him, or he would be struck and killed.
Allah then revealed:
“Indeed, those whose souls the angels take while they have wronged themselves…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4596).

12205 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some people in Makkah accepted Islam but concealed their faith.
When the polytheists marched to Badr, they forced these Muslims to accompany them. Some of them were killed at Badr while among the polytheists.
The Muslims said:
“These companions of ours were Muslims and were forced to go out. Seek forgiveness for them.”
The following verse was then revealed:
“Indeed, those whose souls the angels take while they have wronged themselves…”
The Muslims wrote this verse to those who remained in Makkah.
They set out to emigrate, but while they were partway along the road, the polytheists discovered their departure, pursued them, and forced them to return.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Among the people are those who say, ‘We believe in Allah,’ but when they are harmed for the sake of Allah, they regard the persecution of people as though it were the punishment of Allah.”
 [Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:10]
The Muslims wrote this verse to them, and they became deeply saddened.
Allah then revealed:
“Then indeed your Lord, toward those who emigrated after being persecuted, then strove and remained patient—indeed, your Lord after that is Forgiving and Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:110]
The Muslims wrote this verse to them as well.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2204).

12206 — Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
Ibn ʿAbbās recited:
“Except the oppressed among men, women, and children.”
He then said:
“My mother and I were among those whom Allah excused.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4588).

12207 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
While the Prophet ﷺ was leading the ʿIshāʾ prayer, he said:
“Allah hears whoever praises Him.”
Then, before prostrating, he said:
“O Allah, rescue ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Rabīʿah. O Allah, rescue Salamah ibn Hishām. O Allah, rescue al-Walīd ibn al-Walīd. O Allah, rescue the oppressed believers. O Allah, intensify Your punishment upon Muḍar. O Allah, make their years like the years of Joseph.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4598) and Muslim (675/295). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12208 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The verse:
“Indeed, those whose souls the angels take while they have wronged themselves…”
was revealed.
There was a man in Makkah named Ḍamrah from Banū Bakr. He was ill and said to his family:
“Take me out of Makkah, for I am suffering from its heat.”
They asked:
“Where should we take you?”
He pointed with his hand toward Madinah.
The following verse was then revealed:
“Whoever emigrates in the cause of Allah will find throughout the earth many places of refuge…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/1050).

12209 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
“Allah prescribed prayer through the tongue of your Prophet as four rakʿahs while residing, two rakʿahs while traveling, and one rakʿah during a state of fear.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (687).
In another narration:
“Allah prescribed prayer through the tongue of your Prophet as two rakʿahs for the traveler, four for the resident, and one during fear.”
The words:
“If you fear that the disbelievers may harm you”
describe the usual circumstance at that time. Shortening the prayer remains permissible during safety just as it is permissible during fear.
For this reason, the wording caused some of the Companions to ask about the matter.

12210 — Yaʿlā ibn Umayyah narrated:
I said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb:
“Allah says:
‘There is no blame upon you for shortening the prayer if you fear that the disbelievers may harm you,’
yet the people are now safe.”
He replied:
“I was surprised by the same thing that surprised you, so I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about it. He said:
‘It is a charity that Allah has bestowed upon you, so accept His charity.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (686).

12211 — Abū Ḥanẓalah narrated:
I asked Ibn ʿUmar about prayer during travel.
He replied:
“Prayer during travel is two rakʿahs.”
We said:
“But we are safe.”
He replied:
“It is the Sunnah of the Prophet ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4704).

12212 — Abū ʿAyyāsh al-Zuraqī narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at ʿUsfān when the polytheists faced us under the command of Khālid ibn al-Walīd. They were positioned between us and the direction of prayer.
The Prophet ﷺ led us in the Ẓuhr prayer.
The polytheists said:
“They were in a condition in which we could have attacked them by surprise.”
They then said:
“A prayer will soon come upon them that is dearer to them than their children and their own lives.”
Gabriel, peace be upon him, then descended with these verses between the Ẓuhr and ʿAṣr prayers:
“When you are among them and lead them in prayer…”
When the time for prayer arrived, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered them to carry their weapons.
We formed two rows behind him.
He bowed, and all of us bowed. He then raised his head, and all of us raised our heads.
The Prophet ﷺ then prostrated with the row immediately behind him, while the other row remained standing and guarded them.
When the first row completed its prostration and stood, the other row sat and prostrated in its place.
The two rows then exchanged places.
The Prophet ﷺ bowed, and they all bowed with him.
The Prophet ﷺ then prostrated with the row immediately behind him, while the others stood guarding them.
When the Prophet ﷺ sat, the other row sat and then prostrated.
He then concluded the prayer by giving salām to all of them and departed.
He said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the prayer in this manner twice: once at ʿUsfān and once in the land of Banū Sulaym.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1236), al-Nasāʾī (3/177–178), and Aḥmad (16580). The wording cited here is that of Aḥmad. It was also authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2876) and al-Ḥākim (1/337–338).
The Prayer of Fear has many forms because the enemy may sometimes be positioned in the direction of prayer and at other times in another direction.
At times, the Muslims perform it in congregation, while at other times they do not pray in congregation but pray facing the direction of prayer while on foot or mounted.
Many hadiths have been narrated concerning its various forms, as previously mentioned in the section on the Prayer of Fear.
Imām Aḥmad said:
“Every hadith narrated concerning the forms of the Prayer of Fear may be acted upon. Six or seven different forms have been reported.”
The words:
“There is no blame upon you, if you are troubled by rain or are ill, for putting down your weapons, but remain cautious”
indicate, according to the apparent meaning of the verse, that carrying weapons during the Prayer of Fear is obligatory.
This is one of the two reported positions of al-Shāfiʿī.
Allah granted permission to put down the weapons in cases of rain or illness.

12213 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“If you are troubled by rain or are ill”:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf was wounded.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4599).

12214 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to remember Allah in all of his circumstances.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (373).
The words:
“Indeed, prayer has been prescribed for the believers at appointed times”
mean that it is an obligation, as stated by Ibn ʿAbbās.
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Prayer has appointed times just as pilgrimage has an appointed time.”

12215 — Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Shall I inform you of something better in rank than fasting, prayer, and charity?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Reconciling people, for corruption between people is the shaver.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4919), al-Tirmidhī (2509), Aḥmad (27508), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5092).

12216 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Beware of corrupted relations between people, for they are the shaver.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2508).
The words:
“Corrupted relations between people”
refer to hostility and hatred.
The expression:
“The shaver”
means that it shaves away and destroys religion.

12217 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah will never unite my nation”—or he said: “this nation”—“upon misguidance. The hand of Allah is with the united body.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/115).
Whoever opposes what the believers have unanimously agreed upon, whether in belief or practice, exposes himself to severe punishment, as stated in the verse.

12218 — ʿAmr ibn Maymūn narrated:
When Muʿādh arrived in Yemen, he led the people in the dawn prayer and recited:
“Allah took Abraham as an intimate friend.”
A man among the people said:
“The eye of Abraham’s mother has certainly been comforted.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4348).

12219 — ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words of Allah:
“They ask you for a ruling concerning women. Say: Allah gives you a ruling concerning them and concerning what is recited to you in the Book about orphan girls to whom you do not give what has been prescribed for them, while you desire to marry them…”
This refers to a man who has an orphan girl under his guardianship. He is both her guardian and heir, and she shares his property with him, even down to a date-palm cluster.
He desires to marry her but dislikes allowing another man to marry her, because that man would then share in the property that she already shares with him.
He therefore prevents her from marrying, and this verse was revealed concerning that practice.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4600) and Muslim (3018/7). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12220 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The people later asked for a ruling, so the following verse was revealed:
“They ask you for a ruling concerning women. Say: Allah gives you a ruling concerning them and concerning what is recited to you in the Book…”
She said:
“What Allah mentioned as being recited to them in the Book is the earlier verse in which Allah says:
‘If you fear that you will not deal justly with the orphan girls, then marry whatever women are lawful and pleasing to you.’”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:3]
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (4/1076).

12221 — ʿUrwah asked ʿĀʾishah concerning the words of Allah:
“If you fear that you will not deal justly with the orphan girls, then marry whatever women are lawful and pleasing to you—two, three, or four. But if you fear that you will not act justly, then marry only one or those whom your right hands possess. That is more likely to prevent injustice.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:3]
She replied:
“Son of my sister, this refers to an orphan girl under the guardianship of a man. He desires her wealth and beauty and wishes to marry her while giving her less than the customary bridal gift appropriate for women like her.
They were therefore forbidden from marrying orphan girls unless they treated them justly and completed their proper bridal gifts. They were commanded instead to marry other women.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5064) and Muslim (3018/6). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The words:
“And that you uphold justice for the orphans”
include complete justice toward an orphan girl. If she possesses wealth and beauty, her guardian should choose for her a husband who is better suited than himself.
If she lacks beauty and wealth and he nevertheless wishes to marry her fairly, then he has a stronger claim to do so.
The meaning of the two verses is that during the pre-Islamic period, when people had a wealthy and beautiful orphan girl under their guardianship, they would marry her without giving her a proper bridal gift.
If she possessed wealth but lacked beauty, the guardian would neither marry her himself nor allow another man to marry her. He would keep her unmarried until she died so that he could inherit her property.
Islam came and abolished this injustice against orphan girls.
Islam commanded that if she possessed wealth and beauty and her guardian wished to marry her, he must give her a bridal gift equal to that given to comparable women.
If he did not wish to marry her, he was not permitted to prevent her from marrying another man merely because he desired to inherit her wealth.

12222 — The Mother of the Believers, ʿĀʾishah, said concerning the words of Allah:
“If a woman fears ill-treatment or aversion from her husband…”
This refers to a man who has a wife but no longer greatly desires her and wishes to separate from her.
She says to him:
“I release you from some of my rights concerning me.”
The verse was revealed concerning such a situation.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4601) and Muslim (3021).
Al-Bukhārī (2694) narrated through another route from Hishām with this chain that she said:
“It refers to a man who sees something in his wife that he dislikes, such as old age or something else, and wishes to separate from her.
She says: ‘Keep me as your wife and assign me whatever share of your time you wish.’”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“There is no harm in this if they mutually agree.”
The expression:
“He does not greatly desire her”
means that he lacks strong desire for her in love, companionship, intimacy, and similar matters.

12223 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When Sawdah bint Zamʿah grew old, she gave her allotted day to ʿĀʾishah.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would therefore assign to ʿĀʾishah both her own day and Sawdah’s day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5212) and Muslim (1463).

12224 — ʿUrwah narrated that ʿĀʾishah said:
“Son of my sister, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not favor one of us over another in dividing the time he spent with us.
Rarely would a day pass without him visiting all of us. He would approach each wife without having intimate relations with her until he reached the wife whose allotted day it was, and he would spend the night with her.
When Sawdah bint Zamʿah grew old and feared that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ might separate from her, she said:
‘O Messenger of Allah, I give my day to ʿĀʾishah.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted that from her.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“We believe that concerning this and similar cases Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
‘If a woman fears ill-treatment or aversion from her husband…’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2135).

12225 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Sawdah feared that the Prophet ﷺ might divorce her, so she said:
“Do not divorce me. Keep me as your wife and give my allotted day to ʿĀʾishah.”
He did so, and the following verse was revealed:
“If a woman fears ill-treatment or aversion from her husband, there is no blame upon them if they reach a reconciliation between themselves, and reconciliation is better.”
Whatever mutually acceptable agreement the spouses reach is permissible.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3040).

12226 — Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj said concerning the words of Allah:
“If a woman fears ill-treatment from her husband…”
A man had an elderly wife and then married a younger woman. He favored the younger wife over her, but his first wife refused to accept that arrangement, so he pronounced one divorce against her.
When only a short time remained of her waiting period, he said:
“I may take you back if you are willing to endure my favoring her over you. Otherwise, I will leave you until your waiting period ends.”
She replied:
“Take me back, and I will patiently accept the preference shown to her.”
He took her back but continued favoring the younger wife. She was unable to endure it, so he divorced her again.
When only a short time remained of her waiting period, he repeated what he had said before.
She replied:
“Take me back, and I will be patient.”
Rāfiʿ said:
“This is the reconciliation concerning which we were informed that Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
‘If a woman fears ill-treatment or aversion from her husband, there is no blame upon them if they reach a reconciliation between themselves, and reconciliation is better.’”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq and, through his route, by al-Ḥākim (2/308).

12227 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever has two wives and inclines unjustly toward one of them will come on the Day of Resurrection with one side of his body leaning.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2133), al-Tirmidhī (1173), Ibn Mājah (1969), al-Nasāʾī (3942), and Aḥmad (7936), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4207) and al-Ḥākim (2/186).

12228 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The prayers heaviest upon the hypocrites are the ʿIshāʾ prayer and the Fajr prayer…” the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (657) and Muslim (650/252).

12229 — Al-ʿAlāʾ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
He entered the home of Anas ibn Mālik in Baṣrah shortly after returning from the Ẓuhr prayer. Anas’s home was beside the mosque.
When we entered, he asked:
“Have you prayed ʿAṣr?”
We replied:
“We have only just returned from the Ẓuhr prayer.”
He said:
“Then pray ʿAṣr.”
We stood and prayed.
When we finished, he said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“That is the prayer of the hypocrite. He sits watching the sun until it is between the two horns of Satan; then he stands and pecks out four rakʿahs, remembering Allah only a little.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (622).

12230 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The hypocrite is like a wandering ewe between two flocks, going to this one at one time and to the other at another time.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2784).
The word taʿīr means that it wanders back and forth.
Regarding the words:
“Belonging neither to these nor to those,”
Mujāhid said:
“They belong neither to the Companions of Muḥammad nor to those Jews.”

12231 — Al-Aswad narrated:
We were sitting in a circle with ʿAbdullāh when Ḥudhayfah came, stood before us, greeted us, and said:
“Hypocrisy was revealed concerning people who were better than you.”
Al-Aswad said:
“Glory be to Allah! Allah says:
‘Indeed, the hypocrites will be in the lowest depth of the Fire.’”
ʿAbdullāh smiled, and Ḥudhayfah sat in a corner of the mosque.
ʿAbdullāh then stood, and his companions dispersed. Ḥudhayfah threw small stones toward me, so I went to him.
He said:
“I was surprised at his laughter, although he understood what I meant. Hypocrisy was revealed concerning people who had previously been better than you, but they later repented, and Allah accepted their repentance.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4602).
The words:
“The lowest depth”
refer to chambers with doors that will be shut upon them, with fire kindled beneath them and above them.
This explanation has been narrated from Abū Hurayrah, Ibn Masʿūd, and others through authentic chains.
The words:
“Except those who repent, reform, hold firmly to Allah, and make their religion sincere for Allah”
mean that they replace showing off with sincerity and possess genuine faith within the heart, for hypocrisy is disbelief within the heart.

12232 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, the son of Mary will soon descend among you as a just ruler. He will break the cross, kill the swine, abolish the jizyah, and wealth will become so abundant that no one will accept it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2222) and Muslim (155).
In one narration, Abū Hurayrah then said:
“Recite, if you wish:
‘There is none from the People of the Scripture except that he will surely believe in him before his death, and on the Day of Resurrection he will be a witness against them.’”
In another narration:
“He will remain on the earth for forty years, then he will die, and the Muslims will perform the funeral prayer over him.”
All religious communities will then become one community upon upright Islam, the religion of Abraham, peace be upon him.
It has also been said that the pronoun in:
“Before his death”
refers to each individual from the People of the Scripture.
According to this interpretation, every individual from the People of the Scripture will believe in Jesus, peace be upon him, immediately before that individual’s death.
The first interpretation is more correct.

12233 — ʿUmar narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Do not exaggerate in praising me as the Christians exaggerated in praising the son of Mary. I am only His servant, so say: ‘The servant of Allah and His Messenger.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3445).

12234 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O best of creation.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“That was Abraham.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2369).

12235 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A man said:
“O Muḥammad, our master and son of our master, the best among us and son of the best among us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“People, remain mindful of Allah and do not allow Satan to lead you astray. I am Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh, the servant of Allah and His Messenger. By Allah, I do not wish you to raise me above the station in which Allah has placed me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12551) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6240).
The words:
“And His word”
mean that Allah created him through His command:
“Be.”
The words:
“Which He conveyed to Mary”
mean that the command was conveyed through Gabriel, and Jesus became a human being without a father.
The words:
“And a spirit from Him”
mean one of the spirits created by Allah. Its attribution to Allah is an attribution of honor and distinction.
It has also been said that “the Spirit” refers to Gabriel, as Allah says:
“We sent to her Our Spirit, and he appeared before her as a well-proportioned man.”
 [Sūrat Maryam 19:17]
Gabriel breathed into her by Allah’s command, and Jesus, son of Mary, came into being.
Since this is the true reality of Jesus, peace be upon him—that he is one of Allah’s created beings—do not say:
“Three,”
meaning the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.
Allah, the Exalted, is far above having a child, for a child shares in the dominion of his father, whereas Allah alone possesses absolute sovereignty:
“To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth.”

12236 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
“The last sūrah revealed was Barāʾah, and the last verse revealed was:
‘They ask you for a ruling. Say: Allah gives you a ruling concerning kalālah.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4605) and Muslim (1618/11).

12237 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered upon me while I was so ill that I was unconscious.
He performed ablution, and some of the water from his ablution was poured over me, whereupon I regained consciousness.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, only collateral relatives will inherit from me.”
The verse of inheritance was then revealed.
I said to Muḥammad ibn al-Munkadir:
“They ask you for a ruling. Say: Allah gives you a ruling concerning kalālah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (194) and Muslim (1616/8). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.
In another narration:
“The verse of inheritance was revealed,”
meaning the words of Allah:
“Allah instructs you concerning your children: the male receives the share of two females.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:11]
The word kalālah is derived from iklīl, a crown that surrounds the head from its sides.
For this reason, the scholars explained kalālah as a deceased person who leaves neither a child nor a parent.
Some scholars held that kalālah refers only to someone who has no child, using as evidence the words:
“If a man dies leaving no child…”
However, the authentic Sunnah establishes that the absence of a surviving parent is also required, as previously explained in detail in the Book of Inheritance.
This is the position of the majority of scholars among the Companions, the Followers, the recognized imams, and others.
The words:
“If a man dies leaving no child but has a sister, she will receive half of what he leaves”
also relate to the case in which a daughter and a sister survive. The daughter receives one-half and the sister receives the remaining half, as established in the authentic reports.

12238 — Al-Aswad ibn Yazīd narrated:
Muʿādh ibn Jabal came to us in Yemen as a teacher and governor.
We asked him concerning a man who died leaving behind a daughter and a sister.
He awarded one-half to the daughter and one-half to the sister.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6734).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated it (6741) through another route from al-Aswad, who said:
“During the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Muʿādh ibn Jabal ruled among us that the daughter receives one-half and the sister receives one-half.”
Sulaymān narrated the wording:
“He ruled among us,”
without mentioning:
“During the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Other chains establish that this occurred while the Prophet ﷺ was still alive.

12239 — Huzayl ibn Shuraḥbīl narrated:
Abū Mūsā was asked about a deceased person who left behind a daughter, a son’s daughter, and a sister.
He replied:
“The daughter receives one-half, and the sister receives one-half. Go to Ibn Masʿūd, for he will agree with me.”
Ibn Masʿūd was then asked and informed of Abū Mūsā’s ruling.
He said:
“If I were to rule in that manner, I would have gone astray and would not be among the rightly guided.”
He continued:
“I will judge according to the ruling of the Prophet ﷺ:
‘The daughter receives one-half, the son’s daughter receives one-sixth to complete two-thirds, and whatever remains belongs to the sister.’”
We returned to Abū Mūsā and informed him of Ibn Masʿūd’s ruling.
He said:
“Do not ask me for rulings as long as this learned scholar remains among you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6736).

12240 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“O you who believe, do not violate the sacred rites of Allah”:
The polytheists used to perform pilgrimage to the Sacred House, bring sacrificial animals, honor the sanctity of the sacred rites, and engage in trade during pilgrimage.
The Muslims wished to attack them, so Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“Do not violate the sacred rites of Allah.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (8/22–23).
It has also been said that the meaning is:
“Do not regard as lawful what Allah has forbidden you while you are in the state of consecration,”
as indicated by the words:
“When you leave the state of consecration, then hunt.”

12241 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ about game struck with a broad-headed arrow.
He said:
“Eat whatever it strikes with its sharp edge, but whatever it strikes with its broad side is an animal beaten to death.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5475) and Muslim (1929/4).
The word:
“The animal killed by a fall”
refers to one that falls from a high place, such as a mountain, wall, or rooftop, and dies as a result. It also includes an animal that falls into a well.
The word:
“The animal gored to death”
is a passive expression, meaning an animal that is gored by another animal and dies.
The words:
“What a predatory animal has eaten”
refer to prey caught by a predator such as a lion, leopard, tiger, wolf, or dog.
It also includes birds of prey. If an animal dies because such a predator caught it, it is not lawful to eat.
The people of the pre-Islamic period used to eat what a predator left behind from a sheep, camel, cow, or similar animal. Allah prohibited this for the believers.
The words:
“Except what you properly slaughter”
refer back to the animals just mentioned:
“The strangled, the beaten, the fallen, the gored, and what a predator has eaten.”
If the animal is still alive and is properly slaughtered before it dies, it may be eaten.
More than one scholar among the Companions, Followers, and later generations held that if the slaughtered animal makes a movement indicating that life remained in it at the time of slaughter, then it is lawful.

12242 — Abū Umāmah, Ṣudayy ibn ʿAjlān, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent me to my people to call them to Allah and His Messenger and present the laws of Islam to them.
I went to them, and while we were together, they brought a bowl filled with blood and gathered around it to eat.
They said:
“Come, Ṣudayy, and eat.”
I replied:
“Woe to you! I have come to you from the one to whom Allah revealed the prohibition of this.”
They asked:
“What is that?”
I then recited to them:
“Forbidden to you are carrion, blood, and the flesh of swine.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as cited in Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr, al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/279), al-Ḥākim (3/641–642), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (6/126).
The words:
“And that you seek decisions through divining arrows”
refer to azlām, the singular of which is zulam. The initial vowel may also be pronounced with an a, as zalam.
The Arabs practiced this during the pre-Islamic period.
They used three arrows: one was inscribed:
“Do it,”
another:
“Do not do it,”
and the third was blank.
If the commanding arrow was drawn, the person proceeded with the matter. If the prohibiting arrow was drawn, he abandoned it. If the blank arrow was drawn, he repeated the process.
The word istiqsām means seeking one’s allotted decision through these arrows.
Among the greatest idols of Quraysh was an idol called Hubal. It stood inside the Kaʿbah, and gifts and the wealth of the Kaʿbah were placed beside it. Seven divining arrows were kept there.
They also placed images of Abraham and Ishmael inside the Kaʿbah and put divining arrows in their hands, as established in the authentic hadith.

12243 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Makkah, he refused to enter the House while idols remained inside it, so he ordered that they be removed.
Images of Abraham and Ishmael were brought out with divining arrows in their hands.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“May Allah destroy them! They certainly knew that those two never sought decisions through these arrows.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4288).
As for the report in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (3905), Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Juʿshum said that when he set out in pursuit of the Prophet ﷺ and Abū Bakr as they emigrated to Madinah:
“I sought a decision through the divining arrows as to whether I would harm them. The result I disliked came out: ‘Do not harm them.’”
He said:
“I disobeyed the arrows and continued pursuing them.”
He then sought a decision a second and third time, and each time the result he disliked appeared:
“Do not harm them.”
This occurred only by the decree of Allah, not because of any power in the arrows, for divining arrows can neither harm nor benefit.
The Words of Allah: “Today I Have Perfected Your Religion for You…”

12244 — Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb narrated:
A Jewish man came to ʿUmar and said:
“Commander of the Believers, there is a verse in your Book that you recite. Had it been revealed to us Jews, we would have taken the day of its revelation as a festival.”
ʿUmar asked:
“Which verse?”
He replied:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My blessing upon you, and approved Islam as your religion.”
ʿUmar said:
“I certainly know the day and place in which it was revealed. It was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at ʿArafāt on a Friday.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (45) and Muslim (3017/5). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12245 — Kaʿb said:
“Had this verse been revealed to a nation other than this nation, they would have identified the day on which it was revealed and taken it as a festival upon which they gathered.”
ʿUmar asked:
“Which verse, Kaʿb?”
He replied:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My blessing upon you, and approved Islam as your religion.”
ʿUmar said:
“I know the day and place in which it was revealed: it was both Friday and the Day of ʿArafah. By Allah’s praise, both are festivals for us.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (830) and Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (8/87–89).

12246 — ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār narrated:
Ibn ʿAbbās recited:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My blessing upon you, and approved Islam as your religion. But whoever is compelled by severe hunger, without inclining deliberately toward sin, then Allah is Forgiving and Merciful.”
A Jewish man was present and said:
“Had this verse been revealed to us, we would have taken its day as a festival.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“It was revealed on the day of two festivals: Friday and the Day of ʿArafah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3044), Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (2832), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (8/87).
Revelation did not cease after the Day of ʿArafah. It continued until the Prophet ﷺ died three months later.
The completion mentioned here means the completion of the foundations of the religion and its established legislation contained in the Qur’an and authentic Sunnah, as well as its triumph over all other religions.
Before this day, the laws given to previous prophets were suited to the particular needs of specific places and periods.
After the completion of this religion, all previous religious laws were abrogated. This religion, which Allah completed on the Day of ʿArafah, will remain for all humanity until the Day of Resurrection.
Therefore, no claim by anyone professing prophethood or bringing a new religious law will be accepted after this.

12247 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal narrated:
On the Day of Khaybar, I found a leather bag filled with fat. I clutched it to myself and said:
“Today I will not give anyone any of this.”
I then turned and saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiling.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Expeditions (4214) and Muslim in the Book of Jihād and Expeditions (1772), both through Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl from ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal. The wording cited here is that of Muslim.
The following rulings may be derived from this hadith:
It is permissible to eat from captured food according to one’s need before the war spoils are formally divided.
It is permissible to eat the fat belonging to the Jews that had been forbidden to them, as Allah says:
“We prohibited for them the fat of both animals.”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:146]
This refers to the fat of cattle and sheep.
This was the position of the majority of scholars, including Abū Ḥanīfah, al-Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad, and others.
Imām Mālik, however, disliked eating it because it had been prohibited to the Jews and therefore, in his view, was not included in the food Allah made lawful for Muslims. This hadith is evidence against that position.
It is permissible to eat animals slaughtered by the People of the Scripture, namely Jews and Christians.
Allah says:
“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:5]
The food intended here means animals slaughtered by them, as explained by Ibn ʿAbbās and others.
However, two conditions are required:
First: They must mention the name of Allah at the time of slaughter, even though they hold beliefs about Him from which He is utterly exalted, such as claiming that Allah has taken a son, that Allah is the third of three, or saying that the Messiah is their Lord, along with other beliefs refuted by the Noble Qur’an.
Despite those beliefs, Allah said:
“The food of those who were given the Scripture is lawful for you.”
However, if they slaughter the animal in the name of the Messiah or in the name of ʿUzayr, it is not permissible to eat it.
Second: They must slaughter it according to a recognized lawful method, which requires the flowing of blood.
If they kill the animal through strangulation, electrocution, striking the head, immersing it in hot water until it loses consciousness and dies, shooting it, or using blunt objects until it dies, then it is treated as a strangled or beaten animal and is unlawful to eat.
This ruling applies whether the person who does so is a Muslim or a member of the People of the Scripture.
If we do not know how they slaughtered it, the basic ruling is permissibility, in accordance with the apparent meaning of the verse.
If they did not mention the name of Allah over the animal, this is treated as part of their ignorance, and their slaughtered animals do not become unlawful for that reason, just as the slaughter of a Muslim who forgets to mention Allah’s name or is unaware of the ruling remains lawful.
This is based on the hadith in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (5507), in which people said:
“Some people bring us meat, and we do not know whether Allah’s name was mentioned over it.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Mention Allah’s name over it yourselves and eat it.”
As for imported meat or meat sold in the markets of Muslim countries, if it is known to have been slaughtered by the People of the Scripture, it is lawful for Muslims unless it is known that it was killed through an unlawful method, as previously explained.
This is because the basic ruling of permissibility is established by the Qur’anic text and should not be abandoned unless a verified fact requires prohibition.
As for animals slaughtered by disbelievers who are not from the People of the Scripture, it is impermissible to eat them by the consensus of the Muslims.
Question: Most inhabitants of Europe, America, and other predominantly Christian countries have abandoned the religious obligations and recommended practices of their religion and consider themselves free from religion. Are such people treated like other disbelievers and Magians, or do they retain the ruling of the People of the Scripture?
Answer: Such people are not treated like other disbelievers or Magians unless they openly declare that they have left their religion and adopted another religion.
I have seen a group of British people who chose Hinduism. They shaved their heads and wore the clothing of monks, consisting of two yellow garments: one worn as a lower garment and the other as an upper wrap.
They would walk through the streets chanting:
“Hare Krishna, Hare Rama.”
There are likewise groups of Europeans in Hindu centers in India known as ashrams. Such people have left their former religion and joined the Hindus and Magians who worship cows and fire. Therefore, animals slaughtered by them are unlawful.
Likewise, in recent times, republics surrounding the Soviet Union adopted communism and denied religion altogether. Such people are called atheists who have no religion, and their slaughtered animals are also unlawful.
The words:
“And chaste women from among the believing women and chaste women from among those who were given the Scripture”
refer to chaste women from the People of the Scripture, whether free women or bondwomen.
This verse specifies the more general words of Allah:
“Do not marry polytheistic women until they believe.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:221]
The words:
“When you have given them their due compensation”
mean their bridal gifts. Just as they are required to be chaste and modest, their bridal gifts must be paid to preserve the lawful and honorable nature of the marriage.
There is no disagreement among the majority of scholars regarding the permissibility of marrying women from the People of the Scripture.
It has, however, been narrated from some of the early scholars that they prohibited it because of the shirk contained in beliefs such as saying that the Messiah is the son of Allah or that Allah is one of three.
The first view is more correct, because some of the Companions married Christian women in Syria and did not regard it as objectionable.
A ruler may nevertheless restrict such marriages when doing so is necessary to protect the security and welfare of the country.
The words:
“Being chaste, neither committing fornication nor taking secret lovers”
mean that just as chastity is required of women, chastity and lawful restraint are also required of men.
The words:
“Not committing fornication”
refer to those who engage openly in unlawful sexual relations.
The words:
“Nor taking secret lovers”
refer to men who maintain secret mistresses.
Some scholars held that a chaste man may not marry a woman who persists in fornication. Likewise, a chaste woman may not marry a man who persists in fornication, as Allah says in Sūrat al-Nūr:
“The fornicator does not marry except a fornicatress or a polytheistic woman, and the fornicatress is not married except by a fornicator or a polytheist. That has been forbidden to the believers.”
 [Sūrat al-Nūr 24:3]

12248 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
On the Day of Badr, al-Miqdād said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we will not say to you what the Children of Israel said to Moses:
‘Go, you and your Lord, and fight. We will remain sitting here.’
Rather, proceed, and we are with you.”
It was as though the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was relieved by his words.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4609).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated it (3952) through the same chain, with the addition that al-Miqdād said:
“Rather, we will fight on your right, on your left, before you, and behind you.”
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“I saw the face of the Prophet ﷺ brighten, and those words pleased him.”

12249 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Nāsiḥ al-Ḥaḍramī narrated that ʿUtbah ibn ʿAbd said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded the Muslims to fight. One of his Companions was struck by an arrow, whereupon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Paradise has become assured for this man.”
When they were commanded to fight, they said:
“Then, O Messenger of Allah, we will not say what the Children of Israel said:
‘Go, you and your Lord, and fight. We will remain sitting here.’
Rather, go, you and your Lord, and fight; we will be with you among those who fight.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17641), al-Ṭabarānī through his route in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/123), and Yaʿqūb al-Fasawī (2/349–350).

12250 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated from the Prophet ﷺ in a hadith concerning civil strife:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what if someone enters my house and stretches out his hand to kill me?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Be like the son of Adam.”
Yazīd then recited:
“If you stretch out your hand toward me to kill me, I will not stretch out my hand toward you to kill you. Indeed, I fear Allah, Lord of all worlds.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4257), Ibn Abī Shaybah (7/15), al-Bazzār (1223), and Abū Yaʿlā (789).

12251 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
A scorpion stung one of our men while we were sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, may I recite a healing invocation over him?”
He replied:
“Whoever among you is able to benefit his brother should do so.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2199).

12252 — Abū Qilābah narrated:
He was sitting behind ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz while the people discussed a case and said:
“The caliphs previously imposed retaliation in such a case.”
ʿUmar turned toward Abū Qilābah, who was sitting behind him, and asked:
“What do you say, ʿAbdullāh ibn Zayd?”
or:
“What do you say, Abū Qilābah?”
I replied:
“I do not know of any life whose killing is lawful in Islam except a married person who commits adultery, someone who kills another person without legal right, or someone who wages war against Allah and His Messenger.”
ʿAnbasah said:
“Anas narrated to us such-and-such.”
I replied:
“Anas narrated it to me directly.”
He narrated:
A group of people came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“We have found the climate of this land unhealthy.”
He said:
“Some camels belonging to us are going out to graze. Go out with them and drink from their milk and urine.”
They went out with the camels, drank their milk and urine, and recovered their health.
They then attacked the shepherd, killed him, and drove away the camels.
What punishment could be regarded as excessive for such people? They killed a person, waged war against Allah and His Messenger, and caused fear to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿUmar said:
“Glory be to Allah!”
I asked:
“Do you suspect me of error or falsehood?”
He replied:
“Anas narrated this to us.”
He then said to a certain group:
“You will continue to remain upon goodness as long as this man—or someone like him—remains among you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4610) and Muslim (1671/12). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The words:
“And strive throughout the land spreading corruption”
include corruption committed within towns and cities as well as along roads and highways.
This was the position of the majority of scholars, with the exception of Abū Ḥanīfah.
Mālik even held that if someone treacherously lures a man into a house, kills him, and takes his property, this constitutes armed brigandage.
The authority to impose the punishment belongs to the ruler, not merely to the murdered person’s legal heir.
The apparent meaning of the words:
“Except those who repent before you overpower them; know that Allah is Forgiving and Merciful”
indicates that all the prescribed punishments—execution, crucifixion, cutting off opposite hands and feet, or banishment—are waived when the person sincerely repents before capture.
This is how some Companions understood the verse, including ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Abū Hurayrah.
Other scholars held that repentance waives only what relates to the right of Allah, while the rights of other human beings remain and are not cancelled.

12253 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
A Jewish man who had been blackened with charcoal and flogged was brought past the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ summoned the Jews and asked:
“Is this the punishment you find for adultery in your Scripture?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He then called one of their scholars and said:
“I ask you by Allah, Who revealed the Torah to Moses: is this truly the punishment for adultery that you find in your Scripture?”
He replied:
“No. Had you not adjured me in this manner, I would not have told you. We find stoning in the Torah.”
He continued:
“Adultery became widespread among our nobles. When we caught a noble person, we spared him, but when we caught a weak person, we enforced the punishment against him.”
We then said:
“Let us agree upon a punishment that we will apply equally to nobles and common people.”
So we substituted blackening the face and flogging for stoning.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, I am the first to revive Your command after they caused it to die.”
He then ordered that the man be stoned.
Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“O Messenger, do not let those who hasten into disbelief grieve you…”
until the words:
“If you are given this ruling, accept it.”
That is, they said:
“Go to Muḥammad ﷺ. If he orders blackening and flogging, accept his ruling; but if he rules that the punishment is stoning, beware and do not accept it.”
Allah then revealed:
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the disbelievers,”
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the wrongdoers,”
and:
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the defiantly disobedient.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:44–45, 47]
All of these verses were revealed concerning the disbelievers.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1700).

12254 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the disbelievers,”
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the wrongdoers,”
and:
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the defiantly disobedient.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Allah revealed these verses concerning two Jewish tribes. During the pre-Islamic period, one tribe had overpowered the other.
They agreed that the blood money for a member of the weaker tribe killed by the stronger tribe would be fifty wasqs, while the blood money for a member of the stronger tribe killed by the weaker tribe would be one hundred wasqs.
They continued upon this arrangement until the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah. Both tribes became subdued by the arrival of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
At that time, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not yet gained complete authority over them, and a peace agreement remained in effect.
A member of the weaker tribe killed a member of the stronger tribe. The stronger tribe sent word:
“Send us one hundred wasqs.”
The weaker tribe replied:
“Has it ever occurred between two tribes sharing the same religion, ancestry, and homeland that the blood money of one is half that of the other?”
They said:
“We gave you this only because of your oppression and because we feared you. Now that Muḥammad has arrived, we will no longer give it to you.”
War nearly erupted between them, but they eventually agreed to appoint the Messenger of Allah ﷺ as judge.
The stronger tribe then reflected and said:
“By Allah, Muḥammad will not award you from them twice what you pay them for your people. They have spoken truthfully; they paid us that amount only because we oppressed and overpowered them.”
They said:
“Secretly send someone to Muḥammad to discover his opinion. If he gives you what you want, appoint him as judge. If he does not, be cautious and do not accept his judgment.”
They secretly sent some hypocrites to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to discover his ruling for them.
When they came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Allah informed His Messenger of their entire affair and what they intended.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
