“O Messenger, do not let those who hasten into disbelief grieve you, from among those who say, ‘We believe…’”
until the words:
“Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the defiantly disobedient.”
Ibn ʿAbbās then said:
“By Allah, these verses were revealed concerning those two tribes, and it was those two whom Allah, Mighty and Majestic, intended.”
Good: Narrated in an abridged form by Abū Dāwūd (3576) and by Aḥmad (2212). The wording cited here is that of Aḥmad.
It is narrated from Ibn Ṭāwūs that he said:
“It is not like the disbelief of one who disbelieves in Allah, His angels, His Books, and His messengers.”
ʿAṭāʾ said:
“It is disbelief lesser than major disbelief, wrongdoing lesser than major wrongdoing, and disobedience lesser than major disobedience.”
It is also narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās that he said:
“It is not the kind of disbelief that they imagine.”
Al-Ḥākim narrated it (2/313) through his chain from Ṭāwūs, who reported that Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“It is not the disbelief they imagine. It is not disbelief that removes a person from the religion. The words:
‘Whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed—those are the disbelievers’
refer to disbelief lesser than major disbelief.”

12255 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Al-Rubayyiʿ, the paternal aunt of Anas ibn Mālik, broke the front tooth of a young woman from the Anṣār. Her family demanded legal retaliation and came to the Prophet ﷺ, who ordered that retaliation be carried out.
Anas ibn al-Naḍr, the paternal uncle of Anas ibn Mālik, said:
“No, by Allah, her tooth will not be broken, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Anas, the ruling prescribed in the Book of Allah is legal retaliation.”
The injured woman’s family then agreed to accept financial compensation.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among the servants of Allah are those who, if they swear by Allah that something will occur, Allah fulfills their oath.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4611) and Muslim (1675).

12256 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“They eagerly listen to lies and consume unlawful gain. If they come to you, either judge between them or turn away from them.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:42]
The Prophet ﷺ was given a choice under this verse until the following words were revealed:
“Judge between them according to what Allah has revealed.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (3/1153) and al-Ḥākim (2/312).
The Prophet ﷺ was commanded to judge between them after he had previously been permitted to turn away from them if he wished. Thus, according to a group of scholars, this verse abrogated the earlier permission.

12257 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
When the verse:
“Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“They are the people of this man,”
meaning Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (4/1160) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (8/521).
In some narrations:
I recited before the Prophet ﷺ:
“Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him.”
He said:
“They are your people, the people of Yemen.”

12258 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked about the words:
“Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him.”
He replied:
“These are people from Yemen, then from Kindah, then from al-Sakūn, and then from Tujīb.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6534) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ, as cited in Majmaʿ al-Baḥrayn (3312).
The verse has a general meaning and points to Allah’s power to replace people with others better than them, as stated in Sūrat Muḥammad:
“If you turn away, He will replace you with another people, and they will not be like you.”
 [Sūrat Muḥammad 47:38]
Allah also says in Sūrat Ibrāhīm:
“Have you not seen that Allah created the heavens and the earth in truth? If He wills, He can remove you and bring forth a new creation. That is not difficult for Allah.”
 [Sūrat Ibrāhīm 14:19–20]
History bears witness to this.
The words:
“They do not fear the blame of any critic”
mean that the blame of others does not prevent them from obeying Allah.

12259 — Abū Dharr narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ, my beloved friend, commanded me to observe seven matters:
He commanded me to love the poor and remain close to them.
He commanded me to look at those who have less than me and not look at those who have more than me.
He commanded me to maintain family ties even when my relatives turn away.
He commanded me not to ask anyone for anything.
He commanded me to speak the truth even when it is bitter.
He commanded me not to fear the blame of any critic when acting for Allah.
He commanded me to frequently say:
“There is no power and no strength except through Allah,”
for these words are from a treasure beneath the Throne.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (21415), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Duʿāʾ (1648), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa al-Laylah (354).

12260 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
Allah will question His servant on the Day of Resurrection, until He asks him:
“My servant, you saw an evil act, so why did you not denounce it?”
When Allah enables the servant to present his excuse, he will say:
“My Lord, I trusted in You, but I feared the people.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4017), Aḥmad (11735), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7368).

12261 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A nation from the Children of Israel disappeared, and it is not known what happened to them. I do not think they were transformed into anything except mice. Do you not see that when camel’s milk is placed before a mouse, it does not drink it, but when sheep’s milk is placed before it, it drinks?”
Abū Hurayrah said:
I narrated this hadith to Kaʿb, and he asked:
“Did you hear this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He repeated the question several times.
I then said:
“Do I read the Torah?”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2997/61).
Al-Nawawī said:
The words:
“Do you not see that when camel’s milk is placed before it…”
mean that camel meat and milk were forbidden to the Children of Israel, unlike sheep meat and milk. The refusal of the mouse to drink camel’s milk while drinking sheep’s milk was therefore taken as an indication that it had been transformed from a group among the Children of Israel.
The words:
“Do I read the Torah?”
are an interrogative expressing denial. The meaning is:
“I possess no such knowledge except what I heard from the Prophet ﷺ. I do not transmit from the Torah or any earlier scriptures,”
unlike Kaʿb al-Aḥbār and others who possessed knowledge of the traditions of the People of the Scripture.

12262 — Abū Hurayrah said:
“The mouse is a transformed creature. The sign of this is that sheep’s milk is placed before it and it drinks it, while camel’s milk is placed before it and it does not taste it.”
Kaʿb asked him:
“Did you hear this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He replied:
“Was the Torah revealed to me?”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2997/62).

12263 — ʿĀʾishah said:
“Whoever tells you that Muḥammad ﷺ concealed anything that Allah revealed to him has lied. Allah says:
‘O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord.’”
Another narration adds that she said:
“Had Muḥammad ﷺ concealed anything revealed to him, he would have concealed this verse:
‘When you said to the one upon whom Allah had bestowed favor and upon whom you had bestowed favor, “Keep your wife and fear Allah,” while you concealed within yourself what Allah would reveal, and you feared the people, although Allah had a greater right that you should fear Him. Then when Zayd had ended his relationship with her, We married her to you so that there would be no restriction upon the believers concerning the wives of their adopted sons after they have ended their relationship with them. The command of Allah must be fulfilled.’”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:37]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4612) and Muslim (177/287).

12264 — Anas narrated:
Zayd ibn Ḥārithah came complaining about his marriage, and the Prophet ﷺ repeatedly said:
“Fear Allah and keep your wife.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Had the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concealed anything, he would have concealed this verse.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7420).

12265 — Abū Juḥayfah narrated:
I asked ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib:
“Do you possess any written revelation besides the Qur’an?”
He replied:
“No, except the Book of Allah, an understanding that Allah grants to a Muslim, and what is written in this document.”
I asked:
“What is written in the document?”
He replied:
“The regulations concerning blood money, the freeing of captives, and that a Muslim is not executed for killing a disbeliever.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (111).

12266 — ʿAntarah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
I was with Ibn ʿAbbās when a man came and said:
“Some people come to us and claim that you possess knowledge that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not disclose to the people.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“Do you not know that Allah, the Exalted, said:
‘O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord’?
By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not leave us even a black mark written upon white paper.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr, as cited in Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr (3/150).
The Companions testified in the greatest public gathering—the sermon of the Farewell Pilgrimage—that the Messenger ﷺ had fully conveyed everything he was commanded to deliver.

12267 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ delivered a great sermon, in which he said:
“I have left among you that which, if you hold firmly to it, you will never go astray after me: the Book of Allah. You will be questioned about me, so what will you say?”
They replied:
“We testify that you conveyed the message, fulfilled the trust, and gave sincere counsel.”
He raised his index finger toward the sky and then pointed it toward the people, saying three times:
“O Allah, bear witness. O Allah, bear witness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).
Regarding the words:
“If you do not, then you have not conveyed His message,”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“If you conceal even one verse revealed to you from your Lord, then you have not conveyed His message.”
The words:
“Allah will protect you from the people. Indeed, Allah does not guide the disbelieving people”
mean:
“Muḥammad, convey My message and do not fear, for I will protect and support you.”
Before the revelation of this verse, the Prophet ﷺ was guarded, as stated in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections.

12268 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ remained awake one night while she was beside him.
She asked:
“What is troubling you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“I wish that a righteous man from among my Companions would guard me tonight.”
We then heard the sound of weapons.
He asked:
“Who is there?”
The man replied:
“I am Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ. I have come to guard you.”
The Prophet ﷺ then went to sleep.
Another narration states:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ snoring in his sleep.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2885), Muslim (2410), and Aḥmad (25093). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
After this noble verse was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ ordered his guards to leave.
ʿĀʾishah said:
The Prophet ﷺ continued to be guarded until the verse:
“Allah will protect you from the people”
was revealed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ put his head out from the tent and said to them:
“People, leave now, for Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has protected me.”
Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3046). It is a mursal report.
An example of Allah’s protection and preservation of His Messenger ﷺ is the following authentic report.

12269 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
He accompanied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on an expedition toward Najd. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned, Jābir returned with him.
The midday heat overtook them in a valley filled with thorny trees. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped, and the people scattered among the trees seeking shade.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ rested beneath a tree and hung his sword upon it. We then slept for a while.
Suddenly, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called us, and an Arab Bedouin was sitting near him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“This man drew my sword against me while I was asleep. I awoke and found him holding it unsheathed. He asked, ‘Who will protect you from me?’ I replied, ‘Allah,’ three times.”
The Prophet ﷺ did not punish him, and the man sat down.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2910) and Muslim (842).
The name of this Bedouin was Ghawrath ibn al-Ḥārith, as stated in al-Bukhārī (4136).

12270 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped at a place, the Companions would look for the largest tree they could find and reserve it for the Prophet ﷺ. He would rest beneath it, while his Companions would settle afterward beneath the shade of the other trees.
While he was resting beneath a tree with his sword hanging from it, a Bedouin came, took the sword from the tree, approached the Prophet ﷺ while he was asleep, and awakened him.
He said:
“Muḥammad, who will protect you from me tonight?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah.”
Allah then revealed:
“O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord. If you do not, then you have not conveyed His message. Allah will protect you from the people.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān in his Ṣaḥīḥ, as cited in Mawārid al-Ẓamʾān (1739).

12271 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Had ten Jewish leaders followed me, no Jew would have remained upon the face of the earth without accepting Islam.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3941) and Muslim (2793). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.
Aḥmad narrated it (8555) through Abū Hilāl, from Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn, with the wording:
“Had ten of the Jewish scholars believed in me, every Jew upon the face of the earth would have believed in me.”
The ten intended here were their leading figures. Had those leaders believed, the rest of the Jews would have followed them, because people generally follow their leaders.
Otherwise, more than ten Jews did believe in the Prophet ﷺ, the most famous among them being ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām.

12272 — Salmān narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah, I prepared some food and brought it to him.
He asked:
“What is this, Salmān?”
I replied:
“It is charity.”
He said to his Companions:
“Eat,”
but he did not eat from it.
I then returned, gathered more food, and brought it to him.
He asked:
“What is this, Salmān?”
I replied:
“It is a gift.”
He reached out with his hand and ate from it, and he told his Companions:
“Eat.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me about the Christians.”
He replied:
“There is no good in them or in those who love them.”
I stood up feeling heavily burdened.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“You will surely find the most hostile of people toward the believers to be the Jews and those who associate partners with Allah…”
until the words:
“Their eyes overflow with tears.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for me and said:
“Salmān, these companions of yours are the people whom Allah has mentioned.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6/305).
These priests and monks are described as followers of Arius of Egypt, who appeared in the third century CE, affirmed the prophethood of the Messiah, peace be upon him, and denied his divinity. He and his followers were consequently persecuted.
Some of his followers remained until the time of the Prophet ﷺ and believed in him.
Perhaps the Negus, king of Abyssinia, secretly followed this teaching as well and did not reveal it because he feared the Christian majority who followed the doctrine attributed to Paul.
For this reason, he described the Qur’an and the Gospel as coming from one lamp, even though the currently existing Gospels do not explicitly reject the divinity of the Messiah.
Perhaps he had also seen the Gospel of Barnabas, which the text states was discovered fifty years before his reign and contains a denial of the divinity of the Messiah, an affirmation of his prophethood, and glad tidings of the prophethood of Muḥammad ﷺ.
Thus, the expression:
“From one lamp”
would refer to the Qur’an and the Gospel of Barnabas, or to the beliefs attributed to Arius.

12273 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr said:
This verse was revealed concerning the Negus and his companions:
“When they hear what was revealed to the Messenger, you see their eyes overflowing with tears because of the truth they recognize. They say, ‘Our Lord, we believe, so record us among the witnesses.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11148), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (4/1185), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (8/602).
Ibn Isḥāq said:
I asked al-Zuhrī about the verses:
“That is because among them are priests and monks and because they are not arrogant. When they hear what was revealed to the Messenger, you see their eyes overflowing with tears…”
He replied:
“I have continually heard our scholars say that these verses were revealed concerning the Negus and his companions.”
The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (1/392).

12274 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported:
We used to go on military expeditions with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while we had no women with us, so we said:
“Should we not castrate ourselves?”
He forbade us from doing that. Then he permitted us to marry a woman for a specified period in exchange for a garment.
Then ʿAbdullāh recited:
“O you who believe, do not forbid the good things which Allah has made lawful for you, and do not transgress. Indeed, Allah does not love the transgressors.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4615) and Muslim (1404). This is Muslim’s wording.

12275 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“O you who believe, do not forbid the good things that Allah has made lawful for you”:
A group of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ said:
“We will castrate ourselves, abandon the pleasures of this world, and wander throughout the earth as monks do.”
News of this reached the Prophet ﷺ, so he sent for them and mentioned it to them.
They replied:
“Yes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“But I fast and break my fast, I pray and sleep, and I marry women. Whoever follows my Sunnah belongs to me, and whoever does not follow my Sunnah does not belong to me.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (8/611).

12276 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The verse:
“Allah will not hold you accountable for unintentional oaths”
was revealed concerning a person’s customary expressions:
“No, by Allah,”
and:
“Yes, by Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4613).

12277 — ʿĀʾishah, daughter of al-Ṣiddīq, narrated that her father would never break an oath until Allah revealed the expiation for oaths.
Abū Bakr then said:
“Whenever I take an oath and later see that another course is better, I accept Allah’s concession and do what is better.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4614).

12278 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
One man would provide his family with food generously, while another would provide his family with food sparingly.
The following words were then revealed:
“From the average of what you feed your families, or clothing them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2113).

12279 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The prohibition of intoxicants was revealed while there were five kinds of intoxicating drinks in Madinah at that time, none of which was made from grapes.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4616).

12280 — ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb narrated:
They asked Anas ibn Mālik about faḍīkh, and he replied:
“We had no intoxicating drink other than this drink of yours that you call faḍīkh.
I was standing and serving it to Abū Ṭalḥah, Abū Ayyūb, and several men from among the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in our house when a man came and asked:
‘Has the news reached you?’
We replied:
‘No.’
He said:
‘Intoxicants have now been forbidden.’
They said:
‘Anas, pour out these jars.’”
Anas said:
“They did not question the matter or ask anything further about it after receiving the man’s report.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4617) and Muslim (1980/4). The wording cited here is that of Muslim.

12281 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
I heard ʿUmar speaking from the pulpit of the Prophet ﷺ, saying:
“To proceed: People, the prohibition of intoxicants has been revealed. Intoxicants are made from five things: grapes, dates, honey, wheat, and barley. An intoxicant is anything that covers and overwhelms the mind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4619) and Muslim (3032/33).
Muslim added:
“There are three matters, people, concerning which I wish the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had left us a decisive ruling that we could follow: the inheritance of the grandfather, kalālah, and certain forms of usury.”
The word:
“Gambling”
means games of chance. Backgammon is among gambling games, as stated by Ibn ʿUmar and others.
ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and others said:
“Chess is a form of gambling.”
Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr said in al-Tamhīd (13/175):
“Playing backgammon is categorically forbidden, without exception for any time or circumstance. It makes no difference whether it distracts from prayer or does not, or whether it distracts from prayer and similar duties or does not do so.”
End of quotation.
This is also established in the authentic hadith.

12282 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever plays backgammon is as though he dipped his hand into the flesh and blood of a pig.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2260).
The term nardashīr is of non-Arabic origin. Nard is the name of the game, while shīr means sweet.
It is a captivating game played with a board, pieces, and two dice. It depends upon chance, with the pieces moved according to the numbers shown by the dice. It is commonly known as backgammon.
One says:
“He played nard.”
Al-Muʿjam al-Wasīṭ.
Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr said:
“Nard consists of colored pieces made from boxwood, ivory, or other materials. It is also known as al-ṭabl, al-kiʿāb, al-arn, and nardashīr.”
End of quotation.
Al-Tamhīd (13/175).

12283 — Anas narrated:
I was serving drinks to the people in the home of Abū Ṭalḥah. Their intoxicating drink at that time was faḍīkh.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered a caller to announce:
“Be informed that intoxicants have been forbidden.”
Abū Ṭalḥah said to me:
“Go out and pour it away.”
I went out and poured it away, and it flowed through the streets of Madinah.
Some people said:
“Some people were killed while this drink was still in their stomachs.”
Allah then revealed:
“There is no blame upon those who believed and performed righteous deeds for what they consumed…”
until the end of the verse.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2464) and Muslim (1980). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.

12284 — Anas narrated:
While I was passing the drinking cup among Abū Ṭalḥah, Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ, Muʿādh, Suhayl ibn Bayḍāʾ, and Abū Dujānah until their heads began to incline, we heard a caller announce:
“Be informed that intoxicants have been forbidden.”
No one entered upon us, and no one left from among us. We poured away the drink, broke the jars, some of us performed ablution, some bathed, and we applied some perfume belonging to Umm Sulaym. We then went out to the mosque.
There we found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reciting:
“O you who believe, intoxicants, gambling, stone altars, and divining arrows are only filth from the work of Satan…”
until he reached:
“Will you then desist?”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:91]
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is the condition of those who died while drinking it?”
Allah, Blessed and Exalted, then revealed:
“There is no blame upon those who believed and performed righteous deeds for what they consumed…”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:93]
until the end of the verse.
A man asked Qatādah:
“Did you hear this from Anas?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
A man then asked Anas:
“Did you hear this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He replied:
“Yes—or someone who would never lie to me narrated it to me. By Allah, we did not used to lie, nor did we know what lying was.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (7288) and al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (7/37).

12285 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
When the verse:
“There is no blame upon those who believed and performed righteous deeds for what they consumed, provided they feared Allah, believed, and performed righteous deeds, then continued to fear Allah and believe, and then continued to fear Allah and do good. Allah loves those who do good”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“I was told that you are among them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2459).
It was also narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (10/77) and al-Ḥākim (4/143–144).

12286 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what about those who died while they were drinking intoxicants?”
When the prohibition of intoxicants was revealed, the following verse was revealed:
“There is no blame upon those who believed and performed righteous deeds for what they consumed, provided they feared Allah, believed, and performed righteous deeds.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3052), Aḥmad (2088), Ibn Jarīr (8/665), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (4/143).

12287 — Jābir narrated:
Some people drank intoxicants on the morning of the Battle of Uḥud. They were all killed as martyrs that same day, and this occurred before intoxicants were prohibited.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4618).

12288 — Jābir said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dispatched three hundred mounted men, with Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ as our commander. This expedition was known as the Army of al-Khabaṭ.
The sea cast up for us a creature called al-ʿAnbar. We ate from it for half a month and rubbed ourselves with its fat, until our bodies regained their strength.
Abū ʿUbaydah took one of its ribs and erected it, then selected the tallest man among those with him.
Sufyān once narrated:
“He erected one of its ribs, then a man mounted on a camel passed beneath it.”
Jābir said:
A man from the group slaughtered three camels, then another three, and then another three. Abū ʿUbaydah then prohibited him from slaughtering any more.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4361) and Muslim (1935:18). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12289 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The people questioned the Prophet of Allah ﷺ so persistently that they overwhelmed him with questions. One day he came out, ascended the pulpit, and said:
“Ask me. You will not ask me about anything without my making it clear to you.”
When the people heard this, they fell silent and feared that some grave matter was about to occur.
Anas said:
I began looking to my right and left, and every man had wrapped his head in his garment and was weeping.
A man in the mosque who used to be disputed with and attributed to someone other than his father stood and asked:
“Prophet of Allah, who is my father?”
He replied:
“Your father is Ḥudhāfah.”
Then ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb stood and said:
“We are pleased with Allah as our Lord, Islam as our religion, and Muḥammad as our Messenger. We seek refuge in Allah from the evil of tribulations.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Never before have I seen anything like this day in both good and evil. Paradise and Hell were depicted before me, and I saw them on the other side of this wall.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6362) and Muslim (2359:137). The wording is that of Muslim.
He also said:
Qatādah used to mention this verse when narrating this hadith:
“O you who believe, do not ask about matters which, if disclosed to you, would distress you.”
The expression:
“They overwhelmed him with questions”
means that they persisted excessively and repeatedly. The Arabic expressions aḥfā, alḥafa, and alaḥḥa convey the same meaning.
The word:
“They fell silent”
means that they stopped speaking. It is derived from a word meaning the lip, as though they closed their lips together and did not speak.
The expression:
“He used to be disputed with”
refers to quarrelling and exchanging insults.

12290 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Something concerning his Companions reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so he delivered a sermon and said:
“Paradise and Hell were shown to me, and I have never seen anything like this day in both good and evil. Had you known what I know, you would have laughed little and wept much.”
Anas said:
No day more severe than that ever came upon the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
They covered their heads and could be heard sobbing.
ʿUmar then stood and said:
“We are pleased with Allah as our Lord, Islam as our religion, and Muḥammad as our Prophet.”
That man then stood and asked:
“Who is my father?”
He replied:
“Your father is so-and-so.”
The following verse was then revealed:
“O you who believe, do not ask about matters which, if disclosed to you, would distress you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4621) and Muslim (2359:134). The wording is that of Muslim.

12291 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out after the sun had passed its zenith and led them in the Ẓuhr prayer. After concluding the prayer, he stood upon the pulpit, mentioned the Hour, and spoke of tremendous events that would precede it. He then said:
“Whoever wishes to ask me about anything should ask me. By Allah, you will not ask me about anything without my informing you of it, as long as I remain standing in this place.”
Anas ibn Mālik said:
The people began weeping profusely when they heard this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while he repeatedly said:
“Ask me.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah stood and asked:
“Who is my father, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Your father is Ḥudhāfah.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ repeatedly said, “Ask me,” ʿUmar knelt and said:
“We are pleased with Allah as our Lord, Islam as our religion, and Muḥammad as our Messenger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became silent when ʿUmar said that. Then he said:
“Indeed! By the One in whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, Paradise and Hell were just now shown to me across this wall, and I have never seen anything like this day in both good and evil.”
Ibn Shihāb said:
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah informed me that the mother of ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah said to him:
“I have never heard of a son more undutiful than you! Were you certain that your mother had not committed something that women during the pre-Islamic period used to commit, so that you would expose her publicly before everyone?”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah replied:
“By Allah, had he attributed me to a black slave, I would have accepted him as my father.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2359:136), whose wording is cited, and al-Bukhārī (540).
Her word:
“Committed”
means that she had done something evil, namely adultery.

12292 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some people used to question the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mockingly. One man would ask:
“Who is my father?”
Another man whose camel had gone missing would ask:
“Where is my camel?”
Allah revealed concerning them:
“O you who believe, do not ask about matters which, if disclosed to you, would distress you,”
until the completion of the entire verse.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4622).
This noble verse and these hadiths indicate the dislike of asking about matters that are not needed and whose answers may distress the questioner. It is authentically established:

12293 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Allah dislikes three things for you: idle talk and transmitting everything people say, wasting wealth, and excessive questioning.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1477) and Muslim (593:13).
The Emigrants and the Anṣār were not given the same latitude in asking questions as the Bedouins and similar delegations who came to Madinah to learn the religion and then return to their lands as callers to it.

12294 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān narrated:
I remained with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in Madinah for one year. Nothing prevented me from formally emigrating except my desire to continue asking questions, because once one of us emigrated, he would no longer ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ freely about matters.
I asked him about righteousness and sin.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Righteousness is good character, and sin is that which troubles your conscience and which you dislike people discovering.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2553:15).

12295 — Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib said:
The baḥīrah was an animal whose milk was reserved for idols, and no one was permitted to milk it.
The sāʾibah was an animal they released for their deities, and no load was placed upon it.
He said that Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I saw ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir al-Khuzāʿī dragging his intestines in the Fire. He was the first person to institute the practice of releasing sāʾibah animals.”
The waṣīlah was a young she-camel that produced a female as its first offspring and then another female as its second. If the two females followed one another without a male between them, they released the mother for their idols.
The ḥām was a male camel that had completed a prescribed number of matings. Once it had done so, they dedicated it to their idols and exempted it from carrying loads. They called it the ḥāmī.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4623) and Muslim (2856:51). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12296 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I saw Hell crushing and consuming itself, and I saw ʿAmr dragging his intestines. He was the first person to institute the practice of releasing sāʾibah animals.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4624) and Muslim (901:3). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12297 — Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim narrated:
Abū Bakr stood, praised Allah, and extolled Him. He then said:
“People, you recite this verse:
‘O you who believe, take care of yourselves. Those who go astray cannot harm you when you are rightly guided.’
Yet we heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘When people see wrongdoing and do not change it, Allah will soon encompass them all with His punishment.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4338), al-Tirmidhī (2168), Ibn Mājah (4005), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11157), and Aḥmad (16).

12298 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
While we were gathered around the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, tribulation was mentioned, or it was discussed in his presence. He said:
“When you see that people’s covenants have become confused, their trusts have weakened, and they have become like this,”
and he interlocked his fingers.
I stood and asked:
“What should I do at that time? May Allah make me your ransom.”
He replied:
“Remain in your home, restrain your tongue, adhere to what you recognize as right, abandon what you recognize as wrong, concern yourself with your own private affairs, and leave the affairs of the general public.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4343), Aḥmad (6987), and al-Ḥākim (4/282).

12299 — Abū Umayyah al-Shaʿbānī narrated:
I asked Abū Thaʿlabah al-Khushanī:
“Abū Thaʿlabah, what do you say concerning the verse: ‘Take care of yourselves’?”
He replied:
“By Allah, I asked someone fully knowledgeable about it. I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said:
‘Rather, continue commanding what is right and forbidding what is wrong, until you see greed being obeyed, desires being followed, worldly life being preferred, and every person admiring his own opinion. Then concern yourself with your own soul and leave the common people, for ahead of you are days requiring patience. Patience during those days will be like holding burning coals. The one who acts righteously during them will receive the reward of fifty men who perform deeds like his.’”
Another narrator added that someone asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, the reward of fifty men from among them?”
He replied:
“The reward of fifty from among you.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4341), al-Tirmidhī (3058), Ibn Mājah (4014), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (385).

12300 — Abū ʿĀmir al-Ashʿarī narrated:
A man among them was killed at Awṭās. The Prophet ﷺ said to Abū ʿĀmir:
“Abū ʿĀmir, why did you not intervene and change what was happening?”
He recited the verse:
“O you who believe, take care of yourselves. Those who go astray cannot harm you when you are rightly guided.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became angry and said:
“Where have your minds gone? Its meaning is: O you who believe, those among the disbelievers who go astray cannot harm you when you yourselves are rightly guided.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (17165).

12301 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A man from Banū Sahm traveled with Tamīm al-Dārī and ʿAdī ibn Baddāʾ. The man from Banū Sahm died in a land where there were no Muslims.
When Tamīm and ʿAdī returned with his estate, the deceased man’s family discovered that a silver cup decorated with gold was missing. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ made both men swear an oath.
Later, the cup was found in Makkah. Those possessing it said:
“We purchased it from Tamīm and ʿAdī.”
Two men from the deceased man’s relatives then stood and swore:
“Our testimony is more deserving of acceptance than their testimony, and the cup belonged to our companion.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
The following verse was revealed concerning them:
“O you who believe, testimony between you when death approaches one of you at the time of making a bequest shall be given by two just men from among you…”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2780).

12302 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ delivered a sermon and said:
“People, you will be gathered before Allah barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised.”
He then recited:
“On the Day We roll up the heaven like the rolling of a written scroll. Just as We began the first creation, We will repeat it—a promise binding upon Us. We will surely do it.”
 [Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:104]
until the end of the verse.
He then said:
“Be informed that the first of creation to be clothed on the Day of Resurrection will be Abraham. Some men from my nation will then be brought and taken toward the left. I will say, ‘My Lord, my little companions!’
It will be said, ‘You do not know what they introduced after you.’
I will then say what the righteous servant said:
‘I was a witness over them as long as I remained among them. When You took me, You alone were the Watcher over them, and You are Witness over all things.’
It will then be said, ‘These people continued turning back upon their heels from the time you departed from them.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4625) and Muslim (2860:56). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
This contains clear evidence that the deification of the Messiah entered Christianity after Jesus, peace be upon him, was raised and that he never claimed divinity for himself.
The words:
“If You punish them, they are Your servants; and if You forgive them, You alone are the Almighty, the All-Wise”
express complete submission of the entire matter to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
If the words:
“And if You forgive them”
contain a request for their forgiveness, that reflects the gentle nature of Jesus, peace be upon him. Nevertheless, he entrusted the entire matter to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
For this reason, the verse concludes:
“Indeed, You alone are the Almighty, the All-Wise.”
This resembles the Prophet ﷺ asking his Lord for permission to seek forgiveness for his mother out of compassion for her, but he was not granted permission. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has decreed:
“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:48]
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, concluded the verse with the words:
“Indeed, You alone are the Almighty, the All-Wise.”
The name:
“The Almighty”
indicates Allah’s complete power, should He will to forgive.
The name:
“The All-Wise”
indicates that none of Allah’s decrees and actions are devoid of perfect wisdom.
Thus, His refusal to forgive those who die upon shirk and His forgiveness of others who fulfill the causes of forgiveness both return to His perfect power and wisdom.

12303 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man from the Anṣār who lived in Qubāʾ invited the Prophet ﷺ, so we went with him. When he had eaten and washed one or both of his hands, he said:
“All praise belongs to Allah, Who feeds and is not fed. He bestowed favor upon us, guided us, fed us, gave us drink, and granted us every good trial and blessing. All praise belongs to Allah—a praise that is not abandoned, cannot adequately repay Him, is not denied, and which no one can dispense with. All praise belongs to Allah, Who fed us food, gave us drink, clothed us after nakedness, guided us from misguidance, gave us sight after blindness, and greatly favored us above many of those He created. All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa al-Laylah (301), Ibn al-Sunnī (486), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5219).

12304 — Warrād, the secretary of al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah, narrated:
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah dictated to me in a letter addressed to Muʿāwiyah that the Prophet ﷺ used to say after every obligatory prayer:
“There is no deity except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things. O Allah, none can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold. The wealth and good fortune of a fortunate person cannot benefit him against You.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (844) and Muslim (593:138).
The expression:
“The possessor of good fortune”
is pronounced with a fatḥah on the letter jīm. It means that a person’s wealth and worldly fortune cannot benefit him against Allah.

12305 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When you see Allah giving a servant the worldly things he loves despite his continuing acts of disobedience, that is only gradual enticement toward punishment.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then recited:
“When they forgot what they had been reminded of, We opened for them the gates of everything. Then, when they rejoiced in what they had been given, We seized them suddenly, and they were plunged into despair.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17311), al-Dūlābī in al-Kunā (1/111), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (9268).

12306 — Saʿd narrated:
We were six people sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when the polytheists said to him:
“Drive these people away so that they do not become bold enough to sit with us.”
Saʿd said:
I was among them, as were Ibn Masʿūd, a man from Hudhayl, Bilāl, and two other men whose names I do not mention.
Something occurred in the mind of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ according to what Allah willed, and he considered the matter within himself. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Do not drive away those who call upon their Lord morning and evening, seeking His Face. You are not accountable for them in any way.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2413:46).
Muslim also narrated it (2413:45) through another route from Saʿd, who said:
“The following verse was revealed concerning me:
‘Do not drive away those who call upon their Lord morning and evening.’
It was revealed concerning six people, including me and Ibn Masʿūd. The polytheists had said to him, ‘Do you bring these people close to you?’”
The wording:
“I do not mention their names”
appears in another narration as:
“I have forgotten their names.”
This means that the leaders of Quraysh mocked those who believed and said:
“Are these the ones upon whom Allah has bestowed favor from among us?”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:53]
They also said:
“Had it been something good, they would not have preceded us to it.”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥqāf 46:11]

12307 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah completed the creation, He wrote in a Book that is with Him above the Throne: ‘Indeed, My mercy has overcome My wrath.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3194) and Muslim (2751).

12308 — Salmān al-Fārisī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“On the day Allah created the heavens and the earth, He created one hundred portions of mercy, each one filling the space between the heaven and the earth. He placed one portion of that mercy upon the earth. Through it, a mother shows tenderness to her child, and wild animals and birds show compassion toward one another. When the Day of Resurrection comes, Allah will complete the full one hundred portions with this mercy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2753:21).

12309 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
Some captives were brought before the Prophet ﷺ. Among them was a woman whose breasts were overflowing with milk. Whenever she found a child among the captives, she took the child, pressed it against her stomach, and nursed it.
The Prophet ﷺ asked us:
“Do you think this woman would throw her child into the Fire?”
We replied:
“No, not while she has the power to avoid doing so.”
He said:
“Allah is more merciful toward His servants than this woman is toward her child.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5999) and Muslim (2754).

12310 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The keys of the unseen are five, and none knows them except Allah: no one knows what will happen tomorrow; no one knows what is within the wombs; no soul knows what it will earn tomorrow; no soul knows in which land it will die; and no one knows when rain will come.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1039).
Other hadiths with the same meaning will appear in the tafsīr of Sūrat Luqmān.

12311 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
When the verse:
“Say: He has the power to send punishment upon you from above you”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I seek refuge in Your Face.”
When the words:
“Or from beneath your feet”
were recited, he said:
“I seek refuge in Your Face.”
When the words:
“Or confuse you into factions and make some of you taste the violence of others”
were recited, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“This is less severe,”
or:
“This is easier.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4628).

12312 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
When the verse:
“Those who believe and do not mix their faith with wrongdoing”
was revealed, it was difficult for the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ.
They said:
“Which of us has not wronged himself?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“It is not as you think. It means what Luqmān said to his son:
‘My son, do not associate anything with Allah. Indeed, associating partners with Allah is a tremendous wrong.’”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:13]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4629) and Muslim (124).

12313 — Mujāhid narrated that he asked Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Is there a prostration of recitation in Sūrat Ṣād?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He then recited from:
“We granted him Isaac and Jacob…”
until the words:
“Follow their guidance.”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:84–90]
He then said:
“He—meaning David—is among them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4632).
Al-Bukhārī then said:
Yazīd ibn Hārūn, Muḥammad ibn ʿUbayd, and Sahl ibn Yūsuf added, narrating from al-ʿAwwām, from Mujāhid:
“I said this to Ibn ʿAbbās, and he replied: ‘Your Prophet ﷺ was among those commanded to follow their guidance.’”

12314 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A servant says, ‘My wealth! My wealth!’ Yet only three things from his wealth truly belong to him: what he eats and consumes, what he wears and wears out, or what he gives away and sends ahead. Everything else will depart from him, and he will leave it for other people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2959).

12315 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that Waraqah ibn Nawfal said to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“No man has ever brought what you have brought without being opposed. Should I live to see the day when your people drive you out, I will support you with mighty assistance.”
This occurred within a longer narration.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3) and Muslim (160).
Regarding the words:
“The devils among humankind and jinn,”
a devil is anyone who rebels against obedience to Allah and His commands.
A human devil may be more evil than the devils among the jinn. The jinn devils inspire the human devils until a person’s behavior resembles that of the devils among the jinn. They then inspire one another with embellished and deceptive speech.

12316 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Indeed, the devils inspire their allies”:
They said:
“Do not eat what Allah has killed, but eat what you yourselves slaughter.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, therefore revealed:
“Do not eat from that over which the name of Allah has not been mentioned.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2818), Ibn Mājah (3173), and al-Ḥākim (4/113).
These verses contain Allah’s clarification of which slaughtered animals are lawful to eat and which are unlawful.
There is no disagreement that an animal over which Allah’s name was mentioned is lawful to eat.
In contrast, animals slaughtered upon stone altars and animals that died without proper slaughter are unlawful to eat without disagreement.
As for an animal over which Allah’s name was not mentioned, the scholars held several views.
The first view: An animal over which Allah’s name was not mentioned is unlawful, whether the omission was deliberate or forgetful.
This was one narration from Mālik, one narration from Aḥmad, and the position of some Shāfiʿī scholars.
The second view: Mentioning Allah’s name is not obligatory but recommended. Therefore, whether it is omitted deliberately or forgetfully, the slaughter remains lawful.
This was the position of al-Shāfiʿī and his companions and one narration from Mālik and Aḥmad.
They interpreted the verse:
“Do not eat from that over which Allah’s name has not been mentioned, for it is certainly grave disobedience”
as referring to what was slaughtered for someone other than Allah, based upon His words:
“Or sinful meat over which a name other than Allah’s was invoked.”
They also interpreted the verse as referring to carrion eaten by the people of the pre-Islamic period.
It is narrated that Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“When a Muslim slaughters an animal without mentioning Allah’s name, eat it, for the word ‘Muslim’ itself contains one of the names of Allah.”
The third view: If Allah’s name was omitted forgetfully, the animal does not become unlawful; but if it was omitted deliberately, the animal is unlawful.
This view is narrated from the majority of scholars, including Abū Ḥanīfah, Mālik, and Aḥmad. It was also the opinion of ʿAlī, Ibn ʿAbbās, and other Companions.

12317 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, created His creation in darkness. He then cast upon them from His light on that day. Whoever was touched by that light was guided, and whoever it missed went astray. For this reason, I say that the Pen has dried according to the knowledge of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (6644).
The light is the noble Book of Allah, while the darknesses are ignorance and various forms of misguidance.
Allah has presented many comparisons in His mighty Book. He says:
“The blind and the seeing are not equal, nor are darkness and light, nor shade and scorching heat. Neither are the living and the dead equal. Allah causes whomever He wills to hear, but you cannot make those in the graves hear. You are only a warner.”
 [Sūrat Fāṭir 35:19–23]
There are many other verses with similar meanings.

12318 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“When you wish to understand the ignorance of the Arabs, read beyond verse 130 of Sūrat al-Anʿām:
‘Lost indeed are those who killed their children foolishly and without knowledge,’
until the words:
‘They have certainly gone astray and were not rightly guided.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3524).

12319 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say during the year of the Conquest while he was in Makkah:
“Allah and His Messenger have forbidden the sale of intoxicants, carrion, swine, and idols.”
Someone asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what about the fat of dead animals? It is used to coat ships, grease hides, and provide fuel for people’s lamps.”
He replied:
“No. It is forbidden.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“May Allah destroy the Jews! When Allah prohibited animal fat to them, they rendered it down, sold it, and consumed its price.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2236) and Muslim (1581).

12320 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“May Allah destroy the Jews! Fat was forbidden to them, yet they sold it and consumed its price.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2224) and Muslim (1583:74). The wording is that of Muslim.

12321 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
News reached ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb that a certain man had sold wine. He said:
“May Allah destroy so-and-so! Did he not know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘May Allah destroy the Jews! Fat was forbidden to them, but they rendered it down and sold it.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2223) and Muslim (1582). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī. In Muslim’s narration:
“News reached ʿUmar that Samurah had sold wine.”
The words:
“Every animal with an undivided hoof”
refer to animals and birds whose toes are not separated, such as camels and ostriches.
Regarding the words:
“Or the entrails,”
Ibn Jarīr said:
“It is a plural whose singular is ḥāwiyāʾ or ḥawiyyah. It refers to what coils within the abdomen, gathering and curving around. These are the smaller intestines, the intestinal tracts, and what are also called the internal folds containing the bowels.”
“Banāt al-laban” refers to the smaller intestines. “Mabāʿir” is the plural of “mabʿar,” meaning the location of dung in every four-legged animal.
The words:
“That is how We recompensed them for their rebellion”
mean that this prohibition was imposed because of their transgression and aggression, as Allah says:
“Because of wrongdoing committed by those who were Jews, We forbade them good things that had previously been lawful for them, and because of their frequent obstruction of others from the path of Allah.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:160]
As for the words of Allah in Sūrat Āl ʿImrān:
“All food was lawful to the Children of Israel except what Israel had forbidden himself before the Torah was revealed.”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:93]
The reason was that Israel—who was Jacob, peace be upon him—suffered from an illness and therefore forbade certain foods to himself.
It is narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās that he forbade himself the veins. It has also been said that he forbade himself camel meat, after which his sons and followers prohibited it, even though Allah had not forbidden it to them through revelation or upon the tongue of one of His messengers.
They then added whatever further prohibitions they wished, and this occurred before Allah revealed the Torah.
When Allah revealed the Torah, He prohibited certain things that had previously been lawful but which they themselves had declared forbidden. Allah then imposed those prohibitions upon them as punishment for their rebellion.
The words:
“Indeed, We are truthful”
mean that Allah is truthful in what He reports concerning these Jews, while they lied in claiming that Israel had forbidden all those things to himself. The correct position is that Israel forbade himself only certain foods, as previously mentioned.

12322 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit—who had attended Badr and was one of the representatives on the night of al-ʿAqabah—narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said while a group of his Companions were gathered around him:
“Pledge allegiance to me that you will not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit adultery, kill your children, produce a slander that you falsely attribute between your hands and feet, or disobey in anything right and proper. Whoever among you fulfills this pledge, his reward is with Allah. Whoever commits any of these things and is punished for it in this world, that punishment will be an expiation for him. Whoever commits any of these things and Allah conceals him, his affair rests with Allah: if He wills, He will pardon him, and if He wills, He will punish him.”
We then pledged allegiance to him upon that.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (18) and Muslim (1709).

12323 — ʿAmr ibn Murrah narrated:
I heard Abū Wāʾil say:
I heard ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd say—
I asked him:
“Did you hear it directly from ʿAbdullāh?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He attributed it to the Prophet ﷺ, who said:
“No one has greater protective jealousy than Allah. For that reason, He prohibited immoralities, both apparent and hidden. No one loves praise more than Allah, and for that reason He praised Himself.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4634) and Muslim (2760:34).

12324 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has protective jealousy, and the believer has protective jealousy. Allah’s protective jealousy is aroused when a believer commits what He has forbidden him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5223) and Muslim (2761).
The words:
“Do not kill your children because of poverty; We provide for you and for them”
mean that Allah prohibited anyone from killing his child out of fear of poverty. The people of the pre-Islamic period used to kill daughters because of shame and sons because of fear of destitution.
This is established in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections:

12325 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
I asked—or the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Which sin is greatest in the sight of Allah?”
He replied:
“That you appoint a rival alongside Allah even though He created you.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you kill your child out of fear that he will share your food.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you commit adultery with your neighbor’s wife.”
He said:
The following verse was revealed confirming the statement of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Those who do not invoke another deity alongside Allah, do not kill a soul that Allah has forbidden except with legal right, and do not commit adultery—and whoever does that will encounter a severe penalty.”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4761) and Muslim (86:142). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12326 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ drew a line for us and said:
“This is the path of Allah.”
He then drew lines to its right and left and said:
“These are other paths. Upon every one of them is a devil calling people toward it.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, this is My straight path, so follow it, and do not follow the other paths, for they will separate you from His path.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4142), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2210), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6, 7).

12327 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah presents the example of a straight path. On both sides of the path are two walls containing open doors, and over the doors are lowered curtains. At the entrance to the path is a caller saying, ‘People, enter the path altogether and do not deviate.’ Another caller calls from above the path. Whenever a person intends to open one of those doors, he says, ‘Woe to you! Do not open it, for if you open it, you will enter through it.’
The path is Islam. The two walls are the limits set by Allah. The open doors are the things Allah has forbidden. The caller at the entrance to the path is the Book of Allah, and the caller from above the path is Allah’s admonisher within the heart of every Muslim.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17634), al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ al-Mushkil (2142), and al-Ḥākim (1/73).

12328 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until the sun rises from the west. When the people see it, everyone upon the earth will believe. That will be the time when:
‘No soul will benefit from its faith if it had not believed before.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4635) and Muslim (157). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12329 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are three things which, when they appear, no soul will benefit from faith if it had not believed before or earned goodness through its faith: the rising of the sun from the west, the Antichrist, and the Beast of the Earth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (158).

12330 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated from the Prophet ﷺ, relating from his Lord, Mighty and Majestic:
“Allah recorded the good deeds and the evil deeds, and then explained them. Whoever intends a good deed but does not perform it, Allah records it with Him as one complete good deed. If he intends it and performs it, Allah records it with Him as ten good deeds, up to seven hundred times, and up to many further multiples. Whoever intends an evil deed but does not perform it, Allah records it with Him as one complete good deed. If he intends it and performs it, Allah records it as one evil deed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6491) and Muslim (131).
Muslim added:
“Allah may erase it, and none is destroyed before Allah except one who is truly ruined.”

12331 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that Allah says:
“When My servant intends to commit an evil deed, do not record it against him until he actually commits it. If he commits it, record it as one equivalent evil deed. If he abandons it for My sake, record it for him as a good deed. When he intends to perform a good deed but does not perform it, record it for him as one good deed. If he performs it, record it for him as ten times its value, up to seven hundred times.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7501) and Muslim (128:203). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12332 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A woman would circumambulate the House while naked and say:
“Who will lend me a garment to wear during ṭawāf?”
She would place it over her private parts and say:
“Today, some or all of it may appear,
 but whatever appears of it, I do not make lawful.”
Then this verse was revealed:
“Wear your adornment at every place of prayer.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3028).

12333 — ʿAmr ibn Murrah narrated:
I heard Abū Wāʾil say:
I heard ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd say—
I asked him:
“Did you hear it directly from ʿAbdullāh?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He attributed it to the Prophet ﷺ, who said:
“No one possesses greater protective jealousy than Allah. For that reason, He prohibited immoralities, both outward and inward. And no one loves praise more than Allah. For that reason, He praised Himself.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4634) and Muslim (2760:34).

12334 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A caller will announce: You will remain healthy and never become ill. You will live and never die. You will remain young and never grow old. You will enjoy everlasting bliss and never experience misery.”
That is the meaning of the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“They will be called: This is Paradise, which you have been made to inherit because of what you used to do.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2837).

12335 — Anas narrated from the Prophet ﷺ concerning the words of Allah:
“When his Lord manifested Himself to the mountain,”
that he gestured in this manner, meaning that he showed the tip of his little finger.
My father said:
“Muʿādh demonstrated it to us.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3074), Aḥmad (12260)—whose wording is cited—and Ibn Khuzaymah in al-Tawḥīd (194–200).
The words:
“The tip of the little finger”
mean that the slightest manifestation occurred to the mountain, whereupon it was crushed and broken apart, becoming like sand.
The words of Allah:
“Moses fell unconscious”
mean that he fell into a state of unconsciousness.

12336 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
A Jewish man came to the Prophet ﷺ after having been struck across the face and said:
“Muḥammad, one of your Companions from the Anṣār struck me across the face.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Call him.”
They called him, and the Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Why did you strike his face?”
He replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I passed by the Jews and heard him say, ‘By the One Who chose Moses above all humankind.’ I said, ‘Even above Muḥammad?’ Anger then overcame me, so I struck him.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not give me superiority over the other prophets. The people will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will be the first to regain consciousness. I will then find Moses holding one of the supports of the Throne. I will not know whether he regained consciousness before me or whether his earlier unconsciousness at the mountain was counted for him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4638) and Muslim (2374). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12337 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Two men exchanged insults: one was a Muslim and the other a Jew.
The Muslim said:
“By the One Who chose Muḥammad above all peoples.”
The Jew replied:
“By the One Who chose Moses above all peoples.”
The Muslim then raised his hand and struck the Jew across the face.
The Jew went to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him of what had occurred between him and the Muslim. The Prophet ﷺ summoned the Muslim and questioned him, and he told him what had happened.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not give me superiority over Moses. The people will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will fall unconscious with them. I will then be the first to regain consciousness, and there will be Moses holding the side of the Throne. I will not know whether he was among those who fell unconscious and regained consciousness before me, or whether he was among those whom Allah exempted.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2411) and Muslim (2373:16). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12338 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Receiving news is not like seeing something with one’s own eyes. Allah informed Moses of what his people had done regarding the calf, yet he did not throw down the tablets. When he saw with his own eyes what they had done, he threw down the tablets and they broke.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2447), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (25), and al-Bazzār (5062, 5155).

12339 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every prophet before me was sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all humankind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521).
This necessitates that the Prophet ﷺ be the final prophet, because his prophethood encompasses every time and place.
Allah, the Exalted, says:
“Muḥammad is not the father of any of your men, but he is the Messenger of Allah and the Seal of the Prophets. Allah has full knowledge of all things.”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:40]

12340 — Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Truffles are from the manna, and their liquid is a cure for the eye.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4639) and Muslim (2049:158).

12341 — Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Children of Israel were told:
‘Enter the gate prostrating and say, “Relieve us of our sins,” and We will forgive your sins.’
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:58]
But they altered the command. They entered crawling upon their buttocks and said, ‘A grain within a hair.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4641) and Muslim (3015). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim narrated similar wording.

12342 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿUyaynah ibn Ḥiṣn ibn Ḥudhayfah ibn Badr came and stayed with his nephew, al-Ḥurr ibn Qays ibn Ḥiṣn. Al-Ḥurr was among those whom ʿUmar kept close to him. The reciters and scholars of the Qur’an were the members of ʿUmar’s council and those whom he consulted, whether they were elderly or young.
ʿUyaynah said to his nephew:
“My nephew, do you have sufficient standing with this ruler to seek permission for me to enter upon him?”
Al-Ḥurr replied:
“I will seek permission for you.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Al-Ḥurr sought permission for ʿUyaynah to enter. When he entered, he said:
“Son of al-Khaṭṭāb, by Allah, you do not give us generously, nor do you judge justly between us.”
ʿUmar became angry and was about to punish him.
Al-Ḥurr said:
“Commander of the Believers, Allah, the Exalted, said to His Prophet ﷺ:
‘Adopt forgiveness, command what is right, and turn away from the ignorant.’
This man is certainly among the ignorant.”
By Allah, ʿUmar did not go beyond the verse once it was recited to him. He always stopped and submitted when confronted with the Book of Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4642).

12343 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr said concerning the words:
“Adopt forgiveness and command what is right”:
“Allah revealed this verse only concerning how one should deal with people’s character and behavior.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4643).
Al-Bukhārī also cited it in the Book of Tafsīr (4644) in suspended form from ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr, who said:
“Allah commanded His Prophet ﷺ to accept what was easy and naturally forthcoming from people’s character,”
or words to that effect.
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Take what is freely given”:
“Accept whatever surplus they willingly offer from their wealth, and take whatever they bring to you.”
This was before Sūrat Barāʾah was revealed with the obligatory rates of charity, their detailed rulings, and the final regulations governing zakāh.
Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (10/641).
The words:
“Command what is right”
mean commanding recognized goodness, which includes every good statement and righteous action.
It has also been said that Allah commanded him to pardon and overlook the polytheists for ten years, after which He commanded him to deal firmly with them.

12344 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said to me:
“ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir, maintain ties with the one who cuts you off, give to the one who deprives you, and pardon the one who wrongs you.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17452).

12345 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Whenever we reached an elevated place overlooking a valley, we proclaimed Allah’s oneness and greatness, raising our voices.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“People, be gentle with yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. He is with you. He is All-Hearing and Near. Blessed is His name, and exalted is His majesty.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2992) and Muslim (2704).
The words:
“Be gentle with yourselves”
mean:
“Do not overburden yourselves.”
It may also carry the meaning found in the words of Allah:
“Say: Call upon Allah or call upon the Most Merciful. Whichever name you call upon, to Him belong the Most Beautiful Names. Do not recite your prayer too loudly, nor too quietly, but seek a way between the two.”
 [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:110]
This was because when the polytheists heard the Qur’an, they would insult Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and insult the one upon whom it was revealed—the Prophet ﷺ.
Allah therefore commanded that the prayer not be recited so loudly that the polytheists would hear it and insult Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
The words:
“Nor recite it too quietly”
mean that it should not be so quiet that your Companions cannot hear it. Rather, choose a course between the two extremes: the moderate way.

12346 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās about Sūrat al-Anfāl, and he replied:
“It was revealed concerning the Battle of Badr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4645, 4882) and Muslim (3031).

12347 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been given five things that no one before me was given. Every prophet was sent specifically to his own people, whereas I was sent to every red and black person. The spoils of war were made lawful for me, though they had not been made lawful for anyone before me. The earth was made pure for me, a means of purification and a place of prayer, so wherever a man is when the time for prayer reaches him, he may pray there. I was granted victory through fear cast into the enemy from the distance of a month’s journey, and I was granted intercession.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521).

12348 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was favored over the other prophets in six ways: I was given concise and comprehensive speech; I was granted victory through fear cast into the enemy; the spoils of war were made lawful for me; the earth was made for me a means of purification and a place of prayer; I was sent to all creation; and the succession of prophets was sealed with me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (523).

12349 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
On the day of Badr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever does such-and-such will receive such-and-such from the additional spoils.”
The younger men advanced, while the older men remained with the banners and did not leave them.
When Allah granted them victory, the older men said:
“We were your supporting force. Had you been defeated, you would have returned to us. Therefore, do not take all the spoils while leaving us with nothing.”
The younger men refused and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ assigned them to us.”
Allah then revealed:
“They ask you concerning the spoils of war. Say: The spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger. So fear Allah, set matters right between yourselves, and obey Allah and His Messenger if you are believers.”
The meaning was:
“This ruling was better for them. Likewise, obey me, for I know the outcome of this matter better than you do.”
In another wording, he said:
“Whoever kills an enemy combatant will receive such-and-such, and whoever captures a prisoner will receive such-and-such.”
Another narration adds:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then divided the spoils equally.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2737), al-Ḥākim (2/131–132), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (6/291).

12350 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“They ask you concerning the spoils of war. Say: The spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger”:
The anfāl are the spoils of war. They were exclusively under the authority of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and no one else had an independent right to any part of them.
Whatever the Muslim expeditions acquired was brought to him. Whoever concealed even a needle or a thread from it had committed unlawful misappropriation.
They asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to give them a share, so Allah said:
“They ask you concerning the spoils. Say: The spoils belong to Allah.”
That is:
“The spoils belong to Me. I have assigned them to My Messenger, and you possess no independent right over them.”
“So fear Allah, reconcile the relations between yourselves, and obey Allah and His Messenger if you are believers.”
Allah then revealed:
“Know that whatever spoils you acquire, one-fifth belongs to Allah, the Messenger, his near relatives, the orphans, the needy, and the stranded traveler, if you truly believe in Allah and in what We sent down upon Our servant on the Day of Criterion, the day the two armies met. Allah has power over all things.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:41]
The one-fifth was then allocated to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and to those named in the verse.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/19–20), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8766), and al-Bayhaqī (6/293).
